# Competitive landscape: voice capture and quick capture

**Research date:** 31 August 2026. **Issue:** #65.

This document records everything found in a competitive study of the products that share Delta's use case: get a raw thought out of your head by speaking, and have it come back later structured and findable.

## How this was produced, and how much to trust it

Sixteen research agents ran in two waves and made roughly 1,250 web fetches and searches against product sites, pricing pages, help centres, changelogs, App Store and Play listings, the iTunes lookup API, and independent reviews.

- **Wave 1** — four discovery sweeps from different angles (product-led, app-store-led, quick-capture tradition, demand-side review reading) produced 130 raw entries, deduplicated to **53 products**. One agent ranked them, five agents wrote **10 deep teardowns**, and one adversarial critic attacked the result.
- **Wave 2** — the critic found that the sweep had missed the largest actually-used products in the category. Five more agents produced **16 further teardowns** covering those misses.

**Read the evidence, not the confidence.** The critic's own findings are reproduced in full in [Corrections and unverified claims](#corrections-and-unverified-claims), and they matter: several numbers in the teardowns are second-hand, derived, or contradicted by another section of the same study. Where a teardown says "unverified", believe the word "unverified". Tap counts in particular were mostly _derived from documented mechanisms_, not measured on a device — so do not quote them at anyone.

Scope was set deliberately: **voice-first capture apps** and **quick-capture/inbox tools**. Text-first AI PKM tools and pure meeting recorders were excluded, which is why Granola, Fireflies and Otter appear only as context. See [Excluded products](#excluded-products) for what was dropped and why.

## Our own baseline, for comparison

Verified against the repo at `78ab75c` (31 Aug 2026) so the comparisons below are against what we actually ship, not what the README describes.

**Flow:** voice or typed → transcribe → AI understand (intent, category, project match, people) → confidence gate (`src/features/actions/filing-gate.ts`, thresholds 0.75 / 0.45) → auto-filed (marked "filed for you", undoable) or Inbox → user approves → Timeline. Research is opt-in and never automatic.

**Enums:** `action_type` (note | task | reminder | message) · `action_status` (pending | approved | completed) · `action_category` (inbox | work | personal | meeting | idea) · `capture_processing_status` (recorded | uploaded | transcribed | failed) · `person_role` (recipient | mentioned) · `preferred_language` (en | nl | fr).

**Tables:** `profiles` (incl. `auto_file_captures`) · `voice_captures` (audio_path, transcript) · `actions` — the central entity, carrying `title`, `summary`, `status`, `scheduled_at`, `message_draft`, `confidence`, `requires_clarification`, `project_id`, `category`, and separately `suggested_category`, `suggested_project_name`, `suggested_people`, `auto_filed_at` · `people` + `action_people` (M2M with roles) · `projects` (flat, with generated `summary`) · `notifications` · `research_sessions` / `_sources` / `_findings` / `_finding_sources` · `meeting_contexts`.

**Two things we already do that almost nobody else does.** First, the central entity is an _action_ with an intent type, not a _note_ — Voicenotes, Cleft, flomo, mymind and Day One all have exactly one note-shaped object. Second, we store the AI's guess separately from the committed value (`suggested_*` alongside `category`/`project_id`), which is the schema-level precondition for a review gate. Tana had to rebuild its product to get this.

**Structural gaps, relative to what the field has settled on:** no tags, no links or backlinks, no nesting, no search index, no attachments beyond capture audio, no sharing, and a single temporal field per action.

## What the study actually says

Nine findings that should change what we build, in rough order of how much they should change it.

**1. Capture durability outranks AI quality, and it is not close.** Every product's angriest reviews are about lost recordings, not bad summaries. Voicenotes' own help centre admits: _"Once the upload is interrupted, the file can get corrupted — and unfortunately, not even we can recover it. We don't store partial or unsynced audio."_ Their mitigation is telling users to stay in the app for 30–60 seconds. Cleft's last five releases are titled "More Reliable Capture", "Reliable Controls", "Rock-Solid Reliability", "Built to Last" — a team spending its roadmap on dependability. We have _more_ failure points than either (record → upload → transcribe → understand → file). Write audio to local storage before any network call, drain a retry queue, and never let the understand step block the save.

**2. One-press capture from a locked device is table stakes, and it is native code.** Cleft, TwinMind, Tana, Todoist, Drafts and Apple all ship it. A Lock Screen widget, Action Button intent, Live Activity and Control Center control cannot be built in JavaScript — they need a WidgetKit/AppIntents extension via an Expo config plugin and a dev client. If our fastest path stays unlock → open app → tap record, the "zero typing friction" pitch is false against a two-person team. This is a build decision that has to be made _before_ the positioning is committed.

**3. Correct the AI before commit, not after.** Todoist's parser highlights every recognised token inline and lets you tap it back into plain text before saving; Ramble takes spoken corrections mid-stream. Their Inbox is not a review queue for the whole interpretation — it holds exactly one unresolved foreign key, the project. Our confidence gate parks the _entire_ capture. Consider showing the understand-step result as editable chips in the capture sheet, and routing to Inbox only when project matching is below threshold.

**4. But when you must defer, make it a proposal queue.** New Tana's mechanism is the most relevant single design in the study: AI-generated items _"wait under For Review as proposals until you accept them"_, and nothing AI-derived enters the graph without human acceptance. That is our Inbox — except that our gate auto-files high-confidence captures with no proposal at all. Tana tried un-disableable autofill first (the outliner) and it churned.

**5. flomo is the strongest argument against our thesis and deserves an honest hearing.** Verified 4.93/5 from ~52,000 CN App Store ratings — roughly eight times Voicenotes. It has no folders, no projects, no tasks, no people, no inbox, and **no AI auto-tagging whatsoever**; every tag is typed by a human. Its AI transcribes voice, strips typos and verbal tics, and then deliberately stops. It has retained a large paying base for years by investing in _resurfacing_ — daily review, random walk, vector-similarity related notes, a cognitive map — instead of triage. If capture without classification demonstrably does not rot, then classification is not the wedge; **retrieval** is. That reframes our roadmap.

**6. Retrieval is the metric, not filing accuracy.** mymind's entire testimonial wall is about finding, not organising. Tana's documented churn story is structure without payoff. Apple Voice Memos' flat, tagless, linkless folder is the architectural reason a memo is recorded and never opened again. Instrument "seconds to re-find a capture older than 30 days" and treat gate accuracy as secondary to it. Prebuilt always-live views ("open actions by project", "what I said about X", "waiting on <person>") beat anything the user has to configure.

**7. The category leader is vacating the position we want — read that both ways.** Voicenotes now sells itself as a botless meeting notetaker; Tana bet the whole company on meetings. That is the opportunity (the personal raw-thought loop is opening up) _and_ the warning (two funded teams with better data than ours concluded the personal loop does not pay). Do not wave the second half away.

**8. Segment-specific positioning beats "capture your thoughts", unambiguously.** The largest verified user bases in voice-to-structure are student-specific (Turbo AI ~33k ratings, Coconote ~16.5k). The most engaged small base is explicitly neurodivergent — Cleft's App Store name is literally "For Verbal Thinkers". Twos, Saner and mymind all market to ADHD. Meanwhile generic quick-capture inboxes plateau: Twos at ~25,000 users after five years, Braintoss at ~183 iOS ratings after thirteen. "Speak the thought, it gets structured" is a mechanism, not a market.

**9. The price floor is set by apps we would not respect.** An anonymous commodity recorder ("Voice Recorder: AI Note Taker", Hangzhou Suoyi Network Technology) has ~7,500 US ratings — more than Voicenotes — and lists Dutch first among 15 languages. Coconote covers Dutch too. The Dutch-language gap that Apple leaves open is therefore already filled at the bottom of the market, and any pricing above ~€8–10/mo has to be justified by routing and retrieval, not by transcription.

## What I would change in Delta

Concrete, ordered, and each traceable to a teardown below.

1. **Durable local-first capture.** Write the audio chunk-by-chunk to `expo-file-system` before any network call; only delete after the server confirms a transcript row. Add a visible per-capture state machine (recorded → uploaded → transcribed → understood → filed, plus **stuck**). Copy Braintoss's badge rule exactly: the badge counts only _stuck_ captures, never ones still processing.
2. **Decide the native-extension question.** Lock Screen widget + Action Button App Intent, or drop friction as the differentiator. There is no third option.
3. **Constrain extraction to the transcript.** Require the model to return a verbatim `source_span` for every proposed action and drop any proposal whose span does not literally appear; render the highlighted source text under each proposal. "The AI invents to-dos I never said" is Voicenotes' recurring complaint and it is a trust-killer.
4. **Split the confidence signal in two.** ASR-level confidence (audio quality, silence ratio) and understand-step confidence are different failure modes. Poor audio should never auto-file regardless of how confident the LLM sounds.
5. **Make the Inbox a proposal card, not a form.** One tap to accept the whole proposal; every field individually overridable inline. If accepting costs more taps than Drafts costs to triage manually, the AI is a net loss.
6. **Add smart merging before adding entity types.** Fuzzy/embedding match against existing projects and contacts, defaulting to _update_ rather than _create_. Duplicate "Acme" / "Acme Corp" / "the Acme project" rows will wreck the graph within a month of real use.
7. **Editing the transcript should re-trigger the understand step.** Nearly free for us, since understand is already a pure function of transcript text. This makes a wrong auto-file recoverable without a settings screen — the correction ritual mymind lacks and gets punished for.
8. **Gate side effects, not filing.** Let high-confidence captures file themselves silently, but require explicit confirmation before anything leaves the app (calendar event, message, research run). Filing is reversible; a sent message is not.
9. **Ship full export early** — JSON + Markdown + audio, with Cleft's front-matter contract and a namespaced `delta_id`. Reviewers cite export unprompted as a trust signal, and it is nearly free on Supabase.
10. **Build resurfacing.** A weekly digest of captures that never became anything is a feature the market leader has consciously not built, and per finding 5 it may matter more than the confidence gate.
11. **Never meter capture.** Tana charges ~50 AI credits per 15 minutes of transcription from the same pot as AI chat, taxing the exact behaviour the product depends on. Price seats; meter only research runs.
12. **Add `source_channel` to captures** (mic, watch, share sheet, widget, WhatsApp). Capacities' ingest API carries an `origin` field, and a typed share-sheet capture deserves a different confidence threshold than a rambling voice note.

## Known gaps in this research

Reproduced honestly from the critic, because these are the things to check before betting on any conclusion above.

- **Android was analysed at a fraction of iOS depth in wave 1**, and not one Play rating was verified first-hand there; every Android figure in the wave-1 teardowns is a second-hand APKCombo mirror. Wave 2 covers the Android capture floor properly — see the Android teardowns below.
- **Unit economics were never computed.** Nobody costed a capture: ASR minutes plus understand-step tokens plus storage against €8–10/mo. TwinMind gives away unlimited transcription because it owns its ASR at $0.23/hour; we would be reselling someone else's.
- **Onboarding and cold start are unexamined.** For a product whose value depends on matching against existing projects and people, what the app looks like on day one with zero data is the central design risk and got no dedicated treatment.
- **Accessibility is named as the beachhead but never engineered.** No analysis of VoiceOver, Dynamic Type, or — most importantly — ASR word error rate on disfluent, stammering or dysarthric speech. If the wedge is verbal thinkers, that is a product requirement.
- **EU legal ground is covered only for TwinMind.** GDPR lawful basis, data residency, the EU AI Act's transparency duties and Dutch/Belgian recording-consent law are our actual operating constraints and appear once, in passing.
- **No latency was measured anywhere.** Time-to-first-byte and time-to-transcript were never stopwatched for any competitor.
- **Multi-user and family capture** is repeatedly noted as absent across competitors and never analysed — and `CLAUDE.md` lists the multi-user instance model as an open decision.
- **Long-horizon graph decay is unmeasured.** No competitor was examined at two-plus years of accumulated data to see whether an auto-populated entity graph stays usable. That is the central long-term risk of our architecture.

---

The rest of this document is the raw research output, reproduced in full so nothing is lost. Every teardown carries the sources the agent actually read.

And its $12.99/week shows where the aggressive end of monetisation sits.                                                                                                                                                 |

## Deep teardowns

Twenty-six full teardowns. The first ten were selected by the ranking agent for a deliberate spread of approaches; the remaining sixteen close the gaps the completeness critic found. Each was written by an agent that read the marketing site, pricing page, help centre, changelog, store listing and independent reviews, and each lists the URLs it actually read.

### Voicenotes

<https://voicenotes.com/>

**Positioning**

As of August 2026 the homepage tagline is "AI notetaker that works (magic) in the background" and the pitch is meeting-first: "transforms meetings into automated notes and actionable insights" across Zoom/Meet/Teams/Webex/Slack without a bot joining the call. The App Store listing name has followed the same drift — it is now "Voicenotes AI Notes & Meetings" with subtitle "Record. Transcribe. Ask AI." This is a MATERIAL CHANGE from the product described in our brief: Voicenotes launched (Product Hunt, 13 May 2024) as a personal voice-first thought-capture tool ("AI note-taker that's truly intelligent") and has repositioned toward the meeting-notetaker market where Granola/Fathom/Otter compete. The old personal loop still exists — the help centre still defines "memos" (single-speaker: "a thought, idea, reminder, journal entry") as a first-class note type, and dedicated landing pages for Meetings, Memos and Dictation shipped 16 Apr 2026 — but the front door now sells meetings. Target audience per the site: "professionals and teams conducting meetings"; per the help centre still also journalers, students (lecture note taker page), and idea-capturers.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Company: Coping Hard Inc. Founded/launched publicly on Product Hunt 13 May 2024 (1,494 upvotes; a second launch, Voicenotes Pages, 10 Apr 2025, 779 upvotes) — so roughly 2 years and 3 months old as of Aug 2026. Funding: no round is announced anywhere on the site or help centre; the brief's "bootstrapped" characterisation is consistent with that but UNVERIFIED in this session. Scale: homepage claims "1 million downloads" and a "Product of the year" award (award body unspecified). iOS: 4.77/5 from 6,719 ratings (Apple iTunes API, US storefront, Aug 2026) — strong and up from the 6.7K/4.8 figure in the brief, i.e. still growing. Android: 3.9/5 from ~81 ratings, 100K+ downloads (APKCombo mirror of the Play listing — the Google Play page itself would not render for direct verification, so treat the Android numbers as SECOND-HAND/partially unverified). The iOS-vs-Android quality gap is large. Trustpilot: 4.2 TrustScore, 54 reviews, 85% five-star and 13% one-star, 26 in the last 12 months — a barbell distribution, not a mediocre average. Product Hunt reviews: 4.7/5 from 31 reviews. Shipping velocity is high and current: v2.1.6 released 27 Aug 2026 (four days before this teardown). SOC 2 Type 2 achieved 26 Mar 2026. The 2026 release-note timeline shows a clear strategic swing into meetings (Zoom/Meet/Teams/Slack Huddle integrations, meeting-vs-memo auto-detection, meeting reports, dedicated Meetings landing page) and away from the pure personal-thought-capture story. Nothing about this product looks dead.

**Platforms**

iOS (App Store id6483293628, v2.1.6, updated 27 Aug 2026) · Android (package com.app.voicenotes) · macOS · Windows · Web app · watchOS · Wear OS · Chrome extension · WhatsApp (voice note in / retrieval out)

**Pricing**

Web pricing page (voicenotes.com/pricing), verified Aug 2026 — three tiers, per-user: (1) Basic / Free, $0 — 100 weekly transcription minutes shared across meetings, memos and dictation; unlimited recordings; 100+ languages; 30-day history only; no advanced features or integrations. (2) Pro, $9/user/month — unlimited recording and transcription minutes, real-time notes, live transcription, unlimited history, unlimited audio imports and storage, integrations ("Zapier, Notion, Claude, ChatGPT, WhatsApp & more"), Voicenotes MCP, centralised team billing. (3) Enterprise, $24/user/month — SSO & SCIM, consent management, org-wide sharing controls, dedicated success manager, priority SLAs, custom CRM/dialer integrations, "zero model training on your data". NOTE A PRICING CONTRADICTION: iOS in-app purchases on the same app are far higher — Weekly $8.99, Monthly $14.99, Annual $89.99 / $99.99 (App Store listing, id6483293628). So the mobile-purchased price is ~$15/mo vs $9/mo on web; a 1-star Trustpilot reviewer (Oct 2025) complained specifically about a $99.99 annual charge. A lifetime deal existed historically — an App Store reviewer writes "Having used it one day I bought the lifetime account" — but no lifetime tier is currently listed (status: discontinued/unverified). Free-tier cap of 100 min/week is the real gate: that is ~14 min/day.

**Capture UX**

ENTRY POINTS: iOS app, Android app, Mac, Windows, web, Chrome extension, watchOS, Wear OS, and WhatsApp (send a voice note to a dedicated WhatsApp chat and it lands in your account — help centre article 11712572). LOCKED-PHONE TO RECORDING, LITERAL COUNT: the fastest documented path on iPhone 15 Pro+ is ONE physical press of the Action Button. But the help centre (article 10356830) shows this is not a native integration — you must build it yourself in Shortcuts (5 setup steps: open Shortcuts → + → search "Voicenotes" → pick an action → Settings > Action Button > Shortcuts). The doc says only that pressing it "will launch Voicenotes directly" and explicitly does NOT state whether recording auto-starts; available shortcut actions are listed as "Ask AI, Record, or Search". So: 1 press to launch, with auto-record UNVERIFIED. Without the Action Button, the path is unlock → find app → tap record = 3 interactions. No lock-screen or home-screen recording widget is documented anywhere in the help centre — a notable gap versus Tana. Wall-clock seconds: unverified, no latency claim published. RECORDING LIMITS (article 12810376): iOS/Android 60 min, web 90 min, watch 60 min, Chrome extension 90 min, Mac/Windows 2 hours. On hitting the limit "a new recording starts automatically — you don't have to tap anything" and is "attached as a subnote" to the original. OFFLINE / RELIABILITY — THE CRITICAL FINDING: Voicenotes is cloud-upload-dependent and admits it. Help article 11640013 states "If the app is closed too quickly or backgrounded by the device before the upload finishes, the process gets interrupted and the file can be lost", "Once the upload is interrupted, the file can get corrupted—and unfortunately, not even we can recover it", and "We don't store partial or unsynced audio." The recommended workaround is behavioural: "Stay on the app for about 30–60 seconds after recording ends till the file completes 'Uploading' and starts 'Processing'." They cite ~1 in 1000. Offline recording with local audio buffering shipped on WEB in Aug 2024; the release notes show no 2026 update extending it to mobile. This is an architecture problem, not a bug: there is no durable local write-ahead of the audio on mobile. WHAT THE USER SEES: live transcription and dictation-into-any-app shipped in v2.1.6 (27 Aug 2026), plus a redesigned sidebar nav and a dedicated Tags section.

**Processing and AI**

TRANSCRIPTION ENGINE: not disclosed anywhere in the docs or site — unverified. 100+ languages claimed (site says 60+ on the meetings page, 100+ on pricing and the App Store — inconsistent). Language is a per-account setting with an auto-detect option. AI MODELS: not disclosed — unverified. AUTO-PROCESSING: every note gets automatic transcription, an auto-generated title, and a summary. Setting "Summarize Long Recordings" shows a summary instead of the full transcript for anything over 5 minutes. Since 18 Mar 2026 the app auto-detects meeting-vs-personal note from the number of speakers detected — a genuine, narrow intent classifier — and applies a meeting report (key points, decisions, follow-up actions) or a memo treatment accordingly. Speaker identification/diarisation is supported (article 12449684). "Names to Remember" is a per-account vocabulary/biasing list ("Add words, names, or places unique to you to avoid misspellings"). "About" lets you "Tell the AI about yourself here" for personalised responses — a global persona prompt. "Fix" and "Fix Language" repair transcripts; 15 Apr 2026 gave them "a larger context window, resulting in more accurate, coherent corrections". CREATE (article 10371248): on-demand AI transforms with 8 presets — summary, to-do list, professional email, tweet, blog post, key takeaways, clarify note, and Custom (free-text prompt). Can run over multiple selected notes. TAG AUTOMATIONS (article 12272619) are the auto-run layer: Settings → Integrations and Automations → "Auto create" → select tag(s) → connect a creation. So #meeting auto-produces a meeting report, #tasks auto-produces a to-do list. Tags themselves can be auto-applied by user-defined KEYWORD rules, not semantic classification — "Add more keywords in the corresponding tags to auto-tag them". ON-DEVICE: none; all processing is cloud. HALLUCINATION — OFFICIALLY DOCUMENTED: help article 11070862 defines it as the model "mistakenly creates words or sentences that weren't actually spoken" and admits transcribed words are what the model "believes it hears, even if nothing was said at all." Causes listed: long silences, background noise, wrong language setting, and — importantly — the "Names to Remember" feature itself causing the AI to "hear" listed terms that were not clearly spoken. Their fixes are all user-side (set language, pause during silence, record somewhere quiet). A separate article, "Why do AI models make mistakes?" (9404178), exists alongside it.

**Data model**

FLAT BY DESIGN. The exposed entities are: (1) NOTE — the umbrella object. Every capture is a Note. Four subtypes, distinguished by how they were made rather than by a user-chosen type: MEMO (single-speaker recording — "a thought, idea, reminder, journal entry"), MEETING (multi-speaker, gets speaker identification and a structured report of "key points, decisions, and follow-up actions"), DICTATION (voice-to-text for drafting, incl. system-wide dictation into any app since v2.1.6), and TEXT NOTE (typed directly, article 9415149). Subtype is assigned automatically by speaker count since 18 Mar 2026; the user does not declare it up front. Attributes visible via the webhook payload (article 9895482): id, title, transcript, event, timestamp. Audio file, summary and tags are also attached. Sub-notes exist but only as an overflow mechanism — when a recording hits the device time limit the continuation is "attached as a subnote", not as a user-facing hierarchy. (2) TAG — the ONLY organisational primitive. Explicitly flat: "Voicenotes uses tags instead of folders as they give you greater flexibility—no need to dig through folders or subfolders" (article 10393499). Multi-valued: "One note can have multiple tags, so you can group it under themes like 'Work,' 'Meetings,' or 'Ideas' all at once." No nesting, no parent tags. Tags can be pinned to the top of the tag rail; tapping a tag filters instantly; tags render as a horizontal strip under the search bar, and got a dedicated section in the sidebar in v2.1.6. Tags are applied manually from the note menu OR auto-applied by user-defined KEYWORD matching. No AI semantic categorisation of tags is documented. (3) CREATION — an AI-generated artifact derived from one or more notes (summary, to-do list, email, tweet, blog post, takeaways, custom). Webhook events fire separately for "Summary Creation", "To-Do List Creation", "Main points Creation" with payload {id, content, type, title, event, timestamp} — so a creation IS a distinct persisted entity with its own id, attached to a note. Editable: yes, it is text in the app. (4) PAGE — a public publishing surface on a custom subdomain, up to 10 per account (settings article 11161190; voicenotes.com/pages). Marketed as "create separate pages for your ideas, projects, or personas—all under one account" and can be distributed as a podcast to Apple Podcasts/Spotify. This is the ONLY thing in the product resembling a project container, and it is an outbound publishing channel, not an internal workspace. (5) CALENDAR — a date-based view over notes (article 15391510), not a separate entity. WHAT DOES NOT EXIST — verified by absence across the full help centre, sitemap and 2024–2026 release notes: no folders, no notebooks, no projects, no collections, no contacts/people entity (speaker labels are transcript metadata, not records), no task entity (to-dos are text inside a Creation, or pushed out to Todoist), no reminder entity, no note-to-note links or backlinks, no INBOX or unprocessed/triage state. Every capture goes straight into one undifferentiated reverse-chronological stream, and the only differentiator is a flat tag the user must remember to apply or have pre-wired a keyword rule for. THIS IS THE ARCHITECTURE BEHIND THE COMPLAINT — the 2-star App Store review "Organizing notes is a mess. There's no clear way to categorize recordings or keep projects distinct" is not a UI bug, it is the data model. AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE EDITABILITY: titles, summaries, transcripts and creations are all editable text ("Fix" exists specifically to correct them); but because the AI never assigns a container, there is no filing decision to correct. HOW A CAPTURE BECOMES A TASK: only by leaving the product — user manually opens the note menu → Create → To-do list, which then syncs to the Todoist INBOX (article 10212350; no project selection, no documented due-date parsing), or to Things 3, or out via webhook/Zapier. Tag Automations can make that trigger automatically off a tag. So the capture→task path is one-way export, not an internal state change.

**Organisation and retrieval**

SEARCH: text search over the library, plus tag filtering by tapping a tag. ASK AI (article 10372858) queries "all your text and voice notes" — described as "Context-Aware"; the MCP docs confirm the underlying capability is SEMANTIC (the exposed tool is `search_notes` — "Semantic search across notes (e.g., finding relevant content without exact keyword matches)"), so it is RAG over the whole account. Weaknesses of the retrieval story, from the docs: no documented scoping of Ask AI by tag, date or note type; no documented source-citation back to the originating notes; no disclosed model; no stated limits. CREATE can be run across multiple manually selected notes, which is the closest thing to a synthesis-over-a-project flow — but you must select the notes by hand because there is no project. CALENDAR view gives date-based retrieval. NO resurfacing, spaced-review, or "what did I say about X last month" prompt is documented. On the free tier retrieval is crippled by a 30-day history window. NET: recall is strong at the whole-account level (semantic ask-your-notes works and is well reviewed) and weak at the mid level — there is no way to ask "show me everything about the Delta project" unless you tagged it, and no way to browse a coherent unit of work.

**Outputs and integrations**

EXPORT PER NOTE (article 15391489): copy link (public URL), copy transcript, copy summary, share via email, publish to Pages, download original audio; file exports as Transcript→PDF, Summary→PDF, Transcript→.txt, Summary→.txt. NO bulk/whole-account export is documented — a real lock-in risk. INTEGRATIONS (14 help articles): Zoom, Google Meet, Microsoft Teams, Slack Huddle (all botless meeting capture); Notion; Obsidian plugin (actively maintained — improvements shipped 22 Apr and 22 May 2026 covering rate-limit handling and more dependable note sync); Readwise; Todoist; Things 3; OpenClaw; Zapier; Webhooks; Tag Automations; and the Voicenotes MCP server. WHATSAPP: two dedicated chats — one to record into, one to retrieve from — and the help centre recommends it as a redundancy path for critical recordings. MCP SERVER (article 14336494, public since 25 Mar 2026): endpoint https://api.voicenotes.com/mcp, browser OAuth 2.0 with Dynamic Client Registration (no API keys), exposing four tools — search_notes (semantic), list_notes (filter by date range or tag), get_transcript, create_text_note (with optional tags). This is effectively their public API. WEBHOOKS: six events — note created/updated/deleted, plus summary/to-do-list/main-points creation. Payloads are thin (id, title, transcript OR content+type, event, timestamp); no audio URL, no tags, no filtering by tag, and no signing/auth mechanism documented — weak for anything security-sensitive. NO documented public REST API and no calendar-write integration.

**Strengths**

- Transcription quality is the single most-praised attribute, especially multilingual: "Best at bilingual and english transcription by far...better experience than any other" (Trustpilot 5-star, 22 Aug 2026).
- Genuinely broad capture surface — phone, watch, Mac, Windows, web, Chrome extension, and WhatsApp — so the thought can be caught wherever the user is. WhatsApp-as-capture is a clever zero-install channel.
- Semantic ask-your-notes across the entire account actually works and is the emotional hook: "It's the AI assistant I've always wanted! I've been waiting for years and it has changed my life!" (App Store 5-star).
- Aggressive, current shipping cadence — live transcription, system-wide dictation, Action Button support and a Tags section all in v2.1.6 (Aug 2026), meeting-vs-memo auto-detection in Mar 2026.
- Unusually honest, specific help centre. They publish articles named "Understanding AI Hallucination in Your Transcripts" and "How to avoid losing a recording before it uploads" rather than hiding the failure modes.
- Price undercuts the meeting-notetaker field hard at $9/user/mo on web for unlimited transcription.
- Tag Automations are a smart low-cost automation primitive: one tag can trigger a whole downstream creation pipeline with zero per-note configuration.
- MCP server with OAuth and semantic search makes the corpus addressable from Claude/ChatGPT — good instinct for where retrieval is heading.

**Weaknesses**

- DATA LOSS IS THE #1 RECURRING COMPLAINT AND IT IS CORROBORATED BY THREE INDEPENDENT SOURCES PLUS THE COMPANY'S OWN DOCS. Trustpilot 1-star, Sebastian, 7 May 2026: "Many recordings simply disappear or can't upload and then are gone." Product Hunt: "When creating a voice note there is often a failure for that note to upload to the cloud...your notes are lost Forever." App Store 3-star: "The app has stopped recording mid-session multiple times without warning, and I'm left with incomplete files." And the vendor's own article 11640013: "We don't store partial or unsynced audio" and "not even we can recover it."
- No offline/local durability on mobile. Mobile capture is upload-coupled; the only mitigation offered is asking the user to babysit the app for 30–60 seconds after every recording. Offline+local buffering exists on web only (Aug 2024) and was never extended.
- NO PROJECT SEPARATION — an architecture problem, not a polish problem. Verbatim 2-star App Store review: "Organizing notes is a mess. There's no clear way to categorize recordings or keep projects distinct." There are no folders, no projects, no collections; flat multi-tags are the entire organisational system.
- No triage or inbox state. Nothing is ever "unprocessed" or "filed" — captures land in one stream and stay there, so the pile grows monotonically and the only way back to something is search.
- AI invents content the user did not say — admitted by the vendor: the model outputs what it "believes it hears, even if nothing was said at all", and the "Names to Remember" feature actively increases this by making the AI hallucinate listed terms. Their proposed fixes are all behavioural changes by the user.
- Onboarding/learnability friction. Trustpilot 1-star, John Craig, 19 Apr 2026: "Frustratingly difficult to learn how to use. Gets the punctuation wrong."
- Billing complaints: a 1-star Trustpilot reviewer reported a $99.99 annual charge announced by a late-night email; the help centre carries an article "Why does my subscription show as free after I've paid?" (12381775). The web ($9/mo) vs iOS IAP ($14.99/mo) price gap is a trust hazard.
- Android is materially worse than iOS — 3.9/5 vs 4.77/5. Cross-platform parity is not being held.
- No bulk export of the account. Per-note only. Real lock-in.
- Webhook payloads omit tags and audio URL, cannot be filtered by tag, and have no documented signing — thin for serious automation.
- No lock-screen or home-screen record widget documented; the fastest capture path (Action Button) requires the user to hand-build a Shortcut.
- The pivot to meetings is a strategic risk to the original user: the front door now sells a bot-less meeting notetaker into a crowded field (Granola, Fathom, Otter, Fireflies) while the personal-memo loop that earned the 4.77 rating is demoted to a sub-page. A help article literally titled "When we decide to fire a user" (11622213) sits in the troubleshooting collection.

**Ideas for Delta**

- TREAT AUDIO DURABILITY AS THE FIRST FEATURE, BEFORE ANY UNDERSTAND-STEP CODE. Voicenotes' single worst-reviewed property is losing recordings, and their own doc admits "We don't store partial or unsynced audio." In Delta: write the audio chunk-by-chunk to expo-file-system BEFORE any network call, mark the capture row `local_only` in Supabase-bound local state, and only delete the local file after the server confirms the transcript row exists. Every capture should survive app kill, airplane mode and a dead battery. Ship an explicit 'Not yet uploaded (N)' affordance so the user is never silently lied to.
- BUILD A RETRY QUEUE, NOT A 30-SECOND WAITING RULE. Their mitigation is 'stay on the app for 30–60 seconds'. Ours should be a background upload queue with exponential backoff that drains on next app open or network return, plus a visible per-capture state machine (recorded → uploaded → transcribed → understood → filed). Surface the state on the capture card; never let a capture be in an invisible failure state.
- OUR PROJECT ENTITY IS THE DIFFERENTIATOR, SO MAKE IT THE PRIMARY NAVIGATION, NOT A FILTER. Voicenotes' top structural complaint is 'no clear way to... keep projects distinct' and it has literally no project object. Delta already has projects, actions, contacts, meetings — do not bury them behind a reverse-chronological stream. The home screen after Inbox should be Projects, and every project page should show its captures, open actions, people and research in one place. This is the thing Voicenotes structurally cannot do without a schema rewrite.
- OUR INBOX IS ALREADY THE THING THEY LACK — LEAN INTO IT AND MEASURE IT. Voicenotes has no unprocessed state at all, so nothing is ever 'done'. Give Delta's Inbox a visible zero state, a swipe-to-confirm triage flow, and a count. Track 'time to inbox zero' as the retention metric; a user who reaches zero has experienced the payoff Voicenotes cannot deliver.
- NEVER LET THE UNDERSTAND STEP INVENT AN ACTION. The recurring complaint is 'the AI invents to-dos the user never said', and Voicenotes officially admits the model outputs what it 'believes it hears'. Constrain our action extraction to spans that exist in the transcript: require the model to return a verbatim `source_span` (start/end char offsets into the transcript) for every proposed action, and drop any proposal whose span does not literally appear. Render the highlighted source text under each proposed action in the Inbox so the user can see exactly which words caused it.
- SEPARATE TRANSCRIPTION HALLUCINATION FROM EXTRACTION HALLUCINATION IN THE CONFIDENCE GATE. Voicenotes conflates them. In Delta the gate should read two signals: ASR-level confidence (silence ratio, audio SNR, per-word confidence) and understand-step confidence. A capture with clean audio but ambiguous intent goes to Inbox for a category choice; a capture with poor audio never auto-files at all, regardless of how confident the LLM sounds. Log both so we can tune them separately.
- COPY 'NAMES TO REMEMBER' BUT FIX ITS BUG. A per-user vocabulary of project names, colleague names and jargon is high-value for our domain (we already have a contacts table — feed it in automatically). But Voicenotes documents that this feature CAUSES hallucination by making the model hear listed terms that were not said. Use the vocabulary as a post-hoc fuzzy-match correction over the transcript (only rewrite a token when edit distance is small), never as a decoder bias that can insert words.
- COPY 'ABOUT — TELL THE AI ABOUT YOURSELF' AS A FIRST-CLASS ONBOARDING STEP. One free-text paragraph about the user's role, projects and vocabulary, injected into every understand-step prompt, is cheap and directly raises project-matching and people-matching accuracy. It also gives the user a legible lever to pull when classification is wrong ('the AI keeps miscategorising X' → edit this).
- STEAL TAG AUTOMATIONS, RETARGET AT PROJECTS. Their 'tag X auto-runs creation Y' is the best cheap idea in the product. In Delta: per-project automations — 'anything filed to project Acme also creates a Todoist task', 'anything categorised research triggers the research agent'. Configure once per project rather than per capture.
- MATCH THEIR CAPTURE SURFACE ON THE TWO CHANNELS THAT MATTER AND SKIP THE REST. Voicenotes spreads across watchOS, Wear OS, Chrome, WhatsApp. For Delta the high-leverage two are (a) an iOS lock-screen/home-screen widget that starts recording on ONE tap — Voicenotes does not have one and it is their biggest capture-speed gap versus Tana — and (b) a real Shortcuts/Action Button action that starts recording immediately rather than merely launching the app. Publish the tap count and the time-to-first-byte as a product claim.
- GIVE 'ASK YOUR NOTES' A SCOPE SELECTOR AND CITATIONS. Their Ask AI searches everything, cites nothing, and cannot be scoped. Delta should let the user scope the question to a project, a person, a date range or an entity type, and must render source capture cards under every answer that deep-link back to the original audio. Citation-back-to-audio is trust infrastructure in a product whose input is lossy.
- DO NOT COPY THE MEETING PIVOT. Voicenotes earned a 4.77/6,719-rating personal-capture user base and is now pointing its homepage at the crowded botless-meeting-notetaker market (Granola, Fathom, Otter, Fireflies). Delta's brief already has a meetings entity — keep it as a capture SOURCE that feeds projects and actions, and keep the marketing on the raw-thought loop. That is the position Voicenotes is vacating.
- SHIP BULK EXPORT ON DAY ONE. Voicenotes has per-note export only and no account export. A one-click 'export everything as Markdown + audio + JSON' is cheap for us (Supabase), removes a genuine adoption objection, and is a credible differentiator against a competitor that locks the corpus in.
- KEEP ONE PRICE ACROSS WEB AND APP STORE. Their $9/mo web vs $14.99/mo iOS gap plus surprise-renewal complaints is a preventable trust cost. Price once; if IAP fees force a gap, disclose it in the app.
- INSTRUMENT A 'RECOVERED CAPTURE' EVENT. Because their loss rate is claimed at ~1/1000 yet dominates their 1-star reviews, the perceived rate matters more than the real one. Log every capture that needed a retry and succeeded, and consider telling the user ('this one took 3 tries — it's safe'). Visible recovery converts a would-be 1-star into confidence.

### Tana

<https://tana.inc/>

**Positioning**

CRITICAL FINDING THAT INVALIDATES PART OF THE BRIEF: as of August 2026 "Tana" is TWO SEPARATE PRODUCTS from the same company (Tana Labs, Inc). (1) tana.inc is now a brand-new product — "Tana is an agentic meeting platform. Work gets done in the meeting, not after." It is a native video-call environment with AI agents that have live tool access, generating artifacts, tasks and decisions into a shared context graph during the call. Its stated enemy is "meeting debt". Target segments named on site: product & tech teams, advisors & consultants, VCs & investors, managers & execs. (2) outliner.tana.inc is the product our brief describes — now renamed "Tana Outliner", self-described as "a knowledge management tool built on an infinite outliner with supertags, views, and AI features". The April 2026 update states the rename explicitly: "Tana Outliner" reflects the supertags/nodes knowledge-management system, while "new Tana" targets a broader audience around collaboration, meetings and team workflows. tana.inc/docs and help.tana.inc both 308-redirect to outliner.tana.inc. The May 2026 update says the Outliner team "operates independently with its own roadmap and its own momentum", focused on "core reliability, paper cuts, and long-requested features". The iOS app was RENAMED from "Tana" to "Tana Outliner". Existing paid subscribers get both products through year-end 2026. READ THIS AS THE REAL LESSON: the company itself concluded that the declare-schema-then-capture outliner would not carry the business, and built a second product that puts capture inside meetings and hides the schema behind AI. That is a stronger signal than any review.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Company: Tana Labs, Inc. Europe-based (careers page specifies a CET ±3h timezone requirement); the outliner product dates to 2020–2024 under the plain name "Tana". FUNDING: I could NOT verify a figure in this session — tana.inc/mission and tana.inc/careers disclose no funding, no founders, no team size and no user numbers, and Crunchbase returned 403. The brief's "$25M raised, 160K waitlist" is therefore UNVERIFIED HERE (it is not contradicted either; the careers page describes "a friendly team with enormous ambitions" building "a new AI-native collaboration platform for teams", which is consistent with a funded team). SCALE / RATINGS: Tana Outliner iOS — 4.02/5 from just 50 ratings (Apple iTunes API, US, Aug 2026); last release v1.7.3 on 8 Jun 2026 with release notes reading only "Bug fixes and performance improvements", i.e. nearly three months stale and clearly deprioritised. Android com.tana.android ("Tana - notes, AI, voice memos") — 2.9 stars, 10K+ downloads per the APKCombo mirror; Google Play itself would not render for direct verification, so treat as SECOND-HAND. A 2.9 on Android is bad by any standard. NEW TANA: community launch was May 2026 (per the May 2026 update: voice-driven meeting platform, live digest, "Hey Tana" voice agent, agent builder, canvas, prism-based visual identity), with early access around $20/mo from April 2026. It has no app-store presence at all yet because native mobile is unshipped. STATUS READ: neither product is dead, but the outliner is in maintenance-plus mode ("core reliability, paper cuts, and long-requested features", Q2 priorities listed as collapsible fields, grouping improvements and desktop startup optimization) while all strategic energy has moved to the meeting platform. The company is effectively running a controlled retreat from the pure PKM position — a very loud signal about whether schema-first personal capture retains users.

**Platforms**

Tana Outliner: web, desktop (Mac, Windows, Linux, with offline support), iOS (App Store id6499428869, v1.7.3), Android (com.tana.android) · New Tana: web app / installable PWA (desktop and mobile browsers), desktop app for Mac, Windows and Linux · New Tana native iOS and Android: announced as 'coming soon' — NOT shipped as of Aug 2026 · Both: MCP server; Tana Outliner also has an Input API and a local API

**Pricing**

NEW TANA (tana.inc/pricing, per user, verified Aug 2026): Free $0 — host 5 meetings/month with AI transcripts and summaries, 1 calendar connection, 50 AI queries. Pro $20/user/mo early-bird (regular $30), billed yearly or monthly — "20× more AI than the free plan", unlimited hosted meetings, botless meeting agent for Zoom/Teams/Google Meet, integrations and MCP. Max $80/user/mo early-bird (regular $120) — "5× more AI than pro", dedicated support, unlimited agents/skills/types, integration support. Business — custom, billed yearly: SAML SSO, advanced security/controls, dedicated CSM, audit logs, custom data policies. AI CREDITS are a metered second axis on top of the seat price — you can "top up AI credits with custom amounts whenever needed". 60-day refund window on yearly plans; data remains accessible and exportable after downgrade or cancellation. Free trial, no credit card. TANA OUTLINER: three tiers Free / Plus / Pro, monthly or yearly, each with a monthly AI credit quota plus purchasable extra credits. Verified iOS in-app prices (App Store id6499428869): Plus $10.00/mo or $96.00/yr; Pro $17.99/mo or $167.99/yr. 14-day free trial. NOTE: voice memo transcription is metered against AI credits at "approximately 50 AI credits per 15 minutes of transcription" — so heavy voice capture directly burns the same budget as AI chat. That is a structural disincentive to capture, which is exactly backwards for a capture product.

**Capture UX**

TANA OUTLINER MOBILE — the strongest capture UX of the two products reviewed, and better than Voicenotes on entry points. ENTRY POINTS: (a) Capture tab in the app, offering five methods — Write, Voice, Camera, Media, Files; (b) configurable HOME SCREEN WIDGETS on both iOS and Android giving "one-tap access to capture modes including writing, voice recording, and photo capture", each widget pre-configured with a destination and supertag; (c) LOCK SCREEN widgets and Control Center widgets on iOS — "add shortcuts to open your favorite voice capture workflows"; (d) Spotlight quick actions, Siri voice commands, Shortcuts integration, and Action Button mapping on iPhone 15 Pro+; (e) the voice icon directly on a Supertag in the Supertag tab, which "starts immediately". LOCKED-PHONE TO RECORDING, LITERAL COUNT: with a pre-configured lock-screen widget, ONE TAP from the lock screen opens the configured voice-capture workflow — Tana's docs do not state whether recording auto-arms, so treat as 1 tap to the recorder, auto-start UNVERIFIED. Via the Capture tab it is 2 taps (Capture → Voice) plus, optionally, destination and supertag selection before you speak. Wall-clock seconds: not published, unverified. THE ARCHITECTURALLY IMPORTANT PART: you choose the DESTINATION (Today / Tomorrow / Inbox / a pinned node) and optionally the SUPERTAG **BEFORE** you speak. This is the declare-the-schema-first design. It removes classification risk entirely — the AI never guesses what kind of thing this is — at the cost of putting a decision in front of the user at the exact moment they wanted zero friction. Default destination is "Today"; redirecting requires a long-press on the new target and a confirm. RECORDING LIMIT: 1 hour per recording, with system notifications as you approach it. You can pause, resume or cancel before sending. OFFLINE: not documented anywhere — treat as unsupported/unverified for mobile capture (the desktop app has offline support; the mobile docs are silent). NEW TANA capture is meeting-shaped instead: a native video call in the app, plus "botless capture" of external Zoom/Teams/Meet calls via system audio, plus a "Hey Tana" voice agent (9 languages, shipped May 2026), plus a mid-call Capture button where you grab a conversation segment and pick "Auto" or a specific type (Task, Bug, Decision, custom). Native iOS/Android apps for the new Tana are still "coming soon" — it is web/PWA and desktop (Mac, Windows, Linux) only right now.

**Processing and AI**

TANA OUTLINER: transcription is SERVER-SIDE, not on-device, in 91 languages with three modes — device system language (default), auto-detect, or manual selection. Metered at ~50 AI credits per 15 minutes. THE AUTOFILL STEP IS THE INTERESTING BIT: once a supertag is selected, "Tana Outliner will run AI processes to fill in as much of its contents as possible, including title, description and fields." The docs advise "Mention the field names explicitly for better auto-fill" — i.e. the user is coached to speak the schema aloud, which is a real usability tax. Documented limitations of autofill: auto-initialization only works for date fields, search nodes are unsupported inside AI instructions, and AUTOFILL CANNOT BE DISABLED. Models are user-selectable and current — Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4 and Gemini 3.1 Pro were all added by April 2026, with GPT-5.4 as the default chat model. Multi-provider (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google). No LLM training on user data. Other AI surfaces: AI chat (with voice input and full chat history), AI command nodes (custom prompts bound to a node), AI agents, a meeting agent that does botless transcription, image generation, and Mermaid diagram rendering from chat. AI voice chat on iOS/Android can search both Tana and the web. Two-way voice conversation with an AI assistant configured for a specific supertag is in Early Access on iOS. NEW TANA: real-time transcription in 33 languages, following the org's default content language, editable after the fact (select/delete/remove segments). Automatic screen captures from shared screens with AI-generated descriptions, deduplicated so only visually distinct frames are kept. Live meeting digest with tagline, key takeaways, topics and next steps generated during the call. "Hey Tana" wake-word voice agent in 9 languages. AI auto-detects and supertags Persons, Organizations, Tasks, Locations and Products. Multi-provider LLM support and MCP server integration. Metered by AI credits and AI queries.

**Data model**

THIS IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE PART OF THE TEARDOWN — TWO DIFFERENT SCHEMAS FROM THE SAME COMPANY, THREE YEARS APART.

=== TANA OUTLINER (the original, documented at outliner.tana.inc/learn) ===
NODE is the atom. "Every bullet point in Tana Outliner's outline is a node." Every node gets a unique nodeID on creation, visible in its URL; "even though two nodes may look the same and spell out the exact same thing, they are considered different if they have different IDs." A node owns exactly ONE parent — its single source of truth — and any node can be zoomed into as a full page. Node types available via the API: plain, field, reference, date, url, boolean, file.
REFERENCE is a mirror of a node that links back to the original: "Every reference has a direct connection to their original node. You can't have a reference without an original node that it refers to." Editing any instance updates every mirror; deleting a reference removes only that mirror. Created by @-mention, copy-ref/paste, or inline reference with an alias. Each node shows a REFERENCES section listing every citation of it plus UNLINKED MENTIONS (places its name appears in text without a formal link) which you can promote to real links. A reference counter shows connectivity. References can span workspaces. THE GRAPH IS THEREFORE EMERGENT: nodes are vertices, references are edges.
SUPERTAG is the typed-object layer and the direct analogue of Delta's capture/action/project/contact split. "Supertags turn a node into a typed object with a template of fields." The docs are explicit about the semantics: 'buy milk' IS a Task, not merely related to tasks. Applying a supertag instantiates a template of fields and default child nodes. Fields are added to the template by typing `>` in the supertag config, or added ad-hoc to an instance and then right-click → "Add to template". Default values are supported (e.g. new Tasks default to status Inbox). SUPERTAGS INHERIT: the "Extend" feature lets a "Design Task" extend "Task" — the child inherits the parent's fields, cannot modify inherited content, but can add fields and override defaults. A node can carry MULTIPLE supertags (applied with `#[tag name]`, bulk-appliable across a selection). Every supertag is also a COLLECTION: clicking it opens a dedicated page showing "a table of all supertagged nodes". The supertag definition is itself a node (accessible via `@#`).
FIELD — nine types: Plain, Options, Options from supertag, Date (linked to Day nodes), Number (with min/max), Tana user (@mention of a workspace member), URL, Email, Checkbox. Each single- or multi-valued. "Options from supertag" is the relational primitive — a field on one node that points at other nodes carrying a given supertag. That is exactly how a capture links to a Project or a Contact. A special semantic function, "Part of", turns an Options-from-supertag field into a strict tree where "each node is part of exactly one other node" — i.e. real containment, not just linking. Fields express "has a" relationships. AUTO-INITIALIZE rather than static defaults: on first application of a supertag a field can copy a value from an ancestor node with a matching field, reference an ancestor node with a given supertag, or populate the current date / current user. PINNED FIELDS "always show on top in supertag instances and are displayed with a border line", and lead the filter/sort/group menus.
SEARCH NODE — a saved live query, bullet rendered as a magnifying glass. Conditions include supertag, field-with-value, plain text, dates, regex, TODO/DONE/NOT DONE, CREATED LAST n DAYS, EDITED LAST n DAYS, comparison operators >LT/>GT, >CHILD OF, >OWNED BY, >LINKS TO, and dynamic PARENT / GRANDPARENT references with dot notation into ancestor field values. All conditions AND by default. Returns up to 2500 nodes as references, so editing a result edits the original. IMPORTANT CAVEAT: "live searches only run when expanded" — they do not evaluate in the background.
VIEWS — the same nodes render as outline, table, cards, calendar, kanban or list.
INBOX / UNPROCESSED STATE — YES, but weakly. "Inbox" is one of four capture destinations on mobile (Today / Tomorrow / Inbox / pinned node) and is one of three special targetNodeId values in the Input API (`INBOX`, `SCHEMA`, or a node id; default is Library). It is a destination node, not a status flag — there is no built-in "processed" transition; you triage by dragging/re-parenting or by supertag-driven search nodes. The Task supertag convention of a Status field defaulting to "Inbox" is the closer analogue of a real triage state.
OTHER: WORKSPACES (nodes can move between them; references can span them), the LIBRARY (default home for API-created nodes), DAILY NOTES (day/week/month/year pages with templates), COMMAND LINE (keyboard-driven menu for built-in and custom actions).
INPUT API (outliner.tana.inc/learn/features/input-api) — the schema in machine-readable form: POST to https://europe-west1-tagr-prod.cloudfunctions.net/addToNodeV2 with a workspace API token. Payload = { targetNodeId, nodes: [...] }. Each node = { name, description, dataType, id, attributeId, supertags: [{id}], children: [...] }. System supertags SYS_T01 (a supertag) and SYS_T02 (a field). A "Show API Schema" command generates the correct JSON for any supertag. Constraints: 1 request/second/token, max 100 nodes per request, 5,000-character payload, POST only, and only for workspaces under 750,000 nodes. Also a local API and MCP server.
HOW A CAPTURE BECOMES A TASK/PROJECT/PERSON LINK: the user selects a supertag AT CAPTURE TIME; AI then autofills that supertag's fields from the transcript; a field of type "Options from supertag" resolves the Project or Person reference. So the linking is schema-driven and pre-declared, not inferred.
EDITABILITY OF AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE: fully editable — every autofilled field is just a field you can retype — but there is NO review/approval gate and autofill cannot be turned off. The AI writes directly into your graph.

=== NEW TANA (tana.inc/learn/features) — the redesign ===
The company REPLACED supertags with a simpler model and ADDED the approval gate the outliner lacked.
DOC is the atom now (not the bullet). TYPE replaces supertag: "give your documents a defined set of fields, so everything of the same type shares one structure." Only FOUR field kinds — Text, Link (reference to another doc, with a configurable REVERSE LABEL, i.e. named bidirectional edges), Date, and Member — each single- or multi-valued. Note what was dropped versus supertags: no Number, no Email, no Checkbox, no Options, no inheritance/Extend, no auto-initialize. NO PREBUILT TYPE CATALOGUE AT ALL: "No templates to pick from, no pre-made catalogue. Every type you need, you can just ask for" — you describe what you want to track in AI chat and "the AI will create it, with fields, workflow, and AI instructions." The schema itself is now AI-generated on demand.
TASK — "documents with a state and an assignee, gathered in the tasks view." Four states: INBOX (explicitly "where new tasks land for triage and prioritization"), In Progress (or custom workflow states for typed tasks), Completed, Later. Assignable by dropdown or by dragging a task onto a person in the People view. "Pin to Date" surfaces it in the Today view alongside meetings. Created four ways: manually; by AI during conversation; extracted from meeting outcomes; or automatically when you create an entry of a workflow type.
MEETING — a container holding the video call, a speaker-and-timestamp transcript (33 languages, editable), auto screen captures with AI descriptions (deduplicated), an attendee roster, PINNED DOCS (existing or newly created), and a summary doc (tagline, key takeaways, topics, next steps). Bidirectional calendar sync with Google Calendar and Outlook. Guests join by short link with read-only access to the digest and pinned public docs.
OUTCOME + THE "FOR REVIEW" PROPOSAL GATE — THE SINGLE MOST RELEVANT MECHANISM FOR DELTA. Two paths: during the call the user manually presses Capture on a conversation segment and picks "Auto" or a specific type (Task, Bug, Decision, custom); after the call the system extracts outcomes automatically and, verbatim, AI-generated items "wait under For Review as proposals until you accept them." Once accepted they become normal docs that can be edited, typed, and distributed to people or spaces. Extraction "intelligently assigns tasks to teammates when conversation clearly indicates ownership". Tasks created by AI in chat likewise "appear as proposals for your review." So: NOTHING AI-DERIVED ENTERS THE GRAPH WITHOUT HUMAN ACCEPTANCE — the exact opposite of the outliner's un-disableable autofill.
CONTEXT GRAPH + SMART MERGING — the deduplication answer: rather than creating a new doc each time, the system "updates the doc you already have instead of piling up next to it", and extraction "prioritizes updating pinned documents (like Product Tracks) rather than creating parallel ones." Entities named: projects, people, decisions, docs. Marketing claim: "structured context with explicit relationships, so it can reason over how things connect", positioned against flat-text RAG.
OTHER ENTITIES: SPACE (access-controlled container), PEOPLE view, TODAY (tasks pinned to date + meetings), CANVAS (spatial collaboration), CHAT, SKILL (a reusable natural-language-defined extraction capability producing typed outcomes — action items with due dates, decisions, open questions, metrics, risks, feature requests, bug reports, stakeholder profiles), AGENT ("an always-on agent that runs your skills between meetings", 13 prebuilt ones named including Meeting Chief of Staff, Follow-up Keeper, Deal Desk, Account Guardian, Research Synthesizer; both skills and agents are authored by describing them in plain language and pasting the generated setup into Tana chat).

**Organisation and retrieval**

TANA OUTLINER: retrieval is powerful but expert-only. Supertag pages give an automatic table of every instance. Search nodes are genuinely expressive (supertag + field value + >CHILD OF + >OWNED BY + >LINKS TO + date operators + regex + dynamic PARENT/GRANDPARENT with dot notation into ancestor fields) and results are live references, so editing a result edits the source. The reference section on every node gives backlinks plus unlinked mentions. Views: outline, table, cards, calendar, kanban, list. Daily notes give a date spine. AI chat with voice input can search Tana and the web (mobile). MCP + local API expose the graph to external assistants. THE CATCH: search nodes cap at 2500 results, "only run when expanded" (so a collapsed dashboard is stale), the mobile app cannot run "certain complex search queries requiring full graph data", and search nodes are explicitly unsupported inside AI instructions. Setting a query up requires understanding supertags, fields and operators — this is the "steep learning curve before value" in the brief, and it is real: the payoff arrives only after you have built both a schema and the queries over it. NEW TANA: retrieval is reframed as the context graph plus AI chat plus the Today view plus the tasks view; the pitch is that explicit typed relationships let the AI "reason over how things connect" and reduce hallucination versus flat search. Docs, outcomes, meetings, people and tasks are all cross-linked by Link fields with named reverse labels, so backlinks are semantic rather than generic. Smart merging keeps the retrieval surface from accumulating near-duplicates — the failure mode that kills long-lived note graphs.

**Outputs and integrations**

TANA OUTLINER: Input API (HTTP POST, workspace token, 1 req/s, 100 nodes, 5000 chars, workspaces under 750k nodes), a LOCAL API, and an MCP server for external AI assistants; a global web clipper (Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Space, Apr 2026); "copy node ID" command for MCP workflows; data export supported. NEW TANA: GitHub, Linear, Jira, Slack, Google Calendar and Outlook (bidirectional calendar sync), botless meeting capture for Zoom, Microsoft Teams and Google Meet, MCP server integration, and handoff to coding tools including Claude Code and Cursor (May 2026). Outcomes can be shipped to Linear/Jira/GitHub with full context, and accepted outcomes can be distributed to specific people or spaces. Agents can write digests, tickets and briefings and post to Slack or update CRM. Guest access via short share link, no account needed. Data portability and export are explicitly promised even after downgrade or cancellation. Compliance: SOC 2 Type 2 examination completed March–June 2026, GDPR, HIPAA in progress, no LLM training on user data.

**Strengths**

- The most rigorous data model in this competitive set by a wide margin. Typed objects with field templates, inheritance, node-valued relational fields, containment semantics ('Part of'), and a real emergent reference graph — Voicenotes has flat tags by comparison.
- Declaring the supertag BEFORE speaking eliminates classification risk entirely: the AI only fills fields, it never guesses what kind of thing this is. Zero miscategorisation by construction.
- Best-in-class capture entry points: home-screen AND lock-screen AND Control Center widgets, each pre-bound to a destination and a supertag, plus Siri, Shortcuts, Spotlight and Action Button. One tap from the lock screen into a pre-typed voice capture.
- The 'For Review' proposal gate in new Tana is the correct pattern: AI-extracted outcomes and AI-created tasks 'wait under For Review as proposals until you accept them.' Nothing machine-generated silently enters the graph.
- Smart merging — 'updates the doc you already have instead of piling up next to it' — is a real answer to the duplicate-entity problem that eventually ruins every AI-populated knowledge base.
- Search nodes are genuinely expressive live queries (field comparisons, >CHILD OF, >OWNED BY, >LINKS TO, dynamic PARENT/GRANDPARENT dot-notation into ancestor fields) returning editable live references.
- Model currency and choice: Claude Sonnet/Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4, Gemini 3.1 Pro, multi-provider, no training on user data, SOC 2 Type 2.
- 91-language transcription on the outliner; 33 with speaker ID and timestamps on the meeting platform.
- Well-documented Input API with a 'Show API Schema' command that emits the correct JSON for any supertag — a nice pattern for making a typed graph writable by external agents.
- The company is willing to kill its own sacred cow: replacing supertags with a simpler four-field-kind Type system and adding an approval gate shows real learning from its own churn data.

**Weaknesses**

- The company's own strategic retreat is the loudest weakness. tana.inc — the domain, the brand, the marketing — has been handed to a NEW product, and the original is exiled to outliner.tana.inc under a renamed app. Three years of schema-first PKM did not produce a business.
- 'Steep learning curve before value' is structurally true, not a marketing problem: value arrives only after you have designed supertags, designed fields, and written search nodes over them. Verbatim 2-star App Store review (mountainmusicgeek, 18 Feb 2025): "Unless I'm totally missing something, there's no simple way just to sit down and add notes...just a glorified 'capture tool'."
- Mobile ratings are weak and the app is neglected. iOS 4.02/5 from only 50 ratings after years; Android 2.9 stars. Latest iOS release (8 Jun 2026) shipped with notes reading only 'Bug fixes and performance improvements'.
- Voice transcription is METERED AGAINST AI CREDITS (~50 credits per 15 minutes) on the same budget as AI chat. Charging per minute of capture actively discourages the behaviour a capture product needs.
- Autofill CANNOT BE DISABLED on the outliner (stated as a known limitation), and there is no review gate there — AI writes straight into fields in your graph. The company clearly agreed this was wrong: the new product added exactly the gate the old one lacks.
- Documented autofill limitations that undercut the schema-first premise: auto-initialization works only for date fields, and search nodes are unsupported inside AI instructions.
- Users are coached to speak the schema out loud — 'Mention the field names explicitly for better auto-fill'. That is the opposite of speaking a raw thought, and it is the real cost of the declare-first design.
- No offline documentation for mobile capture. Desktop has offline support; the mobile docs are silent, which for a capture product is a red flag.
- Search nodes 'only run when expanded' and cap at 2500 nodes; mobile cannot run queries needing full graph data. Retrieval degrades exactly where and when you most want it.
- Input API constraints are tight for a capture pipeline: 1 request/second/token, 100 nodes/request, 5,000-character payload, and it stops working entirely for workspaces above 750,000 nodes — a hard scaling ceiling on the very users who succeeded with it.
- The new Tana has no native mobile app at all (web/PWA only), which is a strange place for a company whose old product's best feature was mobile widget capture.
- New Tana dropped a lot of expressiveness: only Text/Link/Date/Member field kinds, no inheritance, no auto-initialize, no options, no numbers. And there is NO prebuilt type catalogue — 'no pre-made catalogue', you must ask the AI to invent your schema, which reintroduces a blank-page problem in a new costume.
- Pricing is steep and double-metered: $20–30/user/mo Pro plus $80–120/user/mo Max, with AI credits as a separate consumable on top. The free tier (5 meetings/month, 50 AI queries) is too small to reach the payoff.

**Ideas for Delta**

- GRILL OUR CONFIDENCE GATE AGAINST TANA'S TWO ANSWERS, THEN TAKE THE SECOND ONE. Tana tried both designs. The outliner does declare-schema-first with un-disableable autofill and no review — it churned. The new Tana does AI-infers-then-proposes with an explicit 'For Review' queue — that is the design they bet the company on. Delta's confidence gate + Inbox is structurally the same as the new Tana, which is validating; the gap is that our gate AUTO-FILES high-confidence captures with no proposal at all. Tana proposes EVERYTHING and accepts nothing silently. Consider narrowing our auto-file band hard at launch (only unambiguous single-intent captures with an exact project-name match) and widening it once we have data on correction rates.
- MAKE THE INBOX A PROPOSAL QUEUE, NOT A HOLDING PEN. Tana's language is precise: items 'wait under For Review as proposals until you accept them', and acceptance turns them into real docs. Delta's Inbox should render each parked capture as a filled-in proposal card — proposed intent, proposed category, proposed project, proposed people — where the user's job is one Accept tap or a targeted field edit, not re-deciding from scratch. Accepting should be the fastest possible gesture.
- ADD SMART MERGING BEFORE WE ADD MORE ENTITY TYPES. Tana's stated rule is 'updates the doc you already have instead of piling up next to it', and their extraction 'prioritizes updating pinned documents rather than creating parallel ones'. In Delta: before the understand step creates a new project or contact, do an embedding + fuzzy-name match against existing rows and default to UPDATING. Duplicate 'Acme Corp' / 'Acme' / 'the Acme project' rows will otherwise destroy the graph within a month of real use.
- OFFER A PRE-DECLARED CAPTURE PATH AS A POWER-USER SHORTCUT, NOT THE DEFAULT. Tana's widgets are each bound to a destination + supertag, giving one-tap typed capture. Delta should ship a general 'just talk' widget as the default AND allow per-project or per-type widgets ('Log to Acme', 'New action') for users who already know what they're capturing. That gets Tana's zero-classification-risk benefit without imposing Tana's decision tax on the common case.
- MATCH TANA'S CAPTURE ENTRY POINTS — THIS IS WHERE THEY BEAT VOICENOTES AND WHERE WE MUST NOT LOSE. Ship: iOS home-screen widget, LOCK-SCREEN widget, Control Center widget, Shortcuts action, Action Button target, Siri phrase, and Android home-screen widget. Voicenotes has none of these documented. One tap from a locked phone into an armed recorder is a defensible product claim; make it the hero of the landing page and state the tap count.
- NEVER METER CAPTURE. Tana charges ~50 AI credits per 15 minutes of transcription from the same pot as AI chat — it taxes the exact behaviour the product depends on. Delta should price seats and meter only the expensive downstream work (research agent runs, long-horizon synthesis). Transcription and the understand step should feel free at the point of use, even if we cap them generously.
- COPY 'OPTIONS FROM SUPERTAG' AS OUR PROJECT/PERSON LINK TYPE. Tana's relational primitive is a field whose allowed values are nodes carrying a given supertag. In Delta that maps to: capture.project_id must reference a row in projects; capture.people is a multi-value reference into contacts. Enforce it in the Supabase schema with real foreign keys rather than free-text names, so the understand step must RESOLVE to an existing entity or explicitly PROPOSE a new one — never write a dangling string.
- COPY 'PART OF' SEMANTICS FOR PROJECT CONTAINMENT. Tana distinguishes a linking field from a containment field where 'each node is part of exactly one other node'. Delta should decide explicitly whether a capture belongs to exactly one project (containment — cleaner for Inbox-zero and for project pages) or can be linked to several (referencing). Pick containment for the primary relation and add a separate many-to-many 'mentions' relation for secondary links.
- BUILD BACKLINKS AND UNLINKED MENTIONS FOR PEOPLE AND PROJECTS. Tana shows, on every node, both formal references and 'unlinked mentions' — places its name appears in text without a link — with one-click promotion. On a Delta project or contact page, list every capture whose transcript contains the name but which is not linked, and let the user attach it in one tap. This retroactively repairs the understand step's misses, which is precisely where an AI-filed system leaks.
- GIVE EVERY AI-WRITTEN FIELD A PROVENANCE MARK AND A ONE-TAP UNDO. Tana Outliner's fatal detail is that autofill cannot be disabled and writes directly into user fields with no gate. In Delta, store `source: 'ai' | 'user'` per field, render AI-set values with a subtle marker until confirmed, and make correcting one field not re-run the whole understand step.
- DO NOT SHIP A BLANK SCHEMA. New Tana's 'no pre-made catalogue... every type you need, you can just ask for' recreates the blank-page problem that made the outliner a 'glorified capture tool'. Delta's fixed entity set (captures, actions, projects, contacts, meetings, research) is an ASSET — it means the product is useful in the first 60 seconds with zero configuration. Resist any urge to add user-defined types until retention is proven.
- MAKE THE RETRIEVAL PAYOFF VISIBLE WITHIN THE FIRST WEEK. Tana's documented churn story is structure without payoff — you build a schema and queries before you get anything back, and search nodes don't even run while collapsed. Delta should ship prebuilt, always-live views the user never configures: 'Open actions by project', 'What I said about X', 'Waiting on <person>', and a weekly digest of what was captured and what is still unfiled. If a user has not experienced retrieval by day 7, we are Tana.
- STEAL 'SKILLS' AS PER-PROJECT UNDERSTAND-STEP INSTRUCTIONS. Tana lets a skill be described in plain language and produce typed outcomes. In Delta, let a project carry a short free-text instruction ('captures here are usually client requirements; extract acceptance criteria as actions') that is injected into the understand-step prompt for anything filed to that project. Cheap, and it makes classification accuracy improve with use.
- KEEP OUR API/EXPORT UNTHROTTLED. Tana's Input API breaks at 750,000 nodes, 1 req/s and 5,000 chars — a ceiling that punishes the most successful users. Ship full-account export (JSON + Markdown + audio) and a sane read API from the start; it costs us little on Supabase and it is a direct differentiator against both competitors.
- WATCH THE MOBILE-QUALITY TRAP. Tana Outliner is 4.02 on iOS and 2.9 on Android with a three-month-stale release; new Tana has no native mobile at all. Delta is Expo/React Native — parity across iOS and Android is nearly free for us and is a genuine wedge against both of these products. Do not let the web build lead and mobile rot.
