### Drafts

<https://getdrafts.com/>

**Positioning**

Tagline: "Where Text Starts" / "A Different Kind of Notes App". Marketing frames it as a launching point rather than a store: "Drafts is a launching-off point for text – use the actions to copy it, share it, or deep link into other apps and services." The habit pitch is explicit: "letting you write first and think about what to do with it later. Try it in your dock for a week, you'll find you can't live without it" and "Post-Its for the Digital Age". For Apple-platform users (iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple Watch) who want a frictionless scratchpad that routes text elsewhere. By Agile Tortoise, Inc. (Greg Pierce).

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Deeply mature and unambiguously alive. Agile Tortoise, Inc. — a solo/very small independent developer (Greg Pierce), no known funding, site footer reads "© 2012-2026 by Agile Tortoise, Inc." and the original Drafts dates to 2012; the current Drafts 5 lineage first hit the store 2018-04-22. iOS: version 53.0, last updated 2026-06-03, 4.78767 average from 10,738 US ratings (verified via the iTunes lookup API, so the widely quoted "4.8 from ~11K" is accurate). Mac app first released 2019-03-26, also on v53.0 as of 2026-06-03; the API returns 0 US ratings for it, which almost certainly reflects storefront reporting rather than zero users (unverified). Release cadence is roughly one major version per quarter with continuous point releases — v40 through v53 all documented in the changelog. Awards, all linked from the homepage and verifiable: MacStories Selects 2025 App of the Year (Federico Viticci's reasoning: Drafts combines "taste, solid design principles, and the ingenuity to accept how software is changing" and represents what "text editors should be in 2025"), MacStories 2022 Lifetime Achievement Award, MacStories 2019 Readers' Choice, Apple App of the Day and Editors' Choice, Macworld Eddy Award 2013, TIME Magazine best iPhone apps 2018. Community forum (forums.getdrafts.com) is modest but genuinely active: 210 active users, 16 new topics and 98 posts in the trailing 30 days as of Aug 2026. Free app + subscription, so download volume is not public.

**Platforms**

iOS (iPhone) — app id 1236254471, v53.0, min iOS 15.6 · iPadOS (multi-window support added v48) · macOS — separate free app, app id 1435957248, v53.0, min macOS 12.4 · watchOS (Apple Watch app with four complications) · No Windows, Android or web version — repeatedly requested in reviews

**Pricing**

Free tier plus Drafts Pro subscription. Verified via App Store IAP listing and docs.getdrafts.com/draftspro: Drafts Pro Monthly $1.99; Drafts Pro Annual $19.99 ("$19.99 USD per year (with Free 7 Day Trial)"); Drafts Pro Patron $29.99 (docs state the Patron tier gives no extra features, it is purely a way to support the developer). Both standard options support Family Sharing. FREE includes "unlimited ability to create, edit and sync drafts, and run actions installed with the app or from the Directory" — i.e. capture, sync, triage and running pre-built actions are never paywalled. PRO-ONLY: creating/editing custom actions, themes, syntaxes, custom app icons, Workspaces, Mail Drop, enhanced widgets and sharing, and advanced Shortcuts/URL-scheme automation. App itself is $0 on both iOS and Mac (separate free apps, IAP unlocks both).

**Capture UX**

The app opens straight to a new empty draft with the keyboard already up — "Drafts opens to a new page with the keyboard ready so you can type immediately" — and each backgrounding closes the current note and opens a fresh one (one 4-star reviewer complains this is not configurable). LITERAL STEPS FROM A LOCKED PHONE (Drafts does not publish a tap count; this is derived from documented mechanisms, so treat the timing as my estimate, not a vendor claim): (a) Lock Screen widget, iOS 16+, documented as having "the same functionality as a small-size Grid Widget" with an assignable New Draft or Dictate command — raise-to-wake, 1 tap on the widget, biometric unlock, editor is live. Realistically 2 deliberate touches and ~2-3 seconds. (b) Control Center control (iOS 18, v45) reachable from the Lock Screen — 1 swipe + 1 tap + unlock. (c) Apple Watch is the fastest true hands-free path and the one reviewers cite: four complications exist (Capture, Capture with Options, Inbox, Flagged). Apple's watchOS 8 change hurt this badly — docs state "All text input now opens to the on-screen keyboard on Series 7 Apple Watches, or the 'scribble' handwriting interface on Series 6 or below Apple Watches, and requires an extra tap on the microphone button to reach dictation." So watch voice capture is: raise wrist, tap complication, tap the mic button, speak. Dictation on the watch "requires a connected phone or network to work"; other edits queue and replay later. (d) Share extension (opens Drafts with edit + tag/flag + append/prepend to an existing draft) vs. Quick Capture extension, which "captures shared text directly as a new inbox draft without additional interaction" — zero-interaction capture from any app. (e) Siri, widgets, file import and dictation are all listed capture routes. DICTATION: custom interface, not system dictation — "Dictation can be used with any language supported by Siri dictation and languages/locales changed on-the-fly", "Extended dictation time", and by default "Drafts will continue to listen for dictation until you tap the create/cancel buttons" (optional silence timeout). Hard limit: "There are iOS system limits on the length of an individual dictation session (around a minute)", after which Drafts silently restarts a session with possible brief text loss at the seam. Offline dictation works only on newer devices in some languages. NO photo/image capture at all — text only, and reviewers ask for it (see weaknesses).

**Processing and AI**

Transcription is Apple's on-device speech recognition, not a cloud ASR: the Transcribe Audio & Video feature uses "Apple's speech recognition APIs" and "the same speech recognition process used in dictation". It accepts "most (but not all) common audio and video formats" (iOS: long-press the + button or share in from another app; Mac: drag-and-drop, File > Import, or Editor > Transcribe). Because of the ~1-minute session cap, longer files are chunked and Drafts inserts `===` separators between segments. It "cannot automatically identify language" and falls back to device locale. Quality caveats are documented for multi-speaker audio. Transcription was temporarily disabled in v45+ on iOS 18/macOS 15 because of "underlying bugs in Apple's Speech APIs" — i.e. they took a visible regression rather than move to a cloud engine. AI is entirely opt-in and lives in Actions, never in the capture path: since v44 the scripting layer exposes OpenAI, AnthropicAI and GoogleAI objects (Perplexity, xAI and Deepseek work via the OpenAI-compatible format), and docs are blunt that "in most cases you will need to use your own API key and account" — bring-your-own-key, no Drafts-side inference billing. Since v48, `SystemLanguageModel` / `SystemLanguageModelSchema` wrap Apple's "private, on-device Foundation Models API" for summarising and tagging on iOS/iPadOS/macOS 26+ with Apple Intelligence, no key and no cost. v49 added a TextKit2 "Modern Editor" supporting the full Apple Writing Tools interface. v52 shipped a separate MCP server + CLI project so Claude Desktop can query and manipulate drafts. v46 added text-to-speech with voice/rate control. Net: there is no auto-titling, auto-tagging or classification on by default — every AI behaviour is a user-authored action step. Custom prompts/templates exist as Drafts Templates and Mustache Templates, plus v47 "Configured Value" steps so a shared action can prompt for settings at install.

**Data model**

Evidenced from the TypeScript-generated Script Reference at scripting.getdrafts.com (typedoc), the User Guide, and the exposed type unions. THE DRAFT is the only stored first-class entity. Its properties, verbatim from the Draft class page: `uuid` ("Unique identifier"), `content` ("The full text content"), `title` ("The first line"), `displayTitle`, `lines: [string]`, `bodyPreview`, `tags: string[]`, `isFlagged: boolean`, `flagType: draftFlagType` (colour, new in v53), `folder: draftFolder`, `isArchived: boolean`, `isTrashed: boolean`, `createdAt: Date`, `modifiedAt: Date`, `createdLatitude` / `createdLongitude` / `modifiedLatitude` / `modifiedLongitude` (geo-stamped on create and edit), `permalink`, `syntax: Syntax`, `languageGrammar`, `urls: [string]`, `linkedItems`, `navigationMarkers`, `selectionStart` / `selectionLength`, `versions: Version[]`, `actionLogs: ActionLog[]`, `tasks` / `completedTasks` / `incompleteTasks`. Methods include addTag/removeTag/hasTag/recentTags, update, saveVersion, query, queryByTitle, completeTask/advanceTask/resetTask, processTemplate/processMustacheTemplate, toJSON. THE INBOX IS A THREE-VALUE ENUM, not a flag: I pulled the `draftFolder` type union and it is literally `inbox | archive | trash`. That is the whole lifecycle. The tab bar mirrors it as Inbox / Flagged / Archive (+ All, Trash). "Inbox" is the unprocessed state and "Archive" is the done state — there is no separate read/handled/snoozed concept. The three triage verbs are Tag, Flag, Archive, exactly as the homepage says: "New entries go into the Inbox. Once there, you can Tag them for organization, Flag them for importance, or Archive them for long-term storage." TAGS are flat strings with two conventions layered on top: "Tags may contain spaces and punctuation but will be normalized to lower case", and "Tags containing a forward slash `/` character will be treated as nested tags, and appear in a hierarchy when filtering tags" (`projects/a`, `projects/b`). There is also a scoped-tag convention searchable as `tag:status::`. Nesting is therefore a string convention, not a parent-child table. FLAGS are boolean plus a colour type (`flagStatus` enum = `flagged | unflagged | any`), described as "similar to those in Apple Mail". TASKS ARE DERIVED, NOT STORED: the Task class has `label`, `state`, `isCompleted`, `line`, plus `labelRange`, `lineRange`, `stateRange`, `interactive`, `interactiveRange` — every one of those is a text range into the draft body. A task is a parsed markdown checkbox, so completing a task rewrites the draft text. There is no task table, no due date, no assignee. WORKSPACES ARE SAVED QUERIES, NOT CONTAINERS — this is the single most important structural fact. The Workspace class holds `name`, `queryString`, `tagFilter`, `tagFilterRequireAll`, `flaggedStatus`, `startDate` / `endDate` (QueryDate), `loadFolder` (draftFolderTab), `inboxIncludesFlagged`, `archiveIncludesFlagged`, `loadActionBarGroup`, `loadActionListGroup`, `preferredLightTheme` / `preferredDarkTheme`, `showDate` / `showTags` / `showPreview` / `showLastAction` / `showSearchPreview`, `installURL`. A draft is NEVER "in" a workspace; a workspace is a filter + view configuration that "can completely transform the list display". Workspaces are Pro-only. LINKING is a text convention: `[[Title of Draft]]` resolves against first lines and, if nothing matches, "will offer to create a new draft with the first line 'Title of Draft' and open it"; variants are `[[u:UUID-OF-DRAFT]]`, `[[w:Workspace Name]]`, `[[s:Search String]]`, plus external `[[google:...]]`, `[[url:...]]`, Wikipedia and Bear. The `linkedItems` property exists on Draft but is not documented on that class page (unverified what it enumerates); no backlink index is exposed in the API. VERSIONS are automatic: "typical at the end of edit sessions, and when loading a different draft in the editor, if the text of the draft has changed, a version will be created", restorable via Get Info > versions (iOS) or the history button (Mac), and not Pro-gated. ACTION LOGS are per-draft records of which action last ran on it (surfaced in the list as "last action"). WHAT DOES NOT EXIST: there is no Project entity, no Person/Contact entity, no attachment or media entity, no folder tree, no reminder/due-date field, no collection, and no notion of AI-assigned structure (so nothing to make editable). Everything richer than "text + tags + folder + flag" is either a convention inside the plain text or lives in the destination app that an Action pushes to. INBOUND EDGE: Mail Drop gives Pro users a unique inbound email address; the subject line becomes the first line, the body becomes the content, and "hashtags in the subject (like `#project-a`) automatically assign tags to the resulting draft", with configurable default tags and truncation markers to strip signatures. Only plain-text attachment types (.txt, .md) survive; others are discarded with a note, and delivery takes "several minutes". STORAGE: sync via iCloud, with v52 moving draft content into E2EE-capable fields under Advanced Data Protection; backups are `.draftsExport` files whose "storage format in JSON", written to Drafts/Backups in iCloud Drive, with partial export from the draft list.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval is a hand-written query language, not semantic search. Documented operators: bare space-separated terms behave as AND (`red blue`); `"red parachute"` for exact phrase; `-` to exclude (`green -blue`); `title:TERM` or `title:"My Draft"` to search only the first line; `tag:TERM`, with `tag:base/` matching all nested children and `tag:status::` matching a scoped-tag family; `flag:any`, `flag:red`, `flag:1`; explicit `AND` / `OR`; and full regular expressions wrapped in slashes (`/\d+/`, `/.*\[\[.*\]\].*/`). Tag filtering has a single-tag mode and a multi-tag mode with AND/OR and negation. Sorting is configured per tab (Inbox, Flagged, Archive, All) via List Options. Quick Search (v40) and a Command Palette (v40) give keyboard-driven jump-to-draft and jump-to-action. Workspaces (Pro) persist a whole query + date filter + tab + theme + action group as a named saved view. There is NO built-in semantic search, no embeddings, no chat-with-your-notes and no resurfacing/review flow — the only way to get AI search is to write your own action against your own API key, or to use the v52 MCP server so an external assistant (Claude Desktop) queries the library. One 5-star reviewer describes exactly this workaround: "I use the hashtag headers as inline tags and then a script / ai post processing shuffles everything into the appropriate category notes in obsidian."

**Outputs and integrations**

This is Drafts' moat and the reason it is a useful destination list for us. Built-in SERVICE action steps, enumerated from the docs: File (local/iCloud Drive), Mastodon, Dropbox, Notion, Airtable, Evernote, WordPress, Ghost, Medium, Google Drive, Gmail, Google Task, List in Google Tasks, Box, OneDrive, OneNote, Outlook, Microsoft To Do, List in Microsoft To Do, Todoist, WebDAV. Twitter/X is explicitly dead: "Twitter (X) is no longer supported directly due to API policy changes". The scripting layer adds many more object bindings: Reminder / ReminderList, Calendar / Event / Alarm, Mail / Message / Share (system share sheet), the Things family (TJSProject, TJSTodo, TJSHeading, TJSChecklistItem, TJSContainer), Shortcut, ShellScript, AppleScript, HTTP / HTTPResponse, Credential, FileManager, XMLRPC, Speech / SpeechVoice, plus OpenAI / AnthropicAI / GoogleAI / SystemLanguageModel. Automation surfaces: Shortcuts (v50 added "over 50 new Shortcuts actions" including queries, version history access and interface control), URL schemes / x-callback-url, AppleScript (v50 extended for Mac), and the v52 MCP server + CLI. The Drafts Directory (actions.getdrafts.com) is a community repository sharing five item types: Actions, Action Groups, Syntaxes, Themes and Workspaces — free users can install and run anything from it, they just cannot author. Extensions: iOS/Mac share sheet, Quick Capture, Web Capture (with a customisable template using `[[url]]`, `[[title]]`, `[[selection]]`), an iMessage app, Apple Watch app, widgets (Grid in 1/8/16 slots, List, Draft, Task), Lock Screen widgets, a Control Center control, and Mail Drop for inbound email. Mail Assistant is a small separate Mac helper app that exists purely so Mac Drafts can send HTML/rich-text email the way iOS can. Export/backup is open JSON (`.draftsExport`).

**Strengths**

- The Inbox model is the cleanest manual specification of what our AI is trying to automate: a three-value folder enum (inbox|archive|trash) plus tag and flag, and nothing else. There is no ambiguity about what 'processed' means.
- Capture genuinely costs nothing: the app opens to an empty draft with the keyboard up, backgrounding always yields a fresh one, and Quick Capture creates an inbox draft from a share with zero further interaction.
- Capture, sync and running pre-built actions are free forever; only authoring is paid. That means the habit forms before any payment decision.
- Enormous, real destination ecosystem — 22 built-in service steps plus Reminders/Things/Calendar/Shortcuts/AppleScript/HTTP via scripting, and a community Directory free users can install from.
- Open, inspectable data: `.draftsExport` JSON backups to iCloud Drive. A 5-star reviewer specifically cites this as a reason to trust it: "everything is stored in human-readable JSON files. If the app were to go away unexpectedly, there would still be the backup JSON files it makes for me."
- Automatic version history at the end of every edit session, restorable — no user setup.
- Query syntax is powerful and learnable (tag:, title:, flag:, -exclude, phrases, AND/OR, regex) and workspaces persist any query as a named view.
- Transcription and Apple Foundation Model AI both run on-device with no API key and no per-use cost.
- Shipped continuously for 13+ years by a solo developer, and still winning App of the Year in 2025 — the strongest possible evidence that a text-first capture inbox is a durable category.

**Weaknesses**

- Configuration is the product's real failure mode, and it starts exactly at Workspaces and custom Actions — the two headline Pro features. 3-star review of v53.0: "Powerful but awkward. You are essentially creating your own UI... Just where did I put that action. What do those icon do, some are clear others are not." 2-star review of v47.2: "the interface is so ungodly horrid that I find it unusable... Basically I was interested in workspaces and no matter how much I try to figure it out or how much I use AI to try to figure it out I can't. And this is from somebody who has been using computers for [decades]." 1-star v41.1: "Messy menu system — I've used this app for around a year or more and I still can't make sense of how the menu system works."
- Text only — no photo, no audio attachment, no image capture. 3-star v43.0: "Drafts does not allow you to take pictures (e.g., of sticky notes, real estate signs, etc.). That would be very helpful since I would love to have just one inbox for quick notes, thoughts, snapshots, and other information. Without that feature, I have to open the Camera app or the Notes app instead."
- Apple-only. 4-star v47.2: "If Drafts had a Windows version (with sync), it'd be my most used app." 3-star v47.2: "Great app but not cross platform. Sadge."
- Subscription resentment among long-time users who only want a scratchpad. 4-star v48.2.1: "Just wish they'd stop trying to get me to upgrade to subscriptionware... this app is literally the equivalent of a scrap piece of paper." 1-star v44.0.1: "app update removed my actions configuration. why? editing actions prompts for a fee now. app was perfect before this. major loss."
- Recurring editor regressions in an app whose whole promise is reliability at the moment of capture: 1-star v51.0 "everything in the editor window is invisible... I cannot access any notes"; 5-star-rated but negative v50.0.3 "The cursor and text no longer shows up"; 3-star v40.1.1 "Now I can't place the cursor back in my text where I want it"; 1-star v40.1 "New update makes it impossible to select text on mobile... went from rock solid to totally unusable"; 1-star v48.2.1 iPad freezes with an external keyboard on iOS 26, user switching to Obsidian.
- Interruptions in the capture path are punished hard. 1-star v48.0: "I only use this for frictionless writing... I want an option to see updates WHEN I FEEL LIKE IT... I ONLY care about not having a distraction when inspiration strikes." Two more 1-star reviews (v50.0.3, v41.1) are solely about review-rating prompts.
- Transcription is hostage to Apple: hard ~1-minute dictation sessions with possible text loss at the seam, `===` separators chopping longer transcripts, no automatic language detection, and the feature was outright disabled in v45+ on iOS 18/macOS 15 due to Apple Speech API bugs.
- Third-party integrations rot: Twitter/X support removed after API changes, Todoist forced a v1 API migration (old APIs off in Feb 2026), and a 1-star v53.0 reviewer reports "This app will not connect in any way, shape, or form to Dropbox... they advertise that it connects to Dropbox."
- No semantic search, no resurfacing/review flow, no auto-tagging by default. Finding an old capture requires you to have tagged it correctly at triage time.
- Mail Drop is Pro-only, drops all non-plain-text attachments, and takes "several minutes" to deliver.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Model our Inbox as Drafts' folder enum, not a boolean. Capture.folder ∈ {inbox, filed, trashed} with 'filed' as the explicit done state, and make the confidence gate write folder='filed' directly so an auto-filed capture never appears in the Inbox at all. A badge that counts auto-filed items would recreate the exact anxiety we are selling against.
- Set the triage speed benchmark from Drafts: tag + flag + archive is roughly three one-tap gestures from a list row. Our AI review card must let the user accept the entire proposal (intent + category + project + people) in ONE tap, with each field individually overridable inline without opening a form. If accepting an AI proposal costs more taps than Drafts costs to triage manually, the AI is a net loss and we should raise the confidence threshold instead.
- Adopt Drafts' query tokens verbatim rather than inventing syntax — `tag:`, `title:`, `flag:`, `-exclude`, `"exact phrase"`, `AND`/`OR`, and `/` for nesting. Drafts refugees are our most likely converts and this is muscle memory. Then extend in the same shape: `intent:task`, `project:`, `person:`, `status:inbox`.
- Copy Workspaces as saved queries with view config attached (queryString + tagFilter + flaggedStatus + date range + default tab + display toggles), and keep them strictly separate from Projects. Delta's Project should own captures; a saved view must never own anything. Conflating the two is what makes most note apps confusing.
- Build our export destination list straight from Drafts' 22 service steps, prioritised by what Drafts users actually route text to: Apple Reminders, Things, Todoist, Microsoft To Do, Notion, Obsidian (via file/URL scheme), Gmail/Outlook, then Dropbox/Google Drive/OneDrive. This is a validated list — we do not need to guess.
- Ship Mail Drop in both directions. Inbound: give every user a unique email address so forwarded mail becomes a capture, and copy Drafts' exact convention that a hashtag in the subject assigns structure — for us, `#projectname` in a subject should feed the Project matcher as a strong hint. Outbound: make 'email this capture to an address' a first-class destination, which costs us one integration and makes Delta a superset of Braintoss.
- Mirror Drafts' free/paid line rather than a capture quota. Free: unlimited capture, transcription, the understand step, Inbox triage, and running built-in destinations. Paid: custom AI prompts/templates, custom destinations, saved views, research-with-sources, and bulk export. Drafts proves the habit must form before the paywall.
- Add an on-device tier for the cheap half of understand. Drafts uses Apple's SystemLanguageModel for summarise/tag with no key and no cost, escalating to BYO-key cloud models only when needed. Delta could run intent detection + auto-title locally and escalate to the cloud only for project/people matching — that cuts per-capture inference cost and answers the privacy objection in one move.
- Make the raw transcript a restorable version, never an overwrite. Drafts auto-saves a version at the end of each edit session and lets you restore it, with no setup. If our AI 'cleanup' rewrites what the user said, the original must be one tap away — reviewers of every app in this category punish irreversible edits savagely.
- Ship a one-button full JSON export early and say so in the App Store description. Drafts' `.draftsExport` is cited unprompted in 5-star reviews as a trust signal ('if the app were to go away unexpectedly...'). For an AI app holding people's raw thoughts this is worth more than a feature.
- Know precisely where configuration starts hurting and never cross that line: it is at Workspaces and custom Actions, the moment the user has to define their own schema. Rule for Delta — the user may CORRECT an AI proposal, but must never be asked to DEFINE a taxonomy, write a script, or configure a destination before the app is useful on day one. Our category list and project matching must ship pre-populated and self-improving.
- Add photo capture into the same Inbox with AI captioning. Drafts has refused images for 13 years and reviewers explicitly want 'one inbox for quick notes, thoughts, snapshots'. Braintoss has photos and no structure; Drafts has structure and no photos. Delta can have both, and it is a clean differentiator against the category leader.
- Lean on non-Apple coverage in positioning. 'If Drafts had a Windows version (with sync), it'd be my most used app' is a 4-star review, not a wishlist post — our Expo web build already answers it and we should say so.
- Absolutely nothing may interrupt the capture path — no review prompts, no what's-new popups, no upgrade nags on the record screen. Drafts has multiple 1-star reviews solely about this, from users who otherwise love it.
- Protect capture entry points as a product invariant: Lock Screen widget, Control Center control, Action Button, Apple Watch complication, and share sheet. Note the watchOS lesson too — Apple's platform changes cost Drafts a tap ('requires an extra tap on the microphone button to reach dictation'), so design our watch flow with a hold-to-talk affordance we control rather than depending on system dictation.

---

### Braintoss

<https://braintoss.app/ (braintoss.com now 302-redirects here)>

**Positioning**

Tagline: "Capture your thoughts, in your inbox." Meta description: "The quickest way to get thoughts out of your head and into your workflow. Braintoss captures your thoughts and sends them to your inbox with one tap." Hero copy: "Free your mind in 20 seconds." and "Pay once, use forever. No subscriptions, no ads." The onboarding promise: "The beauty of Braintoss lies in its simplicity. Installation takes less than 20 seconds — just enter your email address and you're ready. Then, capture any thought in under 20 seconds, wherever you are. Experience the relief of a clear mind, knowing it's all saved to your inbox." Aimed squarely at GTD practitioners and anyone who already lives in their email inbox — the Play listing's older copy is "Capture your to do list!" and a US 5-star reviewer says "This is the perfect app if you subscribe to the GTD method." IMPORTANT CORRECTION TO THE BRIEF: this product is NOT in slow decline on iOS. The App Store listing was last updated 2026-06-22 (v10.0.3), not June 2024, and the '2026 native rebuild' claim is VERIFIED — braintoss.com redirects to a brand-new React/Vite site on Google Cloud, with Terms and a Privacy Policy dated "Effective date: April 4, 2026", operated by Braintoss BV. The decline is real but it is platform-specific and reputational, not abandonment (see maturity/weaknesses).

**Maturity and evidence of use**

A small, long-lived Dutch company mid-way through a risky rebuild, with a genuinely split platform story. Braintoss B.V. (Van der Vinnestraat 29, NL; info@braintoss.com / support@braintoss.app). iOS app first released 2013-01-09 — 13.5 years old. Current version 10.0.3, released 2026-06-22, so the app is ACTIVELY MAINTAINED and the brief's 'June 2024' date is wrong. Ratings are small everywhere (verified per storefront via the iTunes lookup API, Aug 2026): US 3.85 from 48 ratings, NL 3.81 from 85, DE 3.38 from 29, GB 3.21 from 19, FR 5.0 from 2 — roughly 183 ratings across the five, against Drafts' 10,738 in the US alone. Google Play: $3.49, 3.52 average from 413 ratings, 10,000+ downloads, last updated 2024-11-28 — nearly two years stale while the website advertises v10, and the site itself admits it: "Android v10 is still in Play testing. Until it ships, the Play Store listing is the previous Android app." The company clearly re-invested in 2026: a completely new marketing site (React/Vite on Google Cloud Run, Firestore, AWS SES), Terms and Privacy Policy "Effective date: April 4, 2026", a public vNext beta signup programme with a tester checklist, and a Gemini-powered support chatbot with an internal AI-assisted support cockpit. No funding, press coverage or user numbers found (unverified — I could not run web searches to confirm, so the absence of funding/press evidence is a gap rather than a finding). Word-of-mouth reach is real but old: a US 5-star reviewer says "I discovered this app years ago from the Howard Stern show." Verdict: not dead, not vaporware, real paying customers — but a very small business whose reputation was badly damaged during a long v8 stagnation and whose v10 recovery is landing unevenly.

**Platforms**

iOS (iPhone) — app id 576226036, v10.0.3, min iOS 16.6, English only, 9.6 MB · watchOS (Apple Watch app with Quick and Advanced modes) · iPadOS — no dedicated iPad app; requested in reviews ("they really need an iPad version! For an Apple recommended app there really should be an iPad landscape mode") · Android — com.braintoss.app on Google Play, but stale: last updated 2024-11-28. v10 for Android is still only in Play testing as of Aug 2026 · No web, macOS, Windows or CarPlay version

**Pricing**

One-time purchase, no subscription, no IAP, no ads. iOS: $2.99 (€2,99 in NL/DE, £2.99 in GB — verified across five storefronts via the iTunes lookup API). Android: $3.49 on Google Play. The site fetches a localised price at runtime and defaults to 2.99 USD. FAQ question "Is it really a one-time purchase?" answer, verbatim: "Absolutely. You pay once and get all current and future features of Braintoss. No subscriptions, no in-app purchases, and no ads. Simple and transparent." The website itself sells nothing — a section is headed "No Purchases on This Website" and the terms state "Any in-app purchases in the Braintoss mobile app are governed by the applicable app store terms." No usage caps are published for email sends or webhooks. Note the free-tier question does not apply: there is no free tier at all, which is now unusual and is itself a friction point.

**Capture UX**

The app is a single-purpose capture surface: "Braintoss opens directly into the capture screen so you can immediately record your thought — type, speak, or grab a photo." Default input is typing; per the FAQ, "The app usually opens ready for typing — tap Voice when you want to speak (you can change the default in Settings)", so voice-first users must flip a setting once. Sending is one tap: "With one tap your thought is sent to your default inbox. Long-press Send to change destination, subject, or add a short message." LITERAL STEPS FROM A LOCKED PHONE (Braintoss publishes no tap count; the '20 seconds' is the vendor's own marketing framing and covers both setup and capture, so the following is derived from the listing, the FAQ and reviews, not a vendor claim): there is currently NO Lock Screen widget — a 5-star NL reviewer of v10.0 asks for it back: "Is it possible to create another widget that you can place on your lock screen? This used to be possible, but unfortunately it's no longer available." So the iPhone path is: raise to wake → Face ID → tap the app icon (or a Home Screen widget) → capture screen is already live → tap Voice → speak → tap Send. That is roughly 4 deliberate touches plus unlock, and it is genuinely fast because nothing uploads before the transcript exists and nothing waits on an LLM. There is no CarPlay support, requested in two separate reviews (4-star NL v10.0.1 and v8.1.1). Apple Watch is a first-class surface and v10 rebuilt its status handling — release notes: "Clearer status after you send — Saved until your iPhone has really emailed the note, then Sent" and "Better handoff from Watch to iPhone, including when your phone was locked." Both the Share extension and the Watch app run in "Quick or Advanced modes" — Quick sends to the default with no prompts, Advanced lets you pick destination/subject first. Other capture routes: camera ("Snap a picture of that whiteboard, business card, or sketch and send it straight to your inbox") and the iOS share sheet ("Share from other apps to the Braintoss app and then send it to your inbox in one-click" — Safari, Mail, Photos etc.). v10 notification design is worth copying: "Braintoss stays quiet when everything works — a small vibration and on-screen confirmation is enough. You only get a lock-screen alert when something needs your attention: 'Couldn't send yet. Open the app.'"

**Processing and AI**

On-device only, and deliberately so. FAQ, verbatim: "Braintoss uses your phone's built-in speech-to-text, on the device... On iPhone, turn on Speech Recognition and Microphone in iPhone Settings → Braintoss if transcription is missing. Put your main language first in Braintoss Language Preferences." The privacy policy confirms the frameworks: "On-device features such as speech recognition and image analysis for captions may use Apple frameworks (for example Speech, Vision) on your device. That processing stays on the device unless you choose to send the resulting capture to a destination." Language handling is a user-ordered preference list, not auto-detection. THE ONLY 'AI' IN THE PRODUCT is a small intelligence layer on titling, added in v10 — release copy: "subjects and photo notes are filled in more intelligently from what you said or what is in the image, so messages arrive with useful titles without extra taps" and "Subjects and photo notes are now filled in more intelligently, so messages arrive with useful titles with fewer manual edits." That is Apple Speech + Vision output shaped into an email subject; there is no LLM, no summarisation, no classification, no tagging and no rewriting anywhere in the capture path. Generative AI exists at Braintoss but ONLY in support operations, not the product: the website's support chatbot runs on Google Gemini ("When you use the support chatbot (text or voice), your messages and audio are sent to our servers and then to Google's AI service (Gemini) to generate a reply. We do not use these conversations to train our own models"), and their internal support cockpit — whose strings are exposed in the public JS bundle — includes an "L1 classifier + suggest-reply" and AI-drafted App Store/Play review replies capped at 330 characters. Delivery infrastructure is AWS SES for outbound mail with SNS bounce/complaint handling, on Google Cloud Run with Firestore. Audio was historically sent as a .wav attachment (a reviewer asks for mp3 instead).

**Data model**

This is the whole strategic point and it is a study in deliberate absence. Braintoss exposes almost NO entities, has NO inbox of its own, NO notes list, NO folders, NO tags, NO projects, NO people, and NO links. Evidenced from the App Store description, the v10 release notes, the FAQ and the privacy policy. (1) CAPTURE — the only real object, and it is transient by design. Type ∈ {voice, photo, typed text, shared-in content}. Attributes: transcript (on-device), audio attachment, image(s), an auto-generated SUBJECT, an optional short message body, a destination, a timestamp, and a delivery state. Crucially the privacy policy is explicit that this is not stored server-side: "Captures go to the destination you choose; they may pass through our systems briefly for delivery, but we do not keep them as a hosted archive." (2) DESTINATION — an email address OR a webhook URL. Multiple destinations are supported with one marked default; the Android release notes add "Create an alias for any email address", so a destination has a human label separate from its address. Webhooks are new in v10 and are the only structural addition in a decade ("destination picking is clearer when you send to multiple addresses or webhooks"). (3) HISTORY — a local, on-device log of past captures with an Edit action that re-opens destination/subject/body so you can re-send. The privacy policy describes it as local: "The app may store history, drafts, or preferences locally on your device (for example using Core Data or UserDefaults)." This is the closest thing to a note list in the product, and it is a SEND LOG, not a library — there is no search, no organisation and no expectation you will return to it. (4) DELIVERY STATE MACHINE — the one piece of modelling v10 invested in, and it is explicit: Saved → Sent, plus a stuck state, with the rule "The home-screen badge now counts captures that are stuck, not ones still sending in the background" and "If a note gets stuck, we'll let you know on your Watch or iPhone so nothing is lost silently." (5) SETTINGS — destinations, default destination, default input mode (type vs voice), Language Preferences (ordered list), Quick vs Advanced mode for the Share extension and Watch, dark mode. NOTHING NESTS. There is no hierarchy, no relation, no tagging and no triage state, because the unprocessed state deliberately lives in the user's EMAIL INBOX. The subject line is the only structure Braintoss produces, which is exactly why v10's single AI investment was smarter subjects. IS AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE EDITABLE? Yes, but only BEFORE sending: long-press Send (or Edit in History) opens destination, subject and optional body. After it is sent, you edit it in your mail client — Braintoss has no post-hoc edit, because it no longer holds the object. HOW A CAPTURE BECOMES A TASK / PROJECT / PERSON LINK: entirely by delegation to the destination system. The Play listing names the intended targets: "Braintoss works with: Evernote, Trello, Todoist, Dropbox, Things, Microsoft To Do and other apps that allow input via email." A capture becomes a Todoist task because you mailed it to your Todoist inbox address, not because Braintoss modelled a task. Webhooks generalise this to anything (Zapier/Make/n8n style), and the user supplies the schema. There is no API documentation for customers (unverified whether a public API exists at all); the /api/v1/webhooks/email-events endpoint visible in the site bundle is Braintoss's own support infrastructure, not a customer surface.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Effectively none, and that is the thesis. In-app there is only History — a chronological local list of what you sent, with an Edit-and-resend action. There is no search, no filtering, no tags, no folders, no semantic search, no AI search, no chat-with-your-notes and no resurfacing or review flow. Retrieval is 100% delegated to whatever inbox or system you mailed it into: you find an old Braintoss capture by searching Gmail, Todoist or Evernote, not by opening Braintoss. The auto-generated subject line is therefore the only retrieval affordance the product ships, which explains why smarter subjects were v10's headline intelligence feature. For our purposes this is the sharpest possible counter-example: a capture tool that never asks you to check a second place, at the price of being unable to help you at all once the mail is sent.

**Outputs and integrations**

Email is the primary and original output: any address, multiple addresses with a default, and per-address aliases. Webhooks are the v10 addition and the main modernisation. Inbound: the iOS share sheet accepts content from other apps (Safari, Mail, Photos, WhatsApp screenshots per the Play copy). Named downstream integrations, from the Play listing: Evernote, Trello, Todoist, Dropbox, Things, Microsoft To Do, "and other apps that allow input via email", plus a legacy Ford SYNC3 in-car integration on Android. There is no Zapier app, no Notion or Obsidian integration, no calendar integration, and no documented public API for customers (unverified). Apple Watch is a supported capture client rather than an integration. Notably the older App Store reviews complain that the advertised Microsoft To Do integration "only works if you use a Microsoft email account or a custom domain hosted by Microsoft" — a reminder that 'it's just email' quietly leaks the destination's own constraints back onto the user.

**Strengths**

- The core strategic insight is genuinely correct and it is the thing we most need to answer: by refusing to own an inbox, Braintoss never becomes another place to check. The unprocessed state lives where the user already triages.
- One-time price with no subscription and no ads — repeatedly praised. US 5-star: "I've been using Braintoss for 6 years and it's the best app purchase I've ever made."
- Truly instant and offline-capable: on-device Apple Speech and Vision mean capture never waits on a network or an LLM. Nothing about the capture is blocked by processing.
- Radical simplicity as a defensible feature. US 5-star: "The best thing about and number one appeal of this app is its simplicity. It has one function... I for one am glad it isn't loaded down with untested and unreliable features."
- One capture surface for voice, photo and text — Drafts still has no camera after 13 years.
- The v10 delivery state machine (Saved → Sent → Stuck) and its notification discipline are genuinely well designed: silent on success, lock-screen alert only when something needs the user, and the badge counts only stuck items.
- Webhooks in v10 quietly turn it into a universal capture front-end for automation platforms.
- Long-press Send as a progressive-disclosure pattern: one tap for the default, a hold for destination/subject/body. Zero cost when the default is right.
- Genuine 2026 re-investment: native iOS rebuild, new site, new legal basis, public beta programme.

**Weaknesses**

- Years of delivery unreliability destroyed the reputation, and the complaints are all the same complaint — the one thing the product does silently failed. NL 1-star v8.1.1: "Verstuurde berichten naar mail komen niet aan! Onbetrouwbaar dus." NL 1-star v8.1.3: "Emails do not arrive at second email address. Support tickets are not answered. I think this App is not supported anymore." US 3-star v8.1.1: "It doesn't always send the email and sometimes the items sit perpetually unsent." NL 2-star v8.1.2: "Been using this for years, but lately getting ever more frustrated due to increasing unreliability. Lots of emails are not delivered to my inbox, so what's the point of using this app?"
- The v10 rebuild has NOT fully fixed it, and has added new breakage. NL 2-star v10.0.3: "Blij met de nieuwe verbeteringen en look and feel. Maar frustrerend dat de webhook niet werkt en er ook geen duidelijke meldingen komen" (webhook silently does nothing). NL 2-star v10.0.3: "Worked before update very well. After update no mail anymore after using watch app. even after reset/re-install... Sorry, from 5* back to 2*." NL 3-star v10.0.2: "Betaalde app jarenlang naar alle tevredenheid gebruikt. Tot de laatste recente update, sindsdien komt geen enkel bericht meer in mijn mail aan en is de app dus waardeloos." US 3-star v10.0.1: "New version is a huge step backwards."
- The rebuild removed working entry points and buried controls. NL 5-star v10.0 asks for the Lock Screen widget back ("This used to be possible, but unfortunately it's no longer available"). NL 4-star v10.0.3: "In the old app, I could select another email address by +, on the head screen; now I need to go another screen to select another email address as default first."
- AI-first support is actively resented. NL 2-star v10.0.3: "Service only via chatbot with standard answers." Earlier: "Contact gezocht toen werd er geprobeerd een oplossing te vinden... maar geen enkele reactie meer mogen ontvangen! Schandalig."
- Android is effectively abandoned in public: Play listing last updated 2024-11-28, v10 still in testing as of Aug 2026, so Play visitors buy a two-year-old app the marketing site no longer describes. Its 3.52/413 rating reflects that.
- A one-time $2.99 cannot fund a decade of server-side email delivery plus support — the v8 collapse is the visible consequence, and the review record says so directly: NL 1-star v8.1.2 "App was geweldig maar helaas geen ontwikkeling meer... voor 2.99 niet veel waarde."
- Historic trust-destroying bugs: US 3-star v7.0.6 reports phantom sends — "I had 47 Braintoss emails at one time, with no input from me... Used this app frequently, but now uninstalled." US 1-star v8.1.1 could not change the default address after a typo, so captures went nowhere for months.
- Zero retrieval. Once sent, Braintoss cannot help you at all — no search, no history search, no organisation. If your mail client's search is bad, the capture is effectively lost.
- Missing surfaces users keep asking for: no CarPlay (two reviews), no iPad app, English-only iOS listing, and .wav audio attachments rather than mp3.
- Tiny installed base by the standards of this category — ~183 iOS ratings across five storefronts and 10K+ Play downloads after 13 years — which is the real evidence for the brief's thesis: friction reduction alone did not compound into a large business.

**Ideas for Delta**

- This is the direct answer to 'does Delta's Inbox become another place to check': make triage PUSH, not PULL. Offer a no-inbox mode where high-confidence captures file silently and the day's low-confidence ones arrive as ONE digest (push notification, or an actual email) at a time the user picks. Never show an unbounded, ever-growing unread badge — that is precisely the anxiety Braintoss was built to avoid, and the thing that will make our Inbox feel like a chore.
- Copy Braintoss's badge rule verbatim, because it is the correct rule for our pipeline too: the badge counts only STUCK captures, never ones still processing. Our chain (record → upload → transcribe → understand → file) has more failure points than theirs, so 'still working' must never look like 'needs you'.
- Implement an explicit, user-visible capture state machine — Captured → Transcribed → Understood → Filed, plus Stuck — and surface it exactly as v10 does: silent haptic + on-screen confirmation on success, a notification ONLY when something needs the user ("Couldn't process yet. Open the app."). Braintoss's entire 1-star history is silent failure; we have strictly more to fail.
- Never let AI structuring block the save. Write raw audio plus a local/on-device transcript first and mark the capture safe, then run the understand step asynchronously. A Supabase or model-provider outage must degrade Delta to 'unstructured note captured', never to a lost thought. Braintoss's offline on-device transcription is exactly why its capture step never lost data even when delivery did.
- Ship email and webhook as native destinations on day one. It is one integration and it makes Delta a strict superset of Braintoss, absorbing their users without asking them to abandon the inbox they trust. Put 'send to…' on the review card, with the destination remembered per Project.
- Auto-titling deserves its own quality bar and its own eval, separate from intent classification. The single AI investment Braintoss made in a decade was smarter subjects 'so messages arrive with useful titles without extra taps' — and reviewers noticed. Our capture list is unusable if the AI titles are not scannable.
- Use long-press Send as the model for Project override: one tap accepts the AI's proposed project/destination; press-and-hold opens the picker. Costs nothing when the AI is right, and keeps the override reachable from the capture screen — Braintoss lost stars specifically for moving destination selection into Settings.
- Do not remove or bury a capture entry point in any redesign, ever. Braintoss's v10 dropped the Lock Screen widget and moved destination choice one screen deeper, and both cost them ratings from users who otherwise loved the rebuild. Protect our Lock Screen widget, Control Center control, Action Button, Watch and share sheet as invariants.
- Use Braintoss's review history as our own internal argument for a subscription. Their one-time $2.99 could not fund server-side delivery and support, and the visible result is years of undelivered emails and unanswered tickets. Delta pays per-capture inference costs; a one-time price is structurally impossible, and this is the evidence.
- If we front-end support with AI, make escalation to a human ONE tap and clearly labelled. Braintoss now runs a Gemini chatbot plus an internal AI classifier, and a paying customer downgraded them to 2 stars specifically for 'standard answers'.
- Do not market a platform we have not shipped. Braintoss's site advertises v10 while Play still serves a 2024 build — anyone arriving from marketing gets a different product. Our Expo setup gives real parity; ship iOS, Android and web together or state plainly what is live.
- The hardest positioning lesson: speed alone no longer sells. Braintoss is objectively faster to capture than Delta will ever be — no upload, no inference — and after 13 years it sits at ~3.2-3.9 stars with roughly 183 iOS ratings, while Drafts (slower, structured, extensible) has 10,738 US ratings at 4.79. 'Zero typing friction' is table stakes, not our differentiator. Delta's pitch has to be that the thought comes BACK — retrieval, structure, and the project/person context — and our marketing copy should lead with that, not with the record button.
- Consider offering a genuine Braintoss-compatible mode as a migration wedge: point Delta at a user's existing email destination so every capture still lands in the inbox they already triage, while Delta quietly builds the structured, searchable layer behind it. They lose nothing by switching, and the Inbox-proliferation objection disappears entirely for the first few weeks.

### Todoist (Quick Add)

<https://www.todoist.com/>

**Positioning**

Homepage tagline is "Clarity, finally." It calls itself "the world's #1 to-do list app" and says it helps you "simplify work and life," explicitly addressed to "50+ million professionals." Four above-the-fold pillars, in order: (1) capture tasks using natural language, (2) organise and filter (Today, Upcoming, custom filters), (3) plan with due dates, calendar views and recurring tasks, (4) team collaboration spaces kept separate from personal tasks. Note the ordering: capture-by-natural-language is pillar one, not a feature buried in a list. The glossary defines Quick Add as "Used to add a task rapidly. Quick Add opens a floating task entry field" and Smart Date Recognition as "Used to recognize, highlight, and add dates in task names using natural language date parsing" — Todoist names the parser as a first-class product concept, not an implementation detail.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Doist, founded 2007 by Amir Salihefendić; Todoist is 19 years old (homepage counter reads "19 years and 215 days"). Async- and remote-first since 2010. Bootstrapped: "$0 VC funds raised," profitable, and states it has no exit strategy. 97% employee retention, 50% of staff 4+ years; ~102 employees (Latka, 2024). Revenue: Latka estimates $11.2M ARR for 2025 — a third-party estimate, not a company figure. The commonly quoted ~$20M ARR number I could NOT verify from any source I read; treat it as unverified. Company-published traction: 50M+ users, 30M+ app downloads, 2B+ tasks completed, 160+ countries, 1M+ Pro subscribers, 337K+ five-star reviews. US App Store: 4.8/5 from 128K ratings, Editors' Choice, free with IAP. NL App Store: 4.7/5 from 8,800 ratings, ranked #85 in Productivity. Version 26.8.26 shipped 24 Aug 2026 with a roughly weekly release cadence — very much alive and shipping. App UI localised into Dutch plus 18 other languages (note: UI localisation ≠ date-parser language coverage).

**Platforms**

iOS / iPadOS (App Store, requires iOS 17.0+) · Android · Web · macOS · Windows 10/11 · Apple Watch · Wear OS (Ramble in beta) · Browser extensions (Chrome/Firefox/Safari, Task Assist extension)

**Pricing**

Beginner (free, $0): 5 personal projects, 3 filter views, 5 MB file uploads, 1 week activity history, 5 collaborators per project, 4 themes, and Ramble limited to 10 voice sessions per month (resets on the 1st, UTC). Pro: $6.99/month or $59.99/year (identical figures in euros on the NL App Store: €6.99 / €59.99) — 300 personal projects, 150 filter views, 100 MB uploads, full activity history, 8 themes, unlimited Ramble sessions, plus deadlines, task duration, custom task reminders, calendar layout, Insights, and Todoist Assist (Email Assist, Filter Assist, Task Assist). Business: up to 500 team projects, 1,000 team members and 1,000 guests, 150 custom filters per member, 100 MB uploads, shared templates, team roles and permissions, centralised billing, and Insights "Health Status"; the per-seat Business price was not rendered on the pricing page I fetched — unverified. Deadlines are Pro/Business only; labels are on all tiers. Notable free-tier gating for our purposes: the voice capture path is the metered one (10 sessions/month), while typed Quick Add parsing is unlimited and free.

**Capture UX**

Quick Add is a floating task entry field. Desktop: a global OS-level shortcut opens it from any app (macOS and Windows only — the help text is explicit that "Global Quick Add shortcuts only work on desktop apps"); in-app, `Q` opens it. Mobile: a persistent "Dynamic Add" FAB that you can touch-and-drag anywhere within a project, Today or Upcoming view to create a task, sub-task or section at that exact position (iOS/Android only, list layout only).

Taps from a locked iPhone, counted: the fastest supported path is the Lock Screen "Add Task" widget — raise-to-wake + Face ID, one tap on the widget, Quick Add opens, type, one tap to save = 2 taps and roughly 3-4 seconds to a live input field. The Home Screen small "Add Task" widget is the same count after unlock. Cold app path: unlock, tap app icon, tap the add button, type, tap save = 3 taps plus typing. Siri is 0 taps and works with the device locked, but with a hard tax: you must say "in Todoist" at the end of every request or Siri routes it elsewhere, and Siri does not understand the word "project" — you must say "list." Todoist's own doc admits results "can be unpredictable" and suggests "varying your pronunciation." That is a materially worse voice path than Apple's first-party one, and it is the structural penalty every third-party capture app pays on iOS.

Voice capture proper is Ramble: tap the waveform icon inside Quick Add or in the sidebar and speak. It is streaming, not batch — "Ramble captures tasks in real-time as you talk, not after completion," with tasks materialising in a preview as you speak. You correct mid-stream by voice: "Actually, I meant…" edits, "Remove that" deletes, before you confirm. Platforms: web, macOS, Windows 10/11, iOS/iPadOS, Android, Wear OS (beta). 40+ languages. No documented max recording length. Audio is "processed securely and is not shared with third parties or used for training AI models"; whether the raw transcript is retained after task creation is not stated anywhere in the docs — unverified.

The parse-and-commit mechanic, which is the whole reason this product matters to us: as you type, recognised tokens are highlighted inline in the text field AND mirrored as chips in a task-action row beneath it (project, date, deadline, priority, labels, reminders, assignee, location, attachment). Syntax: `#project`, `/section` (only after a project is set), `%label` (`@` still works but "is planned to be retired by the end of 2026"), `p1`–`p4` priority, `!14:00` or `!30 min before` for reminders, `{next Friday}` in curly braces for a deadline, `+name` for assignee, and free natural-language dates. Date grammar is deep: "in 5 days," "mid January," "end of month," "in the morning" (resolves to 9am), "every 3rd Tuesday starting Aug 29 ending in 6 months," and offset arithmetic like "50 days before new year's eve" and "28 days after 21 July." Limits: 150-character max date string, military time needs a colon (13:00 not 1300), and "dates in Czech and Turkish aren't supported yet."

Correction is one tap and it happens before commit. The help text is exact: if it "might recognize part of your task name as a date, like 'monthly' in 'Create monthly report'… just click or tap the word to turn it into plain text." Keyboard users press Delete on the highlighted token. Quick Add itself is customisable — you choose which task-action chips are visible and drag to reorder them, and you can pin the description field open — but the Project chip cannot be hidden "as every task must belong to a project." Smart date recognition can also be killed globally in settings, which is a revealing escape hatch: the docs concede the parser's "aggressive pattern-matching can become annoying enough to warrant complete disabling."

The parser is also a public API surface: `POST https://api.todoist.com/api/v1/tasks/quick_add` takes `text` plus optional `note`, `reminder`, `auto_reminder`, `meta` and returns the fully parsed task object. Todoist treats natural-language parsing as infrastructure, not UI.

**Processing and AI**

Two distinct layers, and the split matters. Layer one is the deterministic Quick Add parser — no LLM, no network round-trip needed for the highlight, instant, and it is what runs on the free tier without limit. Layer two is "Todoist Assist," an LLM suite: Ramble (voice → tasks), Email Assist (forward an email, get a task with due date and notes), Filter Assist (natural language → filter query syntax; English and Spanish only), and Task Assist (a browser extension that suggests tasks, generates completion tips, rewrites tasks, and breaks complex tasks into sub-tasks). Separately, "capture tasks from text, images, and documents": paste text, or upload JPEG/PNG/WebP images or PDF/MD/TXT documents up to 10 MB, and the AI extracts a batch of tasks.

Model provenance is unusually explicit and worth copying: Todoist says it uses "carefully selected large language models (LLMs), available through providers renowned for their high levels of security and privacy," naming AWS Bedrock and Google Cloud Vertex AI, and states flatly "We do not send your data directly to OpenAI. All processing flows through Todoist's secure infrastructure," with provider commitments that data is not used for training. Files and text are "processed securely and aren't stored or used for training" — only the resulting tasks persist.

What Ramble can and cannot fill in is documented precisely, and the gaps are instructive. Supported: task name, date/time, project, priority, label, deadline, duration. Unsupported: sub-tasks, custom reminders, urgent reminders, sections. The text/image/document capture path fills task names, descriptions, dates/times, priorities, labels and sections but explicitly cannot assign projects, assignees, sub-tasks, durations or custom reminders. So even Todoist's own AI does not attempt full auto-filing across every dimension — it fills the cheap fields and leaves the expensive relational ones (project on one path, section on the other) to the human.

No transcription engine is named. No on-device option. No auto-tagging of existing tasks, no auto-categorisation daemon running over your backlog — all AI is invoked at an explicit user moment.

**Data model**

Todoist publishes a glossary that reads like a schema, and the v1 REST/Sync API confirms it. Entities, with their documented definitions:

CONTAINERS. **Project** — "A list of tasks organized together." API fields: id, name, color, view_style (list/board/calendar), is_archived, is_deleted, workspace_id. **Sub-project** — "Projects nested under a parent task" (hierarchical nesting via parent). **Section** — "Used to split projects into parts"; API: id, project_id, name, order, is_archived, is_deleted. Sections live inside projects only; there is no cross-project section. **Folder** — team-workspace-only grouping of team projects. **Team Workspace** — "the overview area where members can browse, preview, and join team projects."

THE ITEM. **Task** — "An item on a list." API fields: id, project_id, section_id, parent_id, content (the title), description, due (a structured due object), deadline (separate from due), duration, priority (1–4), labels[], assignee, order, completed. **Sub-task** — "Tasks nested under a parent task," via parent_id. Nesting is capped at four indent levels ("a sub-task is using indent level 1 and a sub-sub-sub-sub-task is using indent level 4"), and a sub-task must be in the same project as its parent — moving it cross-project promotes it to a top-level task.

TASK ATTRIBUTES, each defined separately in the glossary. **Date**: "where you can schedule when a task should happen" — the intention field. **Deadline**: "The latest day by which a task must be completed… different from a date" — the obligation field. This two-field split is a genuinely good idea: Todoist separates _when I plan to touch this_ from _when it is actually due_, and gives each its own Quick Add syntax (bare natural language vs `{curly braces}`). **Duration**: an estimate, max 24 hours. **Priority**: p1–p4. **Description**: formatted text, links, attachments. **Recurring**: a recurrence pattern on the date. **Reminder**: API object with id, item_id, time-based (timezone-aware) or location-based.

CROSS-CUTTING. **Label** — "Tags that help batch similar tasks together," cross-project by design. Two kinds: personal labels (yours, colour-coded, persist in your list) and shared labels (created by collaborators in shared projects, always grey, and visible only while a task still carries them — i.e. they are refcounted and vanish when unused; any project member can rename or delete one and the change propagates to every tagged task). **Filter** — "Saved task searches," a query language over all the above; personal and workspace variants. **Comment** — attached to a task (item_id) or a project (project_id), with file attachments. **Collaborator** — with states invited/active/inactive/deleted and role-based permissions. **Activity log** — "A history of edits and updates."

THE INBOX. This is the part directly aimed at our design. Todoist's glossary: **Inbox** = "Default place for ideas or tasks until they're added to a project." The Inbox guide is unambiguous: "Any task that isn't added to a specific project will be added to your Inbox by default." Crucially, there is NO unprocessed flag, no triage badge, no per-item "reviewed" state. Inbox membership _is_ the unprocessed state — a task is unprocessed exactly as long as project_id points at Inbox. Processing is manual and calendar-driven, not queue-driven: "Set aside a few minutes each day or week to process all the tasks that have been collected in your Inbox," moving them to projects and adding dates. Todoist's GTD guide layers the same thing: capture ("quickly type or dictate tasks as they occur to you using everyday language"), clarify, organise (move out of Inbox, break into sub-tasks, add dates/deadlines/labels/priority), reflect, engage.

So the model is: the parser fills every attribute it can at capture time, in front of the user, editable by one tap; the ONE thing it routinely cannot decide is which project the task belongs to, and that single unresolved foreign key is what the Inbox exists to hold. The Inbox is not a review queue for the AI's whole interpretation — it is a null-pointer parking lot for exactly one field. Note the asymmetry with our design: everything Todoist auto-fills is corrected pre-commit; the only thing deferred post-hoc is project assignment.

HOW AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE IS EDITED. Pre-commit in all three AI paths. Ramble shows tasks in a live preview and takes voice corrections before you confirm. Text/image/document capture generates a preview list where you "click the x next to a task to remove it individually before confirming" (swipe to remove on mobile). Quick Add highlights are tap-to-revert. Todoist has no post-hoc "the AI guessed this, please approve" state anywhere.

WHAT IT DOES NOT MODEL. No person/contact entity (assignee is just a collaborator user on a shared project — there is no CRM-ish "people" object, no way to attach a person to a personal task). No meeting entity. No note entity — a thought that is not an action has nowhere to live except a task's description or a comment. No backlinks between tasks. No research/source entity. **Insights** exists but is project telemetry (health status, at-risk, progress, completed, assigned) plus **Karma** and **Streak** gamification — there is no resurfacing or spaced-review flow.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval is query-first, not AI-first. Filters are a real query language over project/label/date/deadline/priority/assignee/search-text, saved as named views (3 on free, 150 on Pro, 150 per member on Business), and any filter, label, project, Today or Upcoming can be rendered as list, board (kanban) or calendar layout, with display options to sort and group. Built-in views: Today ("all tasks with dates scheduled for today"), Upcoming (tasks by date over coming days/weeks), Productivity view (goals, Karma, completed tasks), Task view (detail). Labels are the cross-project axis; favourites pin projects/labels/filters into the sidebar. Filter Assist lets you write the query in natural language instead of the syntax (English and Spanish only). Insights adds project-level health/progress/at-risk widgets on Pro/Business. There is no semantic search, no chat-with-your-tasks, no embedding search, and no resurfacing or spaced-review mechanism — the closest thing to review is the recommended manual weekly Inbox sweep and the Karma/Streak nudges. Search is keyword. A real user complaint in the App Store reviews is precisely about findability: "I still have no idea where things are, and can only find them by random chance… I want to look at the things I have done, and I have no idea where that folder is."

**Outputs and integrations**

Public REST + Sync API at api.todoist.com/api/v1, including the natural-language `POST /tasks/quick_add` endpoint — the parser itself is a product surface third parties can call. Integration directory is organised into 13 categories (AI Agents, Automation, Browsers, Calendars, Email, Messaging, Note Taking, Productivity Tracking, Project Management, Task Management, Time Tracking, Todoist Extensions, Other) and references "thousands of other apps" via automation partners; the page does not give a total count. Featured on the directory right now: a **Todoist connector for Claude** (MCP), the Task Assist browser extension, Google Calendar, and ChatGPT — i.e. they are leaning into being an agent-callable backend. Trello, Slack and Jira appear in the collections. Two-way Google Calendar sync; Email Assist ingests forwarded email. Export: per-project CSV import/export, plus downloadable account backups; no documented JSON full-account export (unverified). Apple-platform surfaces: Home Screen widgets (Tasks small/medium/large, Productivity medium, Add Task small), Lock Screen widgets (Tasks, Today's Progress, Next Task, Add Task, Task Goal, Productivity), Apple Watch app with voice task entry, Shortcuts actions, iOS Controls, share sheet for files and links, Live Text scanning to turn camera text into tasks, and iPad Quick Note integration. Siri support requires the "in Todoist" suffix and is unavailable on macOS, Apple Watch and CarPlay.

**Strengths**

- Parse-and-commit: the interpretation is rendered inline and as editable chips WHILE the user is still looking at the input, and a mis-parse costs exactly one tap to revert ("just click or tap the word to turn it into plain text"). Correction happens at the moment of lowest cost and highest context.
- A genuinely deep date grammar that goes far past 'tomorrow at 3': offset arithmetic ('50 days before new year's eve', '28 days after 21 July'), fuzzy anchors ('mid January', 'end of month'), day-parts that resolve to concrete times ('in the morning' → 9am), and complex bounded recurrence ('every 3rd Tuesday starting Aug 29 ending in 6 months').
- Two separate temporal fields — due date (when I plan to work on it) versus deadline (the hard cutoff) — each with its own capture syntax. Most competitors collapse these and force users to lie to their own system.
- The parser is deterministic and free and unmetered; only the LLM layer is metered. Latency and cost for the common case are near zero, and the free tier never hits a paywall on its core magic.
- Ramble's mid-stream voice correction ('Actually, I meant…', 'Remove that') keeps the user in the voice modality for repair instead of forcing a modality switch to touch — the correction loop matches the capture loop.
- Honest scoping of what AI will and won't infer: Ramble explicitly does not attempt sub-tasks/sections/custom reminders; document capture explicitly does not attempt project or assignee. They ship the fields they can get right and hand back the rest.
- Explicit, specific AI privacy posture (named providers — AWS Bedrock, Google Vertex — plus 'We do not send your data directly to OpenAI' and no-training commitments). Cheap trust, high credibility.
- The natural-language parser is exposed as a public API endpoint, making Todoist an attractive backend for agents and CLIs — a distribution moat, not just a feature.
- Nineteen years, bootstrapped, profitable, shipping weekly. The parser has been tuned against real usage for longer than most competitors have existed — that accumulated grammar coverage is the actual moat, not the idea.

**Weaknesses**

- The parser over-fires on ordinary English, and Todoist admits it. Its own docs use 'Create a monthly report' as the canonical failure and concede the 'aggressive pattern-matching can become annoying enough to warrant complete disabling.' A real App Store reviewer: 'when it tries to help out by using natural language to do what you want, it often makes things harder… If I want to have an item like prepare for Sunday, it keeps trying to put it on Sunday's tasks.' Note what that user is describing: a noun that is also a date token, in a task title, silently becoming scheduling metadata.
- The escape hatch is global, not contextual: your only structural remedy is to turn smart date recognition off entirely, losing all the value to avoid the 5% of mis-parses.
- Findability degrades at scale. 'I still have no idea where things are, and can only find them by random chance… I want to look at the things I have done, and I have no idea where that folder is' (App Store review). Keyword search only, no semantic retrieval, no chat-over-your-data.
- No note entity at all. A thought that isn't an action has nowhere to go except a task description — a structural mismatch for anyone whose raw capture is 'a thought' rather than 'a to-do'.
- No person or contact entity outside shared-project collaborators, and no meeting entity. You cannot attach a person to a personal task.
- Siri integration is bad and Todoist knows it: you must suffix 'in Todoist', you must say 'list' not 'project', it doesn't work on macOS/Watch/CarPlay, and the docs literally advise 'varying your pronunciation.' The first-party voice path is strictly better, which is the tax every third-party capture app pays.
- Voice capture is the metered feature — 10 Ramble sessions/month on free. A voice-first user hits the paywall in days, which is fine as monetisation but tells you Todoist views voice as premium rather than core.
- Paywall placement irritates users: deadlines, task duration, custom reminders and calendar layout are all Pro. App Store review: 'paying close to $50 a year for these basic functionalities unfortunately makes the app unfeasible.'
- Missing start dates is a long-running, repeatedly requested gap — 'Right now, if I want something to automatically come up, I have to set the due date and have it become overdue' (App Store review). Users are abusing due dates as start dates because the model lacks the field.
- No resurfacing or review mechanism beyond a manually scheduled Inbox sweep plus Karma/Streak gamification. Nothing brings an old capture back to you.
- Filter Assist is English and Spanish only; date parsing has no Czech or Turkish; the free tier caps filters at 3 — so the retrieval story is weakest exactly where the user is non-English or non-paying.
- Sub-tasks are locked to their parent's project and capped at 4 levels; moving one cross-project silently promotes it to a top-level task.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Move Delta's confidence gate from post-hoc to pre-commit for everything except Project. Todoist's own AI paths fill every cheap field inline and defer exactly one thing — the container — to the Inbox. Concretely: after transcription, render the understand-step result as editable chips (intent, category, people, dates) directly in the capture sheet with a 'Save' button, and route to Inbox ONLY when project matching is below threshold. Today Delta parks the whole capture; it should park one unresolved foreign key.
- Implement tap-to-revert on every AI-assigned chip, with the literal Todoist affordance: the token stays highlighted in the transcript text, and one tap turns it back into plain words. Our transcript is longer than a Todoist task title, so highlight the source span in the transcript and let tapping it detach the derived field — this makes the AI's reasoning inspectable, which a chip alone does not.
- Steal the due-date/deadline split for our `actions` entity. Delta currently has one temporal field per action; add `scheduled_for` (when I plan to touch it) and `due_by` (hard cutoff) as separate columns. Users lie to single-date systems, and the Todoist reviews prove it ('I have to set the due date and have it become overdue').
- Build a deterministic pre-pass parser that runs on-device before the LLM 'understand' step, and let it own dates, times, recurrence and explicit tokens. Todoist's fast path is free, offline, instant and unmetered; only the LLM layer is metered. Delta pays LLM latency and cost on every capture including 'remind me to call mum tomorrow at 9', which a regex-and-chrono pass resolves in 2ms. Reserve the LLM for intent, project matching and people.
- Adopt Ramble's mid-stream voice correction as Delta's native repair loop: while recording, detect 'actually, I meant…', 'scratch that', 'no, make that…' and 'remove that' and apply them to the in-progress structured preview rather than leaving them in the transcript as literal text. This is strictly on-brand for a zero-typing pitch — repair should not force a modality switch to the keyboard.
- Copy the honest-scoping principle explicitly: publish (and enforce) which fields Delta's understand step will attempt and which it will always leave blank. Todoist ships 'Ramble does not do sub-tasks, sections, or custom reminders.' Delta attempting to infer contacts, projects, category AND intent on every capture is how confidence scores get diluted; pick the fields you can hit >90% on and leave the rest to a chip the user taps once.
- Ship a Lock Screen widget and an iOS Control (Control Center) that opens Delta straight into recording, and — critically — support the iPhone Action button via an App Intent so a single long-press from the locked screen starts a Delta capture. Todoist has Lock Screen and Home Screen 'Add Task' widgets and iOS Controls but its voice path still costs more taps than Apple's. If Delta cannot match one press from lock, our zero-friction pitch is false on the exact axis we claim.
- Do not build our own Siri phrase handling as the primary voice route — Todoist's ('you must say in Todoist', 'say list not project', 'vary your pronunciation') is a documented failure and we will not beat it. Ship Shortcuts/App Intents actions instead and let users bind them to the Action button or a Back Tap, which is deterministic and does not depend on Siri parsing our vocabulary.
- Add a global 'smart parsing off' escape hatch AND a per-capture one, but make ours better than Todoist's by scoping it: Todoist's only remedy is to disable date recognition entirely. Delta should let a user pin a specific phrase as literal ('prepare for Sunday') so the parser learns not to re-fire on it — a per-user token denylist stored alongside the project, which is a genuine differentiator over the 19-year incumbent.
- Expose Delta's understand step as a public HTTP endpoint that takes raw text and returns the structured interpretation without saving. Todoist's `POST /tasks/quick_add` made the parser a distribution channel and got them an MCP connector for Claude on their own integrations front page. Delta's parser is more interesting than Todoist's and should be callable by agents.
- Give Delta a real Insights-equivalent that Todoist does NOT have: resurfacing. Todoist's review flow is 'block time in your calendar and sweep the Inbox manually' plus Karma gamification, and their users complain they can never find old items. A weekly 'here are 5 captures from 90 days ago that were never actioned' digest is a feature the market leader has consciously not built.
- Model people as a first-class entity and lean on it hard in marketing. Todoist has no contact object outside shared-project collaborators — you cannot say 'notes about Sarah' — and Delta already has `contacts`. That is a concrete axis where the incumbent cannot follow without a schema change.
- Refcount Delta's AI-suggested tags/categories the way Todoist refcounts shared labels: a suggested category that ends up on zero captures should disappear from the picker automatically. Otherwise LLM-proposed categories accumulate into an unusable list within weeks.
- Cap our project nesting and enforce that a sub-action lives in its parent's project (Todoist: 4 levels, same-project only, cross-project move promotes to top-level). Unbounded nesting plus cross-project children is a query and UI nightmare that Todoist chose to avoid after 19 years.
- Name Delta's AI providers and no-training commitments on a public page, verbatim in the Todoist style ('we do not send your data directly to X', naming the actual cloud). This costs one afternoon and directly addresses the objection a voice product gets first — 'where does my recording go'.
