### Apple Voice Memos + Notes / Reminders (Quick Note, Action button, Siri)

<https://www.apple.com/ios/>

**Positioning**

Not one product but three preinstalled, free, disconnected apps plus two OS-level capture affordances (Action button, Siri) that route into them. Voice Memos positions as a raw audio recorder — App Store copy is about capturing anything, and the reviews that surface are musicians recording guitar ideas, not knowledge workers. Notes positions as "Take note of almost anything," a general container: "Add attachments — PDFs, photos, maps, web links, and more. Record and transcribe audio directly into a note." Reminders positions as remembering tasks "across all areas of life," foregrounding Siri voice entry, iCloud sync, Smart Lists, location triggers, sharing, and "Apple Intelligence integration for task suggestions." The current shipping OS is iOS 26; apple.com/ios now markets iOS 27 "coming this fall," whose headline is "Siri AI powered by Apple Intelligence" — natural back-and-forth conversation, a dedicated Siri app holding all conversations that you can "pick up where you left off" across devices, and Siri taking "actions in more apps" including Reminders. That last item is the direct threat to Delta and it is dated: Siri AI is "coming in English later this year" and "won't initially be available in the EU."

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Apple, the platform incumbent; all three apps are preinstalled, free, and updated with the OS. US App Store ratings: **Voice Memos 4.8/5 from 1.1M ratings**; **Reminders 4.8/5 from 991K ratings**; **Notes 4.8/5 from 634K ratings** — a combined ~2.7M ratings across three apps nobody chose to install, which is the clearest possible evidence of real use. Notes supports English plus 32 other languages in its UI and requires iOS 10.0+; it runs on iPhone, iPad, Apple Vision and Apple Watch. Voice Memos likewise lists English plus 32 languages. Distribution is total: every iPhone shipped. Not remotely abandoned — transcription arrived in iOS 18 (2024), Notes AI summaries with it, layered recording in 18.2, and iOS 27 with Siri AI is dated for autumn 2026. The relevant constraint is not vitality but device gating: the AI layer needs an iPhone 15 Pro / 16 or later, 7 GB free storage, and a matching Siri language.

**Platforms**

iPhone (iOS 26; iOS 27 announced for autumn 2026) · iPad (iPadOS) · Mac (macOS) · Apple Watch (watchOS) · Apple Vision Pro · iCloud.com web (limited Notes and Reminders web apps)

**Pricing**

Free and preinstalled on every iPhone. No tier, no paywall, no usage cap on recording length, transcription or note count. The only monetisation is indirect: iCloud sync beyond the free 5 GB pushes users to iCloud+ (exact iCloud+ prices not fetched — unverified). Apple Intelligence features are also free but device-gated: they require iPhone 15 Pro or iPhone 16 and later (also iPad mini with A17 Pro, iPads with M1+, Apple silicon Macs, Vision Pro, Apple Watch Series 6+ when paired), iOS 18.1 or later, 7 GB of free on-device storage, and the device language must match the Siri language. This is the real 'price': you pay in hardware, not in dollars. Anyone on an iPhone 14 or a base iPhone 15 gets none of the AI layer.

**Capture UX**

This is the friction floor Delta must beat, and it is very low.

FASTEST PATH — 1 press, ~1 second, from a locked screen. The Action button (iPhone 15 Pro/Pro Max, the entire iPhone 16 line, iPhone 16e, and the entire iPhone 17 line) can be assigned to Voice Memos. One long press starts recording; it works from the lock screen without unlocking; a second press stops it. Zero taps, zero app launch, zero looking at the screen. That is the number our pitch has to survive.

ZERO-TAP PATH — Siri, ~3-5 seconds, device locked. "Hey Siri, remind me to call the plumber tomorrow at nine" creates a Reminder with a parsed due date in the default list, hands-free, no unlock. Compare Todoist's Siri path, which requires the suffix 'in Todoist' and the word 'list' instead of 'project': Apple's first-party route has no vocabulary tax at all. This is the specific objection our pitch has to answer.

OTHER PATHS. Control Center: swipe down from top-right, tap the Voice Memos control — 2 gestures, no unlock needed for the swipe. Cold app launch: unlock, locate app, tap the red button — 3 taps plus hunting for the icon. Quick Note on iPhone: a Control Centre control, or the share sheet from within Safari/any app — 2 gestures; on iPad it is a single corner swipe (bottom-right) with finger or Apple Pencil, which is the fastest capture gesture Apple ships. Notes audio: open a note, tap the audio control, record inline. Apple Watch: Voice Memos and Reminders both have Watch apps.

OFFLINE. Voice Memos recording is fully local and works with no network. Transcription and Apple Intelligence summarisation require the AI-capable device but run substantially on-device; recording never blocks on connectivity. Delta, being a cloud-transcription product, cannot match this unless we buffer locally and transcribe later — and we should.

RECORDING LENGTH. No documented limit; storage-bound.

WHAT THE USER SEES WHILE RECORDING. Voice Memos shows a live waveform and an elapsed-time counter. A recurring App Store complaint is that this is all you get: "instead of just telling me how many minutes have elapsed to tell me either how long the memo is to begin with or how much time is remaining," and one user manually starts recordings at midnight so they can calculate segment timestamps by hand. No live transcript scrolls during capture — transcription is post-hoc.

REAL-WORLD FRICTION USERS REPORT. Lock-screen behaviour is not as clean as the marketing implies: "If I lock my screen. My voice memo stops recording" and "I wish that you could still use voice memos when your screen is locked to save battery" (both App Store reviews). Another: "if there is incoming calls or anything else it will turn off the recorder." So the incumbent's capture is fast to START but fragile to SUSTAIN — a real, narrow opening.

**Processing and AI**

Two separate engines with different requirements, and the gap between them is the most exploitable fact in this teardown.

VOICE MEMOS TRANSCRIPTION. Ships since iOS 18, no Apple Intelligence device required. The App Store listing states it plainly: "Transcripts automatically convert speech into text for easy reference," available on **iPhone 12 and later in select English variants**. Note what that means — after transcription, Voice Memos gives you text and nothing else. No summary, no title, no extraction, no routing. It is a transcript sitting under an audio file. Voice Memos does not appear at all in Apple's iOS 26 feature-availability language table, so the App Store's "select English variants" is the best evidence available for its locale coverage; a fuller per-locale list is unverified. iOS 18.2 added layered/multi-track recording, but that is a musician feature (iPhone 16 Pro only).

NOTES AUDIO TRANSCRIPTION + AI SUMMARY. This is the Apple Intelligence one, and it is where the actual competitive pressure sits: record audio inside a note, get a transcript AND an AI-generated summary of key points, free, on-device-class processing, no subscription. Requires an Apple Intelligence device (iPhone 15 Pro / 16 and later). Apple's iOS 26 feature-availability page gives the exact locale list verbatim: "Chinese (Simplified) - not available in China mainland, Chinese (Traditional), English (Australia), English (Canada), English (India), English (Ireland), English (New Zealand), English (Singapore), English (South Africa), English (United Kingdom), English (United States), French (Belgium), French (Canada), French (France), French (Switzerland), German (Austria), German (Germany), German (Switzerland), Japanese, Korean, Portuguese (Brazil), Spanish (Chile), Spanish (Latin America), Spanish (Mexico), Spanish (Spain), Spanish (United States)." Apple's support doc 121115 states the same list more compactly as "English, French, German, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese (Brazil), Spanish, and Chinese (traditional)." That is 26 locales but only **8 base languages**, and **Dutch is not among them** — confirmed twice, from two independent Apple pages. Dutch DOES appear in the broader Apple Intelligence language set (Live Translation in Messages lists "English, Danish, Dutch, French, German, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Norwegian, Portuguese, Spanish, Swedish, Chinese (simplified), Chinese (traditional), Turkish, and Vietnamese"), which makes the Notes-transcription omission a deliberate per-feature restriction rather than a platform-wide gap. So: a Dutch or Flemish speaker in 2026 gets no Apple audio-transcription-with-summary at all, on any device, at any price.

SIRI. Today's Siri parses dates and times into Reminders reliably but does no understanding beyond that — no intent classification, no project matching, no people extraction. The iOS 27 "Siri AI" upgrade (natural conversation, actions in more apps including Reminders, a dedicated Siri app with cross-device conversation history) is announced as "coming in English later this year" and explicitly "won't initially be available in the EU." There is also no user-supplied prompt, no template, and no custom instruction anywhere in this stack — the summarisation is a fixed black box you cannot steer.

OTHER RELEVANT AI: Writing Tools, Phone call recording + transcription (present in the feature table, language list not rendered — unverified), Live Translation, Image Wand in Notes, and Apple Intelligence "task suggestions" surfaced into Reminders from Mail/Messages.

**Data model**

Three separate schemas in three separate stores with no shared entity and no cross-links. That disconnection is the single most important structural fact about this competitor.

=== VOICE MEMOS ===
Entity: **Recording**. Attributes: title (auto-generated — location-based name if Location Services is on, otherwise "New Recording"), creation date, duration, the audio file itself, an optional **transcript** (English variants, iPhone 12+), and a Favorite flag. Containers: user-created **Folders** (flat, one level; a recording lives in exactly one), plus two system smart folders — **Favorites** and **Recently Deleted** (30-day retention). Editing: trim, replace a section, enhance recording. Since iOS 17 recording titles are encrypted in iCloud, which is why older devices sometimes show only timestamps — a real user-visible artefact ("if you move or transfer it or alter the name… the modified date will take the slot of the original recording date," App Store review).
What does NOT exist: tags, links, projects, people, sub-entities, an inbox, a processed/unprocessed state, or ANY connection to Notes or Reminders. You cannot turn a Voice Memo into a reminder. There is no derived structure at all beyond the transcript string. **This is the demand-side hole.** The product's entire job ends at 'audio + text blob in a flat folder', which is precisely why a memo, once recorded, is never revisited — there is no mechanism by which it could resurface. (The specific claim that users accumulate hundreds of unrevisited memos is plausible and matches the missing-retrieval architecture, but I could not verify it from a citable source in this research — treat the number as unverified; the architectural cause is verified.)

=== NOTES ===
Entity: **Note**. Attributes: rich-text body (title is the first line — there is no separate title field), created and modified dates, pinned flag, locked/password flag, inline **#hashtag tags** written into the body itself, and attachments: photos, video, PDFs, map links, web links, scanned documents, drawings/handwriting, formulas and equations, tables, collapsible sections, checklists, and **audio recordings with transcript and AI summary**. Notes can **link to other notes** (iOS 17+), giving a genuine backlink-ish graph. Containers: **Accounts** (iCloud, Gmail, On My iPhone) → **Folders** → **Subfolders**. Cross-cutting: **Smart Folders**, rule-based views over tags, dates, attachment type, checklists, shared status, mentions, locked and pinned. Plus **Recently Deleted** (30 days).
Inbox concept: the closest thing is the **Quick Notes folder** — Quick Notes created via the Control Centre control, the share sheet, or (on iPad) the bottom-right corner swipe land there, and they carry a link back to the app or Safari page they were created from, so opening that page later resurfaces the note. That app-context backlink is the one genuinely clever retrieval idea in the whole Apple stack and it is worth stealing. But it is a folder, not a queue: no unprocessed flag, no badge, no triage affordance, no 'this needs review' state.
AI-assigned structure: the audio summary is generated text placed in the note. It is a paragraph, not fields — there is no structured extraction, no intent, no category, no assignee, no due date. Nothing to approve, nothing to correct, because nothing was inferred beyond prose. Markdown export/copy-paste is reported as arriving in iOS 27.

=== REMINDERS ===
The richest of the three, and the only one with a real task schema. Per Apple's EventKit API (EKReminder inheriting EKCalendarItem): **title**, **notes**, **url**, **location**, **calendar** (the list it belongs to), **priority** (0–9, 0 = none, mapped in the UI to none/low/medium/high), **startDateComponents**, **dueDateComponents**, **completed**, **completionDate**, **alarms[]** (EKAlarm — absolute time, relative offset, or geofenced location trigger), **recurrenceRules[]** (EKRecurrenceRule), **hasNotes**, **hasAlarms**, **hasRecurrenceRules**, **creationDate**, **lastModifiedDate**, **timeZone**.
UI layer adds beyond EventKit: **subtasks** (nested reminders), **tags**, **flag**, **sections** within a list, images/attachments, and **assignee** on shared lists.
Containers: **Lists** (= EKCalendar) → optionally grouped into **List Groups**; **Sections** inside a list; **Smart Lists** — built-in Today, Scheduled, All, Flagged, Completed, Assigned to Me, plus **custom Smart Lists** filtered by tag, date, time, location, flag or priority; **Grocery lists**, which auto-categorise added items into produce/dairy/etc. sections — the only piece of automatic AI-ish classification anywhere in this stack, and notably it is domain-specific rather than general; **Templates** for reusable list structures; **column view** as an alternative layout.
Inbox concept: none. There is a **default list** and every Siri-created reminder silently lands there. No unprocessed state, no review queue.
AI-assigned structure: Apple Intelligence surfaces suggested reminders from Mail and Messages, which the user accepts or ignores. Once accepted it is an ordinary reminder — fully editable, but there is no record that it was AI-derived and no confidence signal.

=== THE CRITICAL GAP ===
No Voice Memo can become a Reminder. No Note can become a Reminder. No Reminder links to a Note. There is no person entity anywhere (an assignee is a Contacts reference on a shared list only), no meeting entity, no project entity, no research/source entity. There is no unified capture inbox across the three. A user who speaks a thought into Voice Memos has produced an audio file and possibly an English transcript, in a flat folder, with no route to anything actionable — the routing step that Delta's understand-step performs simply does not exist in the platform. Everything above the transcript is manual re-entry, in a different app.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Weak by design and this is where the incumbent is genuinely beatable. Voice Memos: flat folders, Favorites, and search over titles and (English) transcripts — no tags, no filters, no smart folders beyond Favorites/Recently Deleted, and nothing that ever brings a recording back to you. Notes: folders + subfolders, hashtag tags, rule-based Smart Folders, note-to-note links, full-text search across body and attachment OCR, and the Quick Note backlink that resurfaces a note when you revisit the app or webpage it was made from. Reminders: Smart Lists (built-in and custom, filtered on tag/date/time/location/flag/priority), plus location and time triggers that actively push an item back to you — the only true resurfacing mechanism Apple ships, and it only works on things you already knew were tasks. Search inside Reminders is unreliable per a 2024 App Store review: the grocery list, the user's most-used list, "isn't being indexed properly, making it difficult to locate previously completed items." There is no semantic search, no chat-with-your-notes, no spaced review, no weekly digest, and no cross-app search that spans all three. iOS 27's Siri app promises conversation history you can "pick up where you left off," but that is Siri conversations, not your captures — and it is English-first and not initially in the EU.

**Outputs and integrations**

Everything travels via the iOS share sheet, Shortcuts, and iCloud sync across Apple devices — and essentially nothing else. Shortcuts exposes actions for Notes, Reminders and Voice Memos, so a determined user can build automations, and Reminders is readable/writable by third parties through the **EventKit** framework (which is exactly how Fantastical, Things and every Mac task client read Apple Reminders). Notes has no public API — export is per-note (share, print, PDF), with Markdown export reported as coming in iOS 27. Voice Memos exports as an audio file with an optional transcript; there is no API and no webhook. No Zapier, no Notion sync, no Obsidian sync, no Todoist sync, no webhooks, no OAuth, and nothing works outside the Apple ecosystem — a Windows or Android user has no access to Notes or Reminders at all beyond iCloud.com's limited web apps. Apple Intelligence can hand queries to ChatGPT, but that is a one-way assist, not an integration surface. The Action button is bindable to any Shortcut, which makes it a legitimate third-party entry point — this is the one integration hook that matters to us.

**Strengths**

- One press of the Action button, from a locked screen, starts recording. No app launch, no unlock, no network. That is the absolute floor of capture friction and nothing a third-party app does can go below it.
- Zero-tap Siri capture with no vocabulary tax — no 'in Todoist' suffix, no substituting 'list' for 'project'. Reminders created by voice while locked, with dates parsed correctly.
- Free, preinstalled, and already on the phone of every single person we would ever pitch. Zero acquisition cost, zero onboarding, zero trust hurdle.
- Fully offline recording. No connectivity dependency for the capture step, and Apple Intelligence processing is substantially on-device — a privacy story we cannot match with cloud transcription.
- Free AI summarisation inside Notes with no subscription, which vaporises the obvious 'pay us for transcription+summary' business model in the eight languages where it works.
- Reminders has a genuinely rich schema — subtasks, sections, tags, flags, priority, URL, location and messaging triggers, recurrence, templates, custom Smart Lists — and it is readable and writable by third parties through EventKit.
- Location and time triggers on Reminders are real resurfacing: the item comes back to you at the right place or time without you searching for it.
- The Quick Note app/URL backlink is a smart, under-copied retrieval idea: a note made from a page resurfaces when you return to that page.
- Grocery lists auto-categorise added items into sections — proof Apple will ship narrow, high-confidence auto-classification when it can guarantee accuracy, and a good model for scoping AI ambition.
- The Action button is bindable to any Shortcut, which means the platform's fastest capture gesture is available to third-party apps too — including ours.

**Weaknesses**

- Voice Memos transcription is English-only: the App Store listing says it works on iPhone 12 and later "in select English variants," and Voice Memos does not even appear in Apple's iOS 26 per-feature language availability table.
- Notes audio transcription + AI summary covers 26 locales but only 8 base languages — Chinese (Simplified/Traditional), English, French, German, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese (Brazil), Spanish — and **Dutch is excluded**, confirmed on two independent Apple pages. Dutch IS supported for other Apple Intelligence features (Live Translation in Messages), so this is a deliberate per-feature restriction. A Dutch or Flemish speaker gets zero Apple audio understanding at any price on any device. That is our home market.
- Voice Memos does nothing above the transcript. No summary, no title generation, no extraction, no routing, no tags, no links, no projects, no people. The recording lands in a flat folder and dies there — there is literally no mechanism by which it could ever resurface.
- The three apps are three disconnected silos. A Voice Memo cannot become a Reminder. A Note cannot become a Reminder. Nothing links across. Every promotion from 'thought' to 'action' is manual retyping in a different app.
- The AI layer is hardware-gated: iPhone 15 Pro / iPhone 16 or later, iOS 18.1+, 7 GB free storage, device language matching Siri language. Everyone on an iPhone 14 or base iPhone 15 has a recorder with no intelligence.
- iOS 27's Siri AI — the upgrade that would close the gap — is "coming in English later this year" and explicitly "won't initially be available in the EU." The incumbent's next move is delayed precisely in our market.
- Capture is fast to start but fragile to sustain, per real App Store reviews: "If I lock my screen. My voice memo stops recording"; "I wish that you could still use voice memos when your screen is locked to save battery"; "if there is incoming calls or anything else it will turn off the recorder."
- No inbox or triage state anywhere. Notes has a Quick Notes folder, Reminders has a default list, Voice Memos has nothing — none of them has an unprocessed flag, a badge, or any review affordance.
- Retrieval is thin and sometimes broken: no semantic search, no chat-over-your-captures, no digest, no spaced review, and a 2024 App Store review reports the Reminders grocery list — the reviewer's most-used list — "isn't being indexed properly."
- No person, project, meeting or source entity in any of the three apps. Assignee exists only as a Contacts reference on a shared Reminders list.
- Recording UI shows only elapsed time and a waveform. Users ask repeatedly for total length, remaining time and in-recording timestamps; one describes manually starting recordings at midnight to compute segment times by hand.
- Locked into Apple hardware. No Android, no Windows, no web beyond limited iCloud.com apps. No API for Notes, no webhooks anywhere, no Zapier, no third-party sync.
- The AI summarisation is a fixed black box: no custom prompt, no template, no steering, and no way to tell it what kind of thing you just recorded.
- Since iOS 17, encrypted iCloud recording titles cause visible metadata weirdness — older devices show only timestamps, and renaming or transferring a memo can overwrite the original recording date with the modified date.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Bind Delta to the Action button via an App Intent on day one, and measure ourselves against the real number: one press, from locked, to recording. If Delta needs unlock + launch + tap while Voice Memos needs one press, our zero-friction pitch is provably false and the whole positioning collapses. This is the single highest-priority platform integration we have.
- Lead the Dutch/Belgian go-to-market with the language gap, because it is documented and checkable: Apple's Notes transcription summary covers 8 base languages and Dutch is not one of them, while Voice Memos transcribes 'select English variants' only. In Daniel's own market, Apple's answer to 'speak a thought and have it understood' does not function at all. That is not a marketing claim, it is Apple's own feature-availability table.
- Second Dutch-market wedge: iOS 27's Siri AI — the upgrade that would otherwise close this gap — is announced as English-only and explicitly not available in the EU at launch. We have a dated, Apple-confirmed window in Europe. Build the Dutch-language understand step now rather than treating multilingual as a v2 concern.
- Answer 'why not just hold the Action button and talk to Siri?' with a concrete demo, not an argument: Siri can create one reminder with a date. Delta takes one 45-second ramble and produces a note, two actions, a project link and two people. Our value is not the transcript and never was — it is that ONE utterance fans out into multiple linked entities. Build the marketing demo around a multi-entity capture that Siri structurally cannot represent, because Reminders has no note, project or person entity to put them in.
- Ship offline capture with deferred transcription. Voice Memos records with no network; if Delta shows a spinner or fails on the Brussels metro, we lose to the preinstalled app on reliability alone. Record locally, queue, transcribe when connectivity returns, and show the capture in the Inbox as 'transcribing' rather than blocking it.
- Harden sustain, not just start. Apple's own reviewers report recordings stopping on screen lock and on incoming calls. Delta must keep recording through screen lock, backgrounding and an incoming call, and must never lose a partial recording — configure the audio session for background recording and persist a partial file continuously. This is an unglamorous reliability feature that is directly a competitor weakness.
- Steal the Quick Note backlink and generalise it: capture the context of a capture. If Delta is opened from a share sheet, from a specific app, at a specific location, or during a calendar event, store that as capture provenance and use it both for project matching (a strong signal our understand step is currently ignoring) and for resurfacing — 'you recorded this the last time you were in this meeting.'
- Copy the Grocery-list lesson on AI scoping: Apple ships automatic classification only where it can guarantee accuracy (produce vs dairy), and refuses everywhere else. That argues for Delta's confidence gate being per-field rather than per-capture — auto-file the fields we are confident about, park only the ones we are not, rather than sending the whole capture to Inbox because one field is uncertain.
- Build the resurfacing loop that Apple structurally cannot, and make it the headline feature rather than transcription. Apple's only resurfacing is a time or location trigger on something already known to be a task. Delta should ship a weekly digest of captures that never became anything, and 'related to what you just recorded' — this is where the 'recorded and never revisited' pain actually lives, and neither Voice Memos nor Notes has any mechanism to address it.
- Write to Apple Reminders via EventKit as an export target. Reminders is the system of record for a huge number of people and is third-party writable; letting Delta push a confirmed action into a chosen Reminders list makes us the intelligence layer rather than yet another silo the user must remember to open. Voice Memos and Notes have no API, so Reminders is the only outbound hook available — take it.
- Do not compete on transcript quality or price. Apple gives away transcription plus a paragraph summary for free in eight languages, so every euro of our value has to sit above the transcript: intent detection, project matching, people linking, and retrieval. Any part of the roadmap that is 'better transcripts' is spent against an incumbent charging zero.
- Offer what Apple's black box does not: user-steerable understanding. Apple's summary has no prompt, no template, no custom instruction. Let Delta users define per-project capture templates ('for client calls, always extract decisions, owners and next steps'), which is impossible on the platform and is exactly what a power user who already owns a free recorder would pay for.
- Ship a Lock Screen widget and an iOS Control alongside the Action button intent, so users on an iPhone 14 or a base iPhone 15 — who get no Apple Intelligence at all — still have a two-gesture path. That hardware-gated cohort is our warmest possible audience: they have the pain and the platform explicitly excludes them from the free fix.
- Do not build a flat folder for captures. Voice Memos' flat, tagless, linkless folder is the precise architectural cause of 'recorded it, never opened it again'. Delta's captures must be born linked — to a project, a person, or at minimum a date and a location — because an entity with no edges cannot be resurfaced by anything.
- Show recording length and elapsed time properly, and timestamp the transcript. Apple's reviewers ask for this repeatedly and are ignored; one manually starts recordings at midnight to compute segment times. Timestamped transcript segments that jump to the audio position are cheap for us and a standing unmet request against the market leader.

---

### Capacities

<https://capacities.io/>

**Positioning**

Tagline: "A studio for your mind" — "a home for everything you think, learn, and create." Aimed at individuals who think for a living (writers, researchers, students, developers, managers, founders), explicitly NOT teams. The core claim is structural, not AI: "Capacities turns your ideas into connected objects—not files buried in folders." Onboarding doctrine: "You'll never have to think about folder structures or complex organizational methods, because this simply doesn't exist in Capacities" and "There are no 'loose' notes in Capacities because when creating new content, you make one decision: What type of note is it?" Note it is NOT a voice product — it has no transcription at all.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Capacities Labs GmbH, Germany-registered, fully remote across several European countries, ~8 people including two co-founders (Steffen and Michael). Public since 2022 — a Show HN-style launch on 2 June 2022 scored 17 points / 7 comments; Product Hunt lists a 2023 launch and a July 2024 mobile launch with 4.8/5 across 66 PH reviews. Funding: not disclosed anywhere I could find (unverified); they market themselves as "an independent team" and sell a "Believer" tier to support it, which reads as bootstrapped. User numbers: only "thousands of thinkers who use Capacities" — no hard figure (unverified).
Clearly alive and shipping fast: Release 66 (Jun 2026, AI Chat Connectors 2.0), 67 (Jul 2026, API 2.0, Weblink Analysis, Presentation Mode), 68 (Aug 2026, phone app 2.0, media via API/MCP), 69 (Aug 2026, Pro+/Believer+). iOS app updated 27 Aug 2026. They also blogged about cutting 90% of database spend by migrating to Postgres (Jan 2026), which is a bootstrapped-team signal.
App-store traction is modest: iOS 4.27★ from only 111 ratings; Google Play 100K+ installs (Play star rating unverified — the listing truncated on fetch). AlternativeTo: 4.9/5 from 8 ratings, 46 likes. Small but real and paying.

**Platforms**

macOS · Windows · Linux · Web · iOS / iPadOS (App Store id 1670188548, v1.69.7, 816.8 MB) · Android (Play package io.capacities.mobile, 100K+ installs) · Raycast extension · MCP server / AI chat connectors · REST API

**Pricing**

Free ("Basic"), $0: unlimited spaces, objects and blocks; sync; custom object types; import/export; content capture from WhatsApp, Telegram, Twitter and email; view-only sharing; full-text search; offline. Media 5 GB total then 100 MB/month. NO AI, NO task management, NO calendar integration, NO smart queries.
Pro $9.99/mo USD (16% off annual): task management, AI assistant with a monthly AI budget ("light daily use"), calendar integrations, smart queries, task actions, related content, Smart Search, unlimited media (fair use), table formulas, icon set, extensions (Raycast, Webhighlights), API access. Free trial, no card.
Believer from $12.49/mo: Pro plus beta access, "support an independent team."
Pro+/Believer+ add-on $8.33/mo billed annually: "substantially larger monthly AI budget", one-time top-ups, and an auto-top-up option that removes the usage ceiling (shipped Release 69, Aug 2026).
Mobile in-app purchase prices are higher than web: App Store IAPs list Capacities Pro at $17.99 and $199.00. The AI budget is the real usage cap and it resets each billing cycle; budget burn varies by model and feature.

**Capture UX**

There is no press-to-record and no hold-to-talk anywhere in the product. Capture is text/media-first.
Routes, all of which default to appending to TODAY'S DAILY NOTE: WhatsApp bot, Telegram bot, email-in, Raycast, mobile share sheet, mobile quick capture (incl. photo/camera), API `POST /save-to-daily-note`, and AI Chat Connectors. Docs: "our input integrations—such as WhatsApp, Raycast, or email—simply add incoming content to today's daily note." Space routing is a prefix, e.g. `/3 Some thought`.
Voice: you CAN send a WhatsApp/Telegram voice message, but "Your voice recordings will be saved as audio files" and transcription is still marked "🚀 Coming soon: Your voice messages will automatically be transcribed to written text" as of Aug 2026. So a voice note lands as an untranscribed Audio object — unsearchable.
Taps from locked phone: Release 68 (Aug 2026) added experimental home-screen widgets on iOS and Android for Daily Notes, Tasks, Quick Capture and AI Assistant. With the Quick Capture widget placed, it is roughly wake → 1 widget tap → keyboard, i.e. ~1 tap plus typing; there is no lock-screen widget, Action Button or Control Center entry documented, and no measured figure is published (exact tap count unverified). Global task shortcut on desktop is Cmd+Shift+T (Mac) / Ctrl+Shift+T (Windows). Recording length: not applicable, no recorder. Offline support exists on the free tier; Product Hunt reviewers nonetheless cite "restricted offline access" as a limitation.

**Processing and AI**

No ASR, no transcription engine, no diarization, no audio pipeline — this is a text knowledge base with an LLM layer bolted on.
AI assistant (Pro+): "Capacities AI assistant can utilize models from OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini or xAI; depending on the provider preference setting." An "Auto" mode picks the most cost-efficient capable model (Release 68). The assistant is graph-aware: it can "Search for notes", "Read notes", read "the full content of notes in the backlink section", "Generate a query" and "Fetch query results" from existing queries. Multi-step planning; it can "propose creates, updates, and deletions requiring user approval."
Auto-structuring is deliberately closed-vocabulary: "AI identifies relevant tags from your existing tags and assigns them to that object", same mechanism for collections, plus property auto-fill for text, description, date and number fields. On Location auto-fill the docs stress it suggests "from objects you already have—it will not invent a long list of entities you never created."
AI chat commands: summarize, continue writing, translate, fix grammar, critique. Image analysis extracts title, description, OCR text and colour palette. Weblink Analysis (Release 67/68, Pro) reads saved pages with Browser/Reader/Summary tabs plus highlights and annotations.
Search: full-text everywhere; "Smart Search"/Extended Search ("Search 2.0", Jan 2025) adds semantic, conceptually-related matching and is Pro-gated; saved queries have Auto/Exact/Semantic modes. Fully AI-powered semantic search queries are listed as planned, not shipped.
Privacy: "No data will be shared if you don't use any AI features." AI calls go through enterprise APIs under DPAs that prohibit training on user data.

**Data model**

SPACE (API `structureId: RootSpace`) is the top container; a user can have several and content does not cross them. Within a space everything is an OBJECT: "A piece of content in Capacities. This includes notes, images, pdfs, tags. Everything is an object." Every object has exactly one OBJECT TYPE and carries its type's `structureId`.
BUILT-IN TYPES (11): Page, Tag, Image, Weblink, Audio, PDF, File, Tweet, AI Chat, Query, Table. Plus system types Daily Note (`RootDailyNote`) and Task. Person, Project, Meeting, Book, Organization, Location are NOT hardcoded — they are CUSTOM types the user creates from a template gallery ("+ new type"). Editing a type edits every instance retroactively: "Changes there apply to every person object, past and future: you are editing the type, not a single page."
PROPERTIES on a type. Defaults: Title (plain text), Aliases, Description (can be AI-generated), Icon, Created at (drives calendar), Tags. Normal: Text, Cover Image, Number, Checkbox, Content (block-based: headings, lists, to-dos, embeds), Datetime (optional time and/or date range), Label Property (single/multi-select dropdown), and OBJECT SELECT — the relation type: it "let[s] you link a property to an object type using a dropdown", supporting single or multiple selections, TWO-WAY LINKED properties, and fixed sets.
WORKED EXAMPLE (docs, network-of-people): the Person template ships with "title, tags, contact fields, image, category (for example personal / professional / family), and a body for notes"; the docs then recommend adding Datetime props "Last seen" and "Last reached out", text props "Memory jog" and "Job title", and Object Select props to Organization, Location, Meeting and Project. Meeting ships with Date, "People Involved" and a meeting-type label; capturing a meeting means "Add attendees to People Involved and create or link a Person object for anyone new" and "Link projects and organizations when the conversation touches them." A Project page then shows its meeting history via an embedded query, and "the organization's backlinks show everyone linked through that property." This is exactly the retrieval behaviour Delta wants — and it is built from ordinary relation properties plus backlinks, not magic.
BLOCKS are the sub-object unit ("literally the building blocks of your notes") and can themselves be tagged and linked.
COLLECTIONS (`RootDatabase`) are manual, single-type groupings: "Collections let you group one object type manually." TAGS are cross-type and are objects in their own right: "Tags group your content by theme or topic. This lets you connect information across your entire Capacities space", applicable to objects AND blocks, and usable as options inside object-select properties. The stated distinction: "Tags connect across types and ideas (big picture connection). Labels help filter within a type." There are NO folders and no hierarchy anywhere.
BACKLINKS: "Instead of linking just from object A to object B, object B links back to A too." Two-way linked properties render the relation on both pages.
QUERIES (`RootQuery`) are objects too — "Queries are objects too, so you can link or embed them like any other object" via `@{query name}`. Rules filter on "properties, tags, content, or object types", plus search terms and date properties.
TASKS are FIRST-CLASS OBJECTS with their own dashboard, not block to-dos. Two distinct date fields: a deadline ("when something must be finished by a certain day") and a scheduled date; deadline tasks rank above deadline-less ones. Built-in dashboard views: INBOX (unscheduled, unstatused), Today (today + overdue), Scheduled, CONTEXT (grouped by linked object), Tags, Open/Completed, plus custom query sections. A task created inline on an object page auto-links to that object, which is what populates the Context view.
INBOX / UNPROCESSED STATE — there are two, and they are different kinds of thing. (1) The DAILY NOTE is the capture inbox: every integration appends there, and the docs frame it as "a low-friction inbox before material matures into the knowledge graph." (2) The TASK INBOX is a real filter state (unscheduled + unstatused). Ordinary objects carry no "unprocessed" flag — triage is a human ritual, not a state machine.
CAPTURE → STRUCTURE, documented explicitly. At capture the filters are "Do I want to remember this?" and "Am I interested in this?", and everything funnels into today's daily note "to avoid decision fatigue during input." At review the filters change to "Is this useful to me now?" and "Is it interesting now, or could it be later?" Cadence: "a weekly review is a strong default." During review each line is deleted, converted into a Task object with status and scheduling, promoted into a typed object (person, project, meeting) with properties and backlinks, or tagged for reference.
AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE IS ALWAYS EDITABLE AND GATED: the assistant proposes creates/updates/deletes "requiring user approval", and auto-tagging only picks from tags that already exist.
API (evidence for the entity model): v1 Beta REST is DEPRECATED and discontinues 1 September 2026. Bearer token from desktop settings. `GET /spaces` → `{id,title,icon}`. `GET /space-info?spaceid=` → "all structures (object types) with property definitions and collections", fields `id, title, pluralName, propertyDefinitions, labelColor, collections`. `POST /lookup {searchTerm, spaceId}` → `{id, structureId, title}`. `POST /save-weblink {spaceId, url, titleOverwrite, descriptionOverwrite, tags, mdText}` — "Object type of weblink will be analyzed and saved as an object." `POST /save-to-daily-note {spaceId, mdText, origin, noTimeStamp}`. OpenAPI 3.0 at api.capacities.io/openapi.json. API 2.0 shipped in Release 67 (Jul 2026); Release 68 added media upload (images, PDFs, files) via API and MCP for Pro; Release 66 shipped "AI Chat Connectors 2.0" which can "Create pages and tasks, append to objects, and build any object type from your connected AI apps."

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval is graph-first rather than search-first. Full-text search on all tiers with the command palette (create tasks, switch spaces, open calendar). Pro adds Extended Search with filtering and Smart Search semantic matching ("focuses on conceptually related matches"), and Global Search across all spaces via Raycast. Filters: start a query with an object type name to include/exclude types, filter by tag pages or tag queries, filter on any property including dates. Saved queries are objects and can be embedded anywhere with `@{query name}` with Auto/Exact/Semantic modes. Backlink panels on every object are the main resurfacing mechanism; "Related Content" (Pro) surfaces connections the user made but did not explicitly link. Graph view exists. The AI assistant is a retrieval layer over the same graph — it searches notes, reads notes, reads backlinked note content, and can generate or run queries. The review ritual (weekly, over the daily note) is the deliberate resurfacing flow. Notably absent: AI semantic search queries are on the roadmap, unreleased.

**Outputs and integrations**

Inbound: WhatsApp bot, Telegram bot (both accept formatted markdown, images, files, voice messages saved as audio, code files converted to code blocks, weblinks, `#tags`, and `[[page/Title]]` links; group chats can feed a shared space), email-in, Raycast, Web Highlights, Readwise (deliberately tag-gated, see below), share sheet, camera. Outbound: full import/export, view-only share links, presentation mode (Release 67).
Task actions (Pro) push to-dos out to Todoist, TickTick, Things, Apple Reminders, Google Tasks and Microsoft To Do — "send tasks from Capacities to your task manager of choice" via a paper-plane icon or Option/Alt+Enter, carrying due date (`@today`), labels (`#important`) and a follow-up action (mark done or delete).
Calendar integrations (Pro). REST API (v1 deprecated 1 Sep 2026; API 2.0 current) plus an MCP server / AI Chat Connectors that can create pages and tasks, append to objects, build any object type, and upload media. No webhooks or Zapier documented. No Notion/Obsidian live sync — import only.
The Readwise design decision is the interesting one: they refused to auto-sync all highlights and instead built a tag-gated sync, on the reasoning that importing everything "would worsen information overload" and that requiring a tag makes importing "a deliberate decision rather than passive accumulation", so Capacities does not become "another place of accumulation."

**Strengths**

- The entity model is the product and it is coherent all the way down: everything is a typed object, types are user-editable and edits apply retroactively to every instance, and relations are ordinary two-way properties. Nothing is a special case.
- Documented triage doctrine that separates capture from processing, with different questions at each moment: "Do I want to remember this?" / "Am I interested in this?" at capture, "Is this useful to me now?" / "Is it interesting now, or could it be later?" at weekly review. This is the clearest articulation of capture/triage separation in any shipping notes app.
- Zero-decision capture: every integration dumps into today's daily note explicitly "to avoid decision fatigue during input."
- AI is constrained rather than creative: auto-tagging picks only from existing tags, auto-fill suggests only "from objects you already have—it will not invent", and all creates/updates/deletes need explicit user approval. This is why their AI structure is trusted.
- Tasks are first-class objects with a genuinely good view set — Inbox (unscheduled+unstatused), Today (incl. overdue), Scheduled, Context (grouped by linked object), Tags — plus separate deadline vs scheduled dates.
- Cheap multi-channel ingest: WhatsApp and Telegram bots on the FREE tier, accepting markdown, media, links, tags and `[[wiki links]]`, with space routing via a `/3` prefix.
- Task actions export to six external task managers rather than trying to replace them.
- Principled product restraint, publicly argued (the Readwise tag-gated sync post, the "why we limit customization" post). It buys credibility with the PKM audience.
- Free tier is unusually generous on structure — unlimited objects, custom object types, sync, offline, import/export — and gates only AI, tasks, calendar and smart queries.

**Weaknesses**

- Mobile is a second-class citizen and it is the dominant complaint. App Store 3★, 30 Aug 2025: "An exciting but frustratingly fractured experience... While the app boasts access across devices, the functions available on each one are notably inconsistent." 4★, 28 May 2025: "In love with the concept, but mobile app needs some love... The text editing experience on iOS is filled with some pretty glaring and bad bugs." Reviewers report you cannot create object types on iOS.
- No voice capture and no transcription at all. WhatsApp/Telegram voice messages land as untranscribed Audio objects; transcription has been "coming soon" in the docs through Aug 2026.
- Only 4.27★ from 111 iOS ratings — low volume and a mediocre score for a paid PKM app; Play shows 100K+ installs but the rating could not be verified.
- Pricing friction. App Store 1★: "Dishonest Pricing — $199/year? No way. It's still in development. No student discount." In-app prices ($17.99/mo, $199/yr) are far above the web price ($9.99/mo).
- The AI budget model makes users ration the feature. Budget resets monthly, burn varies by model, and the fix is a $8.33/mo add-on or top-ups — friction on exactly the feature that differentiates Pro.
- Stability and onboarding complaints: 1★ "The latest version simply freezes on start, shows the logo and nothing happens"; 1★ "the app summary seemed so promising... but it's so dull, right? No color, no instructions, like an ADD-er's worst nightmare!"; iPad handwriting recognition "just does not recognize or write 90% of the things I write with my pencil"; desktop→mobile sync failures.
- Steep learning curve and up-front modelling burden — you must decide the object type at creation time ("you make one decision: What type of note is it?"), which is the opposite of frictionless capture. Product Hunt reviewers cite the learning curve and limited customization vs Obsidian.
- API churn: the v1 Beta REST API is being switched off on 1 September 2026, so every third-party integration built on it breaks.
- No webhooks, no Zapier, no live two-way sync with Notion/Obsidian.
- Tiny team (~8) with no disclosed funding carrying desktop, web, iOS, Android, API, MCP and an AI assistant — the breadth-vs-depth strain shows in the mobile reviews.
- 816.8 MB iOS binary.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Lift their two-filter triage doctrine straight into our confidence-gate copy, but assign the questions to the right moments: at capture the only implicit question is "do I want to remember this?" (which pressing record already answers), so the Inbox review header should ask their SECOND question verbatim — "Is this useful to you now, or could it be later?" — because that is the question that lets someone delete without guilt.
- Make Inbox review a batched weekly ritual with a default cadence, not a per-capture notification. Their docs say "a weekly review is a strong default" and that funnelling everything to one place exists "to avoid decision fatigue during input." Our confidence gate currently forces a decision per capture; a Delta "weekly review" screen that walks the parked Inbox in one sitting would be a better fit for the same doctrine.
- Copy the closed-vocabulary rule for our understand step: project and contact matching should only propose EXISTING Projects and Contacts by default ("it will not invent a long list of entities you never created"), and creating a new Project or Contact should require a distinct, explicit user gesture. This single rule removes most of the ways an AI-proposed entity graph goes bad.
- Adopt "you are editing the type, not the page": let a user add a property to Project once and have it appear on every existing Project, past and future. Our entities are currently fixed schemas; a user-definable property set per entity type — including a relation property — is what turns our captures/actions/projects/contacts tables into a graph people will keep using.
- Implement contact↔capture and project↔capture as ONE two-way relation with backlink rendering, not as per-screen queries. Capacities gets "a note about a person pulls from every project that person is tagged in" for free out of two-way object-select properties plus a backlink panel. Build the relation primitive once; do not hand-write a query per screen.
- Steal three of their task views wholesale for our actions list: Inbox (no date, no status), Today (due today PLUS overdue), and above all CONTEXT (actions grouped by their linked Project/Person). Context is the view that makes an entity graph feel worth maintaining.
- Split our reminder date into two fields like they do: a deadline ("when something must be finished by") and a scheduled/do date. Ranking deadline-bearing actions above deadline-less ones is a free quality win.
- Do not try to be a task manager. Ship their "task actions" pattern instead: one-gesture export of an action to Todoist / TickTick / Things / Apple Reminders / Google Tasks / Microsoft To Do, carrying due date and tag. It converts a weakness into an integration story.
- Copy the WhatsApp/Telegram bot as a zero-install second capture surface — but beat them where they are weak: they save voice messages as untranscribed audio files with transcription still "coming soon." A Delta bot that accepts a forwarded voice note and replies with the transcript plus the proposed structure is a directly demonstrable advantage.
- Steal the shape of `POST /save-to-daily-note {spaceId, mdText, origin, noTimeStamp}` for our own ingest endpoint. The `origin` field is the detail worth copying: knowing which channel a capture arrived through (mic, watch, WhatsApp, share sheet) is a real input to our confidence gate — a typed share-sheet capture deserves a different threshold than a rambling voice note.
- Do NOT price on an opaque monthly "AI budget." Their model makes users ration the flagship feature and generated a 1★ pricing review. Price on something a user can picture — minutes recorded or captures per month.
- Keep mobile parity as a hard rule. Their single most repeated complaint is that features exist on desktop and not on phone ("the functions available on each one are notably inconsistent", can't create object types on iOS). Mobile IS Delta's product; any feature that ships web-only should be treated as unshipped.
- Reject their capture-time typing decision. "You make one decision: What type of note is it?" is exactly the friction we exist to remove — our understand step should propose the type after the fact. Cite this as the concrete thing our confidence gate replaces.

### TwinMind

<https://twinmind.com/>

**Positioning**

Tagline: "Never forget anything, always know what's next." Positioned as "a twin for your mind" that "captures your day and organizes it into a growing second brain." App Store subtitle: "Second brain for life and work." The organising promise is explicitly an entity graph built without user effort: "By evening, everything you lived gets sorted and filed away: people, places, projects" into "your own private Wikipedia", described in the 2.0 release notes as an "encyclopedia for your life." Target: professionals (50-60% of users), students (25%), personal use (20-25%). Capture philosophy is the exact inverse of ours — ambient, always-on, decision-free at input.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

ThirdEar AI Inc, Menlo Park, California. Founded March 2024. Three co-founders, all AI PhDs who "have worked together since 2019 to build moonshots at Google X": Daniel George (CEO, previously VP and Applied AI Lead at JPMorgan), Sunny Tang (CTO) and Mahi Karim (Chief Scientist); the team also claims work contributing to a Nobel Prize-winning discovery.
Funding: $5.7M seed at a $60M post-money valuation, announced 10 September 2025 (the TechCrunch headline rounds to $6M). Led by Streamlined Ventures with participation from Sequoia Capital. Angels: Stephen Wolfram (who wrote the first check — his first-ever startup investment), Anand Rajaraman, Michael Liou, Dan Roth, Hanhan Xu, Gautam Prakash.
Traction at the raise: 30,000+ users, ~15,000 monthly actives; 11 employees, now ~15+ per the about page. Geographic spread: US, India, Brazil, Philippines, Ethiopia, Kenya, Europe.
App-store evidence now: iOS "TwinMind: AI Notes & Memory" 4.78★ from 856 ratings, v2.0.3 released 31 August 2026 — a full 2.0 rebuild. Google Play 500K+ downloads (Play star rating unverified — the listing truncated on fetch). Product Hunt: #2 of the day on 25 November 2024 for the desktop/sidebar product, mobile launch 3 April 2025, 885 followers, 0 PH reviews. Chrome extension changelog shows steady shipping through May 2026. Definitively alive, well funded and moving fast.

**Platforms**

iOS / iPadOS (App Store id 6504585781, v2.0.3, 136.7 MB, iOS 17+) · Apple Watch (watchOS 10+, complication + Double Tap on Series 9/10/Ultra 2) · Android (Play package ai.twinmind.android, 500K+ installs) · macOS · Windows · Web app · Chrome extension · MCP server (Max tier) · Ear-3 transcription API

**Pricing**

The published prices disagree across their own surfaces, which is itself a finding.
Free $0: unlimited transcription hours, unlimited summaries, unlimited AI chats "with no memory limits", daily digests and recaps, "save unlimited hours of audio", on-device speech recognition, real-time suggestions. Unusually generous — unlimited ASR on the free tier.
Pro: $15/mo on the pricing page, but $14.99/mo and $143.99/yr in the App Store IAP list and the help centre. Adds speaker identification, premium ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini models, phone-call recording, audio file upload and transcription, a 2M-token context window, 24-hour email support.
Max: $40/mo advertised as "40% off" on the pricing page, but $49.99/mo and $359.99/yr in App Store IAPs and the help centre. Adds "5x limits on all features", auto-tag and draft emails, Claude Opus / GPT-5.6-class models, extended-thinking models, "Integrate any app with MCP", priority support and beta access.
Also live as IAPs: "TwinMind MAX Dev" $29.99/mo, $289.00/yr and "TwinMind PRO Dev" $14.99/mo, $149.99/yr.
Enterprise: custom, with custom limits, admin controls, SOC 2 and dedicated support.
Separately, the Ear-3 ASR is sold as an API at $0.23/hour.
Reviews show a history of repricing and of removing features from an existing tier (see weaknesses).

**Capture UX**

This is the strongest part of the product and the sharpest contrast with Delta.
From a locked iPhone: with the Capture widget on the lock screen it is ONE TAP — "Tap it and the capture starts." On iPhone 15 Pro or later the Action Button gives one-press recording without unlocking at all (0 taps, 1 press). Adding the widget in the first place costs about 3 taps (touch and hold → customize → add TwinMind). There is also a Chat widget for querying the second brain without opening the app. No measured seconds-to-recording figure is published (unverified), but the Action Button path is effectively instant.
Apple Watch: open the app and tap "Capture" once; or add a watch-face complication; or on Series 9 / Series 10 / Ultra 2 use the Double Tap gesture to start a capture without touching the screen. The watch then transfers to iPhone with a sync indicator — "Once a capture reaches your phone, TwinMind takes over from there: transcript, summary, and adding it to your twin all happen on the phone." First sync is slower; later ones are automatic in the background.
Mac: hover the floating mic and click "Capture Notes", double-tap Right Option, or use the Home tab button. Press-and-hold Fn to dictate, double-tap Fn for hands-free mode. The app also auto-detects calendar meetings and Zoom/FaceTime joins and asks whether to record.
Ambient duration: marketing says "16+ hours of capture without draining battery" (TechCrunch reports "16 to 17 hours"); the help centre says "optimized for 12 hours of battery life based on our internal testing" — the two disagree. Transcription continues with the screen off. Built in native Swift specifically to get around Apple's background restrictions; the CEO: "We spent about six to seven months last year just perfecting this audio capture."
Max recording length: none. "No fixed time limit; recordings can run indefinitely throughout the day." Uploaded audio files are supported "in any format even up to 24 hours long without needing to split them manually" (Pro).
Offline: "You can continue recording even without an internet connection. Your recording will be saved locally", syncing and generating transcripts/summaries once online; the on-device Ear-2 model runs offline and the app auto-switches down from cloud-only Ear-3.
Other capture surfaces: Chrome extension with a recording sidepanel, live timer, pause/resume, crash recovery for incomplete recordings, and vision AI that reads open tabs (Gmail, Slack, Notion). WhatsApp: "send a new voice note to TwinMind on WhatsApp, or forward an existing one" and it returns a transcript plus a summary and files it into memory.

**Processing and AI**

Owns its ASR, which is the technical moat. Ear-3: 5.26% word error rate, 3.8% diarization error rate, 140+ languages with automatic language detection, marketed as "the first and only model with true global coverage" and "the lowest WER at 5.26%, outperforming the previous best, Eleven Labs, by 12.47%." Sold via API at $0.23/hour. Ear-3 is CLOUD-ONLY; Ear-2 is the on-device/offline model and the app falls back to it automatically without connectivity. Custom vocabulary is supported. Speaker identification is Pro-gated.
Everything above the transcript is third-party LLMs: the privacy policy names OpenAI, Anthropic (Claude), Google (Gemini), Groq, ElevenLabs and Fireworks AI, all contractually barred from training on user data and configured for zero data retention. Pro unlocks premium ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini; Max unlocks Claude Opus / GPT-5.6-class and extended-thinking models.
Outputs per capture: transcript (timestamped, includes local timezone), summary, extracted to-dos, and entity page updates. Product-level outputs: a daily digest with "yesterday's highlights and today's priorities", proactive suggestions, "insights before you ask", a "predict" feature (2.0), auto-tagged email and auto-drafted replies (Max).
AI output is editable: users can "Edit Notes" or "Edit Summary", and "you can even regenerate the summary and TwinMind will create an updated version based on your notes" — i.e. the human edit becomes the input to the regeneration, which is a good pattern.
Audio handling: "Audio recordings processed in real-time; deleted immediately after transcription by default. Stored only if you enable 'Save Audio.'" The CEO is blunt: "TwinMind does not let users access audio recordings later—the audio is deleted on the fly." Storage is cloud by default (AWS/GCP, encrypted); an iOS "Backup Notes Off" mode keeps transcripts local only and disables sync and cloud-dependent features, though "minimal metadata still collected for billing/diagnostics." SOC 2 Type II certified, Google CASA Tier 2.

**Data model**

Documentation lags the marketing badly here — the help centre FAQ describes none of the entity pages — so this is assembled from marketing copy, the App Store listing, the MCP tool surface and TechCrunch. Where it is inference I say so.
CAPTURE / MEMORY is the atomic unit. One capture yields: a transcript (timestamped, local timezone), a summary, extracted to-dos, and optional saved audio (deleted immediately after transcription unless "Save Audio" is on, and not user-retrievable by design). A capture can be bound to a calendar Meeting when auto-detected from Google Calendar or from joining Zoom/Meet/Teams/FaceTime. TechCrunch describes the product as passively capturing ambient speech "to build a personal knowledge graph."
TIMELINE is the real backbone. Help centre: memories are held in "chronological order and weighted by importance", and "your notes and transcripts form a dynamic timeline that the AI references for context", enabling queries like "What did I discuss with ___ last month?" So the primary index is temporal + an importance weight, and the entity pages are DERIVED on top of it rather than being the storage model.
ENTITY PAGES — People, Places, Projects. Homepage: "By evening, everything you lived gets sorted and filed away: people, places, projects" into "your own private Wikipedia"; 2.0 release notes call it an "encyclopedia for your life." App Store: "People and memorable moments get their own auto-updating pages." So PERSON and MOMENT are confirmed at page level; PLACE and PROJECT are named in marketing copy but I found no doc or screenshot enumerating their attributes (attributes unverified). The pages update themselves from new captures; the user neither creates nor curates them.
TO-DO is a genuine first-class entity, not a line in a transcript. The strongest evidence is the MCP surface, which exposes a dedicated `Todo Search` tool returning "tasks with deadlines, priorities, and status" — so a to-do carries at minimum deadline, priority and status, and is queryable independently of the memory it came from.
SUMMARY is separately indexed too: MCP `Summary Search` browses summaries "filtered by keywords, topics, and dates", distinct from the note/task search.
EMAIL is a second ingest stream into the same brain (Max): "All your emails are tagged automatically, the instant they arrive", and drafts are generated from "past emails & conversations captured by the mobile app." CALENDAR EVENTS and email metadata are read through authorised Google integrations and drive capture prompts.
INBOX / UNPROCESSED STATE: NONE. There is no triage queue, no review state, no confidence gate, no "needs attention" flag anywhere in the docs, help centre or MCP surface. The evening auto-sort is the only processing step and it is entirely unattended. This is the single most important structural difference from Delta: TwinMind reached almost our exact entity model (people / places / projects / to-dos / meetings) from ambient firehose input, and consequently has no concept of a capture that has not yet been accepted.
EDITABILITY OF AI-ASSIGNED STRUCTURE: transcripts, notes and summaries are editable and the summary can be regenerated from the edited notes. Whether the auto-generated People/Places/Projects pages can be merged, renamed, corrected, split or deleted — the thing that matters most when entity extraction runs on 16 hours a day of ambient speech — is NOT documented anywhere I could find. Unverified, and the silence is suspicious.
READ/WRITE ASYMMETRY: the MCP server is strictly read-only, with four tools — `Search` ("across your notes and tasks by keyword or topic"), `Summary Search`, `Fetch` ("full memory details including complete transcripts"), `Todo Search`. "All access is strictly read-only; the AI cannot create, edit, or delete content." Auth is per-user browser OAuth, not API keys. There is no documented public write API for third parties; the only public write path is the Ear-3 transcription API, which writes nothing into the graph.
STORAGE MODES: cloud-by-default (AWS/GCP, encrypted) versus iOS "Backup Notes Off" local-only mode which disables sync and cloud features.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval is conversational first and structural second. "Ask your life anything—answers drawn from conversations, email, and the web"; the Mac app has an "Ask me anything" button that queries across all notes and memories "eliminating the need to manually search transcripts"; a Chat widget does the same from the home screen. Under that sits the chronological, importance-weighted timeline the AI references for context, which is what makes "What did I discuss with ___ last month?" work. Structured browsing is via the auto-generated People/Places/Projects pages and "View all memories" on the web app. Proactive resurfacing is the daily digest — "yesterday's highlights and today's priorities" — plus recaps, "insights before you ask" and a "predict" feature in 2.0. Externally, the MCP server exposes four separate read tools (notes+tasks search, summary search, full-memory fetch, todo search) so Claude or ChatGPT can retrieve from the graph directly. Notably there is no documented manual filtering, tagging, folder or saved-view system — you cannot organise it yourself, you can only ask it.

**Outputs and integrations**

Inbound: always-on mobile mic, Apple Watch, Mac app (hotkeys, meeting auto-detect), Windows app, Chrome extension (recording sidepanel plus vision AI reading open tabs incl. Gmail, Slack, Notion), phone-call recording (Pro), audio file upload up to 24h (Pro), WhatsApp voice-note forwarding, Google Calendar, Gmail.
Outbound: MCP server (Max) with read-only tools, connectable to ChatGPT (plugin), Claude (connector) and Claude Code CLI (HTTP transport) — "turn memories into reports, prototypes, and drafts." Email Assistant auto-tags the inbox and drafts replies in the user's voice (Max). Note sharing. Ear-3 transcription API at $0.23/hour as a standalone developer product. Enterprise tier adds admin controls.
Not present: no webhooks, no Zapier, no Notion/Obsidian/Todoist sync, and no write API — nothing can push structure into TwinMind, and TwinMind cannot push tasks out to a task manager.

**Strengths**

- Owns its ASR end to end. Ear-3 at 5.26% WER / 3.8% DER across 140+ languages is a real technical moat, sold separately at $0.23/hour, with an on-device Ear-2 fallback for offline. Nobody else at this size owns the model.
- Capture cost is as close to zero as the platform allows: 1 lock-screen widget tap, or a single Action Button press without unlocking, or a Double Tap on the Watch with no phone in hand.
- Engineering depth in the boring part — "six to seven months" on native Swift audio capture alone to get 16+ hours of background recording past Apple's restrictions.
- A free tier that gives away unlimited transcription hours, unlimited summaries and unlimited AI chat — a genuinely aggressive land-grab, and the reason for 500K+ Play installs.
- Entity pages that maintain themselves: "People and memorable moments get their own auto-updating pages", with zero curation work from the user.
- Real distribution and credibility: 4.78★ from 856 iOS ratings, 500K+ Android installs, $5.7M seed with Sequoia participating and Stephen Wolfram's first-ever startup check.
- Clean read-only MCP surface with four well-chosen tools (notes+tasks, summaries, full memory fetch, to-dos) that makes the memory usable inside Claude and ChatGPT without them building a chat product.
- Defensible privacy posture on paper: audio deleted immediately after transcription by default, an iOS local-only "Backup Notes Off" mode, SOC 2 Type II, Google CASA Tier 2, and zero-retention contracts with every LLM provider.
- The edit-then-regenerate loop is a good pattern — a user edits the notes, and the summary is regenerated FROM the edited notes.

**Weaknesses**

- Repeated repricing and stripping features out of a tier people already paid for. iOS 1★: "They keep on increasing the price and launching more and more expensive tiers, leaving out the earlier adopters in lurch, rated one star." 1★: "The developer has not only imposed strict usage limits on the pro version, but also revoked the permission to use the advanced model in thinking mode for the pro version." 1★: "Unfortunately the app isn't going to be free anymore for pro users unless you were grandfathered in, although it's still being advertised as such."
- Login and onboarding failures blocking first use. 1★: "Can't create an account or login to even try the app." 1★: "Can't even get past the log-in screen even after giving it all my info... I've tried everything including Apple log in." 1★ on forced SSO: "You have to use your google or Apple account to access. Why not create your own account."
- Marketing spam complaint. 1★: "Be cautious. They will collect your info and spam your email… even if you delete the app."
- No inbox, no triage, no review state anywhere. Everything captured becomes permanent memory; nothing is ever marked not-worth-keeping. That is the structural cost of a firehose input model.
- Whether auto-generated People/Places/Projects pages can be merged, corrected, renamed or deleted is undocumented (unverified) — the highest-risk gap when entity extraction runs on 16 hours a day of ambient speech and mishears names.
- Consent liability is pushed wholly onto the user: users must verify recording is legal in their jurisdiction, obtain consent from all participants and give clear notice, and "TwinMind provides tools. We do NOT monitor or verify your compliance." No in-product consent tooling is documented.
- Audio-deleted-by-default means a wrong transcript is unrecoverable — the CEO states audio is "deleted on the fly" and users cannot access recordings later.
- The docs contradict the marketing on their own site: "16+ hours of capture" (homepage) vs "optimized for 12 hours of battery life" (help centre); $15/$40 (pricing page) vs $14.99/$49.99 (help centre and App Store IAPs).
- Documentation lags badly. The help centre FAQ describes no People/Places/Projects pages and no to-do management at all, and the on-site changelog only covers the Chrome extension through May 2026 — the flagship data model is documented only in marketing copy.
- Ear-3 is cloud-only, so going offline silently downgrades quality to Ear-2; whether the user is told is unverified.
- No write API and no outbound task sync — memories and to-dos are trapped inside TwinMind except through read-only MCP.
- Zero Product Hunt reviews and no independent long-form hands-on review I could verify, despite 885 PH followers — the qualitative evidence base is thin relative to the install count.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Their entity model is nearly identical to ours (people, places, projects, to-dos, meetings) and they got there from the opposite capture philosophy — which makes the Inbox our actual differentiator, not the graph. Frame Delta explicitly against them: TwinMind builds an encyclopedia you never agreed to; Delta builds a graph you approved entry by entry. That framing also settles the CLAUDE.md 'brain vs assistant' question in favour of an approved brain.
- Match their capture cost or the press-to-record argument collapses. Concretely: an iOS lock-screen Capture widget that starts recording on ONE tap, plus an Action Button binding for iPhone 15 Pro+ that records without unlocking. If Delta costs four taps and TwinMind costs one press, 'deliberate capture' just reads as 'slow capture'.
- Add a Watch capture target later and copy their architecture exactly: the watch records and hands off, and "transcript, summary, and adding it to your twin all happen on the phone." For us it is cheaper still — the watch only needs to ship audio to Supabase; our understand step already runs server-side.
- Copy their handoff feedback, not just the handoff: an explicit sync indicator and a warning that the first transfer is slower. The number one trust failure in voice capture is not knowing whether the thought was saved; TwinMind pays this attention and it is cheap for us.
- Do NOT copy audio-deleted-by-default. They can afford it because they own Ear-3; we cannot re-run a transcription we no longer have. Retain audio at least until the Inbox item is accepted or rejected, then offer deletion — and make that retention window a stated privacy feature rather than an omission.
- Use their daily digest as the trigger for our Inbox review. "Yesterday's highlights and today's priorities" is the natural, non-nagging moment to surface the captures our confidence gate parked. It converts triage from a chore into a morning briefing, and it fits Capacities' weekly-review doctrine at a daily cadence.
- Build two DERIVED indexes alongside the capture store, because TwinMind proves people query them separately: an actions index carrying deadline + priority + status, and a summaries index searchable by keyword, topic and date. Our API/MCP should expose them as distinct search tools rather than one undifferentiated search over captures.
- Ship a read-only MCP server with roughly their four tools — search captures and actions, search summaries, fetch a full capture including transcript, search actions. Read-only first is the right risk posture, and it makes Delta useful inside Claude and ChatGPT without us building a chat UI at all.
- Accept forwarded WhatsApp voice notes as a zero-install capture channel and reply in-thread with the transcript plus the proposed structure (intent, category, project, people) with an accept link. TwinMind returns transcript+summary; Capacities returns an untranscribed audio file. Returning proposed STRUCTURE in the thread beats both and demos our whole loop in one message.
- Never strip a feature out of a tier we have already sold. Their three worst reviews are all about exactly that. If our AI cost scales with minutes, price on minutes from launch rather than on 'premium models' we might later reclaim.
- Offer email sign-in from day one. Multiple 1-star TwinMind reviews are literally people who could not get past the login screen, and one is specifically about forced Google/Apple-only SSO.
- Build the consent tooling they explicitly refuse to build ("We do NOT monitor or verify your compliance"): a visible recording indicator, an optional spoken disclosure, and a per-capture 'others were present' flag that changes how we store and share it. Press-to-record makes this cheap for us and expensive for them, and it is the strongest wedge in EU markets where Delta will actually be used.
- Make entity-page corrections a first-class flow — merge two Contacts, rename a Project, detach a wrongly-linked capture. TwinMind's silence on whether you can fix an auto-generated Person page is the clearest sign of where an unattended entity graph rots.
- Keep one source of truth for our limits and caps. TwinMind publishes 16 hours on the homepage and 12 in the help centre, and two different prices for the same tier on three surfaces. That inconsistency is what a fast-shipping team looks like when marketing and docs drift; write our caps once and render them everywhere.
