### mymind

<https://mymind.com/>

**Positioning**

Taglines on the marketing site: "mymind is the extension for your mind" and "Remember everything. Organize nothing." The /what page names the audience explicitly: "mymind is for busy people. For those who move too fast to be bothered with folders, labels and systems. For thinkers and doers who could use an extension of their mind." It sells itself to "visual minds of all kinds" (designers, writers, marketers, researchers, developers) and runs a dedicated /adhd landing page whose core promise is the absence of structure: "mymind has no imposing structures or systems that make me feel like I've failed before I even begin. No setup, no folders, no rules." The /why page is an explicit manifesto against filing: "Why do we use folders? Dropdowns? Are these outdated UI patterns still useful or are they just clutter?" and "mymind doesn't interfere, doesn't bother and doesn't ask to be maintained... those who like to procrastinate with folders and unmaintainable systems will find plenty of other apps." The /manifesto page adds the privacy stance: no social features, no collaboration, no vanity metrics, no tracking, no ads, always private. Note: this is a save-and-find-again product, NOT a voice product — there is no audio capture anywhere in it.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Real, actively shipping, and independent. iOS app "mymind – Extend your mind" by mymind, Inc.: version 3.11.0 released 12 June 2026, 4.04 average from 408 US ratings (iTunes lookup API, read Aug 2026), free download with subscription. Android and macOS apps and browser extensions all ship. The app is at major version 3 with a long, dated public trail of feature posts running from Feb 2021 to 2026 (Spaces v1 in March 2023, Advanced AI Summary, PDF analysis, Mastodon/Reddit/Bluesky/GitHub support, new iOS and macOS apps), so it has been in continuous development for 5+ years. Funding: explicitly none — "We're an independent company who's built many other independent products. We're not reliant on any external funding" and "Our service is entirely supported by paying members of the platform, and those are the only people we answer to" (mymind.com/our-promise). Team size is not published; support is a named handful of people (reviewers repeatedly thank "Caedon" by name). Founding date, founder names, headcount, revenue and user numbers: I could not verify any of these this session (the usual attribution to Tobias van Schneider and Roland Ligtenberg is not confirmable from sources I read) — unverified. Third-party footprint is thin: AlternativeTo lists 23 likes and 3 ratings; the Google Play and Chrome Web Store listings blocked automated reads (404 / Google consent redirect) so download and install counts are unverified. Not dead: shipping monthly-ish in 2026 with a top-tier ($299/yr "Newton") still on the roadmap.

**Platforms**

iOS (iPhone/iPad) — App Store id 1520332347 · Android — Google Play package com.mymind.access · macOS app · Web app at access.mymind.com · Chrome / Edge / Brave extension (id nmgcefdhjpjefhgcpocffdlibknajbmj) · Safari extension (Mac App Store id 1532801185)

**Pricing**

Four tiers, USD, billed by mymind directly (Stripe), from access.mymind.com/pricing as read August 2026: (1) The Bookmarker — $4.99/mo, explicitly "no AI features", visual bookmarking only, no card limit. (2) Student of Life — $7.99/mo or $79/yr (~20% annual saving): unlimited cards, intelligent bookmarks, AI image tagging, OCR text-in-image recognition, Smart Spaces, Serendipity. Explicitly EXCLUDES advanced AI, reading mode, article backup, video support, Same Vibe, AI summaries, custom iOS icons, PDF analyzer. (3) Mastermind — $12.99/mo or $129/yr: everything above plus the advanced AI system, AI summaries, reading mode, article backup, video uploads up to 500MB, Same Vibe, PDF analyzer, custom iOS icons. (4) Newton — $299/yr, listed as "not yet available but we are working on it". No free trial is advertised on the pricing page and no free tier is listed; a 2024 App Store reviewer (Tacoz917, v3.3.1) refers to "passing the free 100 limit", implying a 100-card free allowance at some point — unverified against current pricing. No team, family or student plan. Refund policy is discretionary and generous: full refund within 14 days of an unwanted renewal, partial refunds pro-rated on usage, case-by-case via service@mymind.com. The critical structural fact: the AI that the whole product concept depends on is gated to the $12.99/mo top tier.

**Capture UX**

There is no voice capture at all — this is the main contrast with Delta. Capture is share-sheet and click driven. Documented entry points: (a) browser extension one-click save, plus right-click on an image or highlighted text to save it, with an option to add tags or a note at save time; (b) iOS/Android native share panel — mymind's own Android post calls this "the best part about our app... which makes saving websites, articles, images or text highlights perhaps the easiest it's ever been"; (c) Camera Mode, reached by swiping left inside the mobile app, to photograph an object; (d) Quick Note, reached by swiping up from the bottom of the mobile app — the site calls it "like an old-fashioned sticky note in your back pocket" and claims 85% of notes captured are under 140 characters; (e) screenshot then share to mymind, which then OCRs the image; (f) drag-and-drop on desktop. Taps from a LOCKED phone: mymind publishes no tap count and ships no lock-screen widget, no Control Center control, no Action Button support and no Siri intent that I could find in any of its docs or release posts. Realistically it is unlock (Face ID) → find/open app → swipe up for Quick Note = roughly 3 deliberate actions plus app cold-start, or from inside another app: share button → pick mymind → confirm = 2–3 taps. Nothing in the product is designed for capture-before-you-forget under 2 seconds; it is designed for capture-while-browsing. There is no recording, so no max length, no offline queue concept, and nothing to see "while recording". Deliberate anti-feature: there is no import. The /why page defends this — "There's a reason we don't have an import feature. We like the idea of starting from scratch" — and the FAQ confirms mass-import is not supported.

**Processing and AI**

No transcription engine (no audio). The AI work is all post-save enrichment of saved artefacts. Per the AI Usage Policy (last updated 31 Oct 2024): "We use state of the art AI models to analyze your content with multiple providers depending on the context", naming Amazon Bedrock for private content — data sent there is "used only during processing and discarded immediately afterward without logging, retention, or sharing" — and unnamed third-party AI services/proxies for public content, which "may log requests for security and diagnostic reasons" but are not tied to user accounts. Explicit commitment: "Your data is not used for training purposes." Concrete AI features: automatic tagging of images by object, colour and brand; automatic tagging and categorisation of saved URLs ("Now for the first time, mymind adds AI tags to websites you save"); content-type detection — "Your new mind knows if an item is an article, a product, a book or even a recipe", extended over time to movies/TV, books, albums/songs, Wikipedia, Reddit, Mastodon, GitHub, Bluesky and tweets; OCR/text recognition inside images, screenshots and handwritten notes, searchable via `text:` syntax; dominant-colour extraction so you can search by colour; PDF analysis and tagging (Mastermind); Advanced AI Summary — "Your mind will now read the contents of the websites you save and provide a short paragraph summary" (Mastermind only, auto-applied to new saves, manually triggered for old ones); "Same Vibe" visual-similarity retrieval; Serendipity, a random-resurfacing browse mode. A telling wrinkle from the FAQ: "invisible tags" — "Invisible tags (for bookmarks) means we're indexing it based on the domain name and keywords in the title" — i.e. mymind indexes on signals it deliberately does not display. On tag quality the FAQ says smart tagging improves over time, users can report inaccurate tags via a feedback form, and "manual tagging features are planned". Infrastructure: AWS, encrypted at rest including the database, HTTPS in transit, daily multi-location backups (mymind.com/data-security). Languages: not documented; English-only UI.

**Data model**

ONE first-class entity: the Card. Everything saved — note, bookmark, image, article, quote/highlight, product, book, recipe, movie/TV, album/song, tweet, PDF, video, screenshot, Wikipedia/Reddit/Mastodon/GitHub/Bluesky item — is a Card that the AI stamps with a type. There is no separate Note object, no Task, no Person/Contact, no Project, no Meeting. Card attributes evidenced across docs, release posts and the pricing page: auto-assigned type (drives a type-specific visual template — the ADHD page says "Books look like books, quotes look like quotes"); auto-tags, which may be VISIBLE (images, most URLs) or INVISIBLE (bookmarks indexed on domain + title keywords); user-added tags; extracted dominant colours; OCR'd text; an AI summary paragraph (Mastermind); a free-text note field, which can be enlarged in Focus Mode and supports Markdown and triple-backtick code blocks; source URL; created date. NESTING/GROUPING: Spaces, which mymind describes as saved searches. Two kinds. Smart Spaces auto-populate from search criteria and keep updating — "If your space is a Smart Space, you're not manually organizing, updating or curating anything inside your space. It's all magically done for you." Regular Spaces "only show what you add manually. They're like collections in the way you may know them from other apps." Cards can be manually added to or removed from either kind, so a card can sit in several Spaces. Spaces do not nest. The Spaces guide (published as "v1") admits editing a Space after creation, reordering and recolouring were deferred to v2, and a 2024 App Store review (Juan Americo, v3.6.3) still complains "smart spaces lack after-creation editing capability" — i.e. the auto-organising rule is not reliably editable. "Top of Mind" is a deliberately capacity-limited pin area — the post argues "If everything is a priority, nothing is" and cites the 3-priorities rule. There is NO inbox, NO unprocessed/triage state, NO archive, NO folders, NO nesting, and (per the FAQ) NO import. Card-to-card links/backlinks do not exist; a "knowledge graph" that "connects everything automatically or manually" is listed on /what as "coming soon" and I found no release post shipping it — treat as unshipped/unverified. Is AI-assigned structure editable? Partially and asymmetrically: users CAN add their own tags, including in bulk (Shift+click to multi-select cards, then tag or delete together), and three reserved tags — Private, Hidden, NSFW — hide a card from the overview and from search. But nothing in the documentation describes removing or correcting a wrong AI tag; the FAQ's answer is to report it through a feedback form. Retrieval is the only path back to a card: full-text + tag + OCR search with progressive refinement ("Type in a search term, hit ENTER and then type in another one. It's a search within a search"), operators including exclusion (`shoes -red`), `object:car`, `text:car`, `format:pdf`, `#tag`, natural-language dates ("last week", "yesterday"), colour and brand. Sharing: per-card temporary private link toggled inside an expanded card, showing the recipient that single card only, auto-expiring after 24 hours. No Spaces sharing, no collaboration by design. No public API, no webhooks, no documented export.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Search IS the product; there is no filing step to get right. Deep Search stacks terms iteratively (search within a search), and supports exclusion, `object:`, `text:` (OCR), `format:`, `#tag`, natural-language time ranges, colour, brand and card type. The iOS 3.11.0 release (12 June 2026) added card-type filter bubbles under the search bar and keyword auto-complete — and, tellingly for a product whose only retrieval path is search, its headline was "We significantly improved the search performance... you should notice a big improvement in responsiveness and speed", which implies search had been slow for a long time. Tag hopping: every tag on a card is clickable and filters to that tag, so you browse laterally ("jewelry" → "electric blue" → "visual arts"). Smart Spaces act as standing saved queries. Serendipity is a deliberate resurfacing/shuffle mode; Same Vibe (Mastermind) finds visually similar cards. Reading Mode and Focus Mode are distraction-free consumption surfaces, with article backup on Mastermind. Time Travel browses by date. There is no review queue, no spaced repetition, and no chat-with-your-notes feature documented anywhere I read.

**Outputs and integrations**

Deliberately almost none — this is the opposite pole from Cleft. Inputs: browser extensions (Chrome/Edge/Brave share one build, id nmgcefdhjpjefhgcpocffdlibknajbmj; a separate Safari extension ships as a Mac App Store app, id 1532801185), iOS/Android native share panel, mobile Camera Mode, drag-and-drop on desktop. Outputs: a per-card temporary private link that expires in 24 hours. No public API, no webhooks, no Zapier, no Notion/Obsidian/Todoist sync, no calendar, no email-in, and no documented bulk export — none of which appears anywhere in the site, FAQ, pricing page or release posts. No import either, as a stated principle. Sign-in is Google or Apple only ("Both Google and Apple sign-up provide a simple, secure and 2-factor enabled way"); Apple's hide-my-email is supported. Supported file types: GIF/JPG/PNG/HEIC images, PDFs, and video up to 500MB for Mastermind members.

**Strengths**

- The contrarian bet is coherent all the way down: no folders, no inbox, no triage, no import, no collaboration. Every page of the site defends the same idea, which is why it reads as a product rather than a feature list.
- Retrieval is genuinely deep for a consumer app: OCR inside screenshots and handwriting, colour search, brand and object recognition, `object:`/`text:`/`format:` operators, natural-language dates, and iterative search-within-a-search.
- Type-aware rendering does real cognitive work — a book card looks like a book, a quote like a quote — so visual recognition substitutes for the folder you never made.
- Smart Spaces are the right compromise: a saved query gives you the feeling of a folder without the maintenance cost of one, and cards can still be added or removed by hand.
- Privacy is a genuine differentiator and is specified, not just asserted: Bedrock for private content with no retention, no training on user data, Google/Apple-only auth, 24-hour expiring share links, encrypted at rest.
- Business model integrity — self-funded, member-supported, discretionary refunds — which for a memory tool people expect to keep for a decade is itself a feature.
- The ADHD positioning is not a bolt-on: it's an entire landing page arguing that the absence of structure is the accommodation, and it recurs unprompted in real reviews ("if you have adhd this is what its like to NOT have adhd", App Store, v3.8.4).

**Weaknesses**

- Price is the single most repeated complaint in App Store reviews, and it hits the AI tier specifically: "Good design, crazy expensive!" (2★, v3.7.0), "Cool app, high price... the price is way too high for what it is" (3★, v3.4.1), "Not worth having a monthly subscription... at the price point they want monthly I'll just use my free notes app" (2★, v3.10.0), "Overhyped & Underwhelming. Functionality is basic, not worth the price" (1★, v3.6.2). The $12.99/mo AI tier is the product; the $4.99 tier explicitly has no AI.
- No correction ritual for a wrong AI tag. Users can ADD tags but nothing documents removing or fixing a bad auto-tag; the FAQ's remedy is a feedback form and "manual tagging features are planned". When the only retrieval path is search over AI-assigned metadata, a silent mistag is an item you never see again.
- Smart Spaces — the auto-organising centrepiece — could not be edited after creation for years. Shipped as "v1" in March 2023 with editing deferred to "v2"; still complained about in 2024 ("smart spaces lack after-creation editing capability", 2★ v3.6.3), and a reviewer on v3.6.0 could not create Spaces at all ("circle creation produces errors").
- Search — the product's whole value — was slow enough that its speed fix was the headline of the June 2026 release. That is a five-year-old product admitting its core loop was underperforming.
- Recurring iOS layout bugs across multiple versions: "the iOS app has a bug where the left side menu hangs over the interface" (3★ v3.4.1), repeated by another reviewer on the same version, plus "Lovely concept but very-buggy" waterfall-layout glitches (2★ v3.9.1) and signup timeouts ("app keeps timing out and won't let me sign up", 1★ v3.8.5).
- No import, by policy. Users arriving with a decade of bookmarks must start from zero — defensible philosophically, a hard adoption tax in practice.
- No export, no API, no webhooks, no integrations. Data goes in and only comes back out through the UI or a 24-hour share link — a serious lock-in risk for a tool that promises to hold your memory "forever".
- No task, project, person or action entity at all, so anything actionable you save dies in the collection. Multiple reviewers ask for adjacent features (card reordering, multi-select, duplicate detection, Word/Excel, family sharing) that the no-structure philosophy makes awkward to grant.
- No family/team plan — cited directly as the blocker to subscribing ("Primary barrier to subscription is lack of family sharing support", 4★ v3.8.5).
- Third-party validation is thin for a five-year-old paid product: 408 iOS ratings at 4.04, 3 ratings on AlternativeTo, and no independent hands-on review I could retrieve this session.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Change Delta's success metric from filing accuracy to retrieval latency. mymind's own testimonial wall is dominated by finding, not organising ("Finding a specific memory is a search away", "Add it to mymind and you will find it again – forever"), and its June 2026 headline feature was making search fast. Instrument Delta for 'seconds to re-find a capture older than 30 days' and treat the confidence gate's accuracy as a secondary metric that only matters insofar as it moves that number.
- Steal invisible tags outright, and use them to make the confidence gate cheap. When Delta's understand step proposes a Project or Person below the auto-file threshold, do NOT show it — but DO write it into the search index as a hidden signal. The capture still parks in the Inbox, the screen stays honest, and searching the project name still surfaces it. This converts low-confidence AI from a liability into free recall.
- Make every auto-filing decision reversible in one tap from the note itself, because mymind demonstrates the failure mode of not doing it. Delta should render the AI's decisions as editable chips on the capture — intent, category, project, people — each with a one-tap 'wrong' that re-files immediately and logs a correction signal. mymind's answer to a wrong tag is a feedback form; that is the gap our Inbox exists to close, so make the correction live on the note, not in a settings screen.
- Model a Delta Project as a saved query PLUS an explicit member set, exactly like Smart Spaces plus regular Spaces — and ship rule-editing on day one. mymind shipped Spaces v1 without it and was still eating complaints 18 months later. Concretely: project = {name, match rules (keywords, contacts, date range), explicitly-added capture ids, explicitly-removed capture ids}, where explicit membership always wins over the rule. That also makes 'retain notes explicitly added to projects' (our existing fix) a first-class part of the model rather than a patch.
- Copy Top of Mind with a hard cap of 3. Cheap to build on our existing captures table (a boolean plus a server-side limit), and it gives Delta a 'what am I actually on' surface that is not another list. The cap is the feature: 'if everything is a priority, nothing is'.
- Do NOT copy mymind's no-import principle. It is its most-cited adoption tax. Delta should import Apple Voice Memos and existing text notes on first run — an import that back-fills the Inbox is also the fastest way to prove the understand step works before the user has spoken a word.
- Ship export before we ship more entity types. mymind has no export, no API, and no webhooks, and it is the strongest lock-in criticism you can level at a memory product. Delta's Supabase backing makes a JSON + Markdown export nearly free; do it while the schema is still small.
- Type-aware rendering is worth the build cost. mymind's cards look different per type and users cite that as why they can find things visually. Delta already detects intent — render a task capture, a meeting capture and a research capture as visually distinct cards rather than seven variants of a grey note row.
- Price ceiling check: mymind charges $12.99/mo for an AI tier that only saves and finds, and price is its top complaint theme. Delta captures, understands, routes and researches — but that only justifies a higher price if the routing actually reaches the user's other tools. Until Delta has outbound integrations, treat ~$8–10/mo as the ceiling.

---

### Cleft (Cleft Notes)

<https://cleftnotes.com/>

**Positioning**

"Talk Naturally, Get Structured Notes" / "Talk it out. Get a structured note." The App Store name is literally the segment: "Cleft - For Verbal Thinkers", with the promo line "Speak. Capture. Clarity." and the description opening "You're a verbal thinker. Cleft captures thoughts before they vanish. The way you think. Out loud. On device, even offline." The site quotes David Pierce (The Verge): "An AI voice notes app that really works" — I could not independently retrieve that Verge piece this session, so treat the attribution as the vendor's claim, unverified. The beachhead is named out loud rather than implied: the manifesto says "Cleft exists for verbal thinkers, neurodivergent minds, and anyone whose best ideas arrive faster than typing can keep up", and the team designs "for ADHD, accessibility, messy speech, and low-energy days first" on the principle that "When you design for edge cases first, everybody benefits". The docs README brands it "Built by the disabled community, for the disabled community." Crucially, the manifesto also settles the front-door question that Delta keeps deferring: Cleft refuses to become "another everything app" and states "Cleft should feed the tools people already use, from Notion and Obsidian to meetings and Shortcuts."

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Small, real, bootstrapped, and shipping fast. App Store: "Cleft - For Verbal Thinkers", seller Cleft AI Limited, track id 6479458038, version 1.14.3 released 3 July 2026, 4.66★ from 68 US ratings and 4.5★ from 16 UK ratings (iTunes lookup API, read Aug 2026), free with IAP, 283MB, minimum iOS 17.0, English-only listing, categories Utilities + Productivity. Company: co-founders Justin Mitchell and Jonathan Cosgrove, built in Ireland, "Bootstrapped and customer-funded" with the stated goal "to build a sustainable business, not chase hypergrowth or an exit", serving "10,000+ people" (vendor's own figure, unverified externally). Both founders identify as neurodivergent and describe a sibling product, Amber, for disabled people. Release cadence over the last 8 months: 1.10.2 (6 Jan 2026), 1.10.3 (7 Jan), 1.11 (3 Feb), 1.12 (6 Mar), 1.12.1 (16 Mar), 1.12.2 (21 Mar), 1.12.3 (22 Mar), 1.13.0 (4 May), two service-side reliability pushes (11 and 17 June), 1.14.0 (22 June), 1.14.1 (23 June), 1.14.2 (1 July), 1.14.3 (3 July) — roughly fortnightly, with a public roadmap showing 26 shipped, 8 active and 23 planned items and visible upvote counts. The App Store description claims a Fast Company "25 Best New Apps" placement and the site quotes David Pierce of The Verge; neither could be independently retrieved this session — unverified. Definitively not dead; the interesting signal is the titles of the last five releases: "More Reliable Capture", "Clearer Writing, Calmer Controls", "Reliable Controls", "Rock-Solid Reliability", "Built to Last" — a team spending its releases on dependability, not model quality.

**Platforms**

iPhone (iOS 17.0+) · iPad (split view, Stage Manager) · Mac (native AppKit/SwiftUI app, not Catalyst) · Apple Watch (standalone recording; complications; Ultra Action Button) · CarPlay (since v1.13.0, May 2026) · No Android (roadmap item, 54 upvotes) and no web app

**Pricing**

Two tiers, App Store billing only (Stripe + RevenueCat on the backend). BASIC — free, no trial gate: voice-to-text notes up to 5 minutes per recording, on-device transcription, structured notes, the 3 built-in output styles plus a 50-character standing rule, tags, search, Spotlight, export, widgets, Siri & Shortcuts, Apple Notes integration, Local Sync into a fixed 'Cleft' folder, 7-day support response. CLEFT PLUS — $6.99/month or $39.99/year (the pricing page frames the annual as "saves 52%"; the $39.99 works out at $3.33/mo): 30-minute recordings, 500-character standing rules plus 2 custom named styles, append-by-voice to an existing note, image/file attachments, custom copy formats, remove the Cleft signature, password-protected share links, Notion sync (including auto-send), Local Sync to arbitrary folders for Obsidian/NotePlan/Logseq, Zapier, priority support, TestFlight preview access, and Apple Family Sharing for up to 5 people on the ANNUAL plan only ("Monthly Plus is for one Apple ID"). Regional pricing follows App Store rates. Downgrading keeps your existing notes accessible. The brief's claim of a 56% annual price cut is plausible but I could not verify a historical price change from any source I read — unverified.

**Capture UX**

This is the strongest part of the product and the direct benchmark for Delta. FROM A LOCKED IPHONE IT IS ONE ACTION: the Lock Screen widget — "Tap the widget to start a recording. The Live Activity takes over from there with a pause and resume button you can reach from the Dynamic Island" — so 1 tap, no unlock, call it ~1–2 seconds; or on iPhone 15 Pro and later, the Action Button: "Press the Action Button once to start recording. Press it again to stop" — 1 physical press, screen state irrelevant. Other entry points, all documented: Control Center tile (iOS 18+) — pull down, tap "Cleft Record" (2 actions); Siri — "Hey Siri, new Cleft note" (0 taps); three home-screen widgets — New Note, Continue Last, Pinned Note (1 tap each, unlocked); in-app + button, or ⌘N / global ⌥⌘N on Mac; CarPlay dashboard recording (iOS 26.4+, added in v1.13.0 May 2026, with a steering-wheel mark on the resulting note and cancel support); Apple Watch — complication tap, or Digital Crown → app grid, or Ultra Action Button, then tap record with a haptic confirming it is running. The Watch records fully standalone: "You can record on the watch even when your iPhone is out of range. The audio queues locally and transfers the next time the phone is nearby" — but the watch only captures; transcription happens on the iPhone once synced. WHILE RECORDING: elapsed time in the Dynamic Island (added v1.13.0), a Live Activity on the lock screen with pause/resume, a lock-screen timer (v1.10.2), haptics throughout, a restart button that "discards the current recording and starts fresh without leaving the recording screen", pause/resume, and the ability to pick the output style before or during recording (v1.14.0). Recording survives audio-device changes mid-session — AirPods to USB mic to Bluetooth (v1.12.2). MAX LENGTH: 5 minutes free, 30 minutes Plus; "extended recording sessions" is still only a planned roadmap item, and a paying reviewer asks for longer (Kwottley, v1.6). SPEED AFTER STOP: v1.11 (Feb 2026, "Liquid Fast Edition") cut time-to-note from ~20 seconds to ~3 seconds; getting-started says "Tap stop. Your note is ready almost immediately." OFFLINE: you can record and read your last 50 cached notes offline, and Preflight Mode pre-downloads the library, but "Sharing, syncing, and other online features are unavailable until you reconnect" and cloud AI formatting needs the network — so the App Store's "Offline-First" lead is ahead of the shipped product; "offline-first for Cleft 2.0" is still listed as Active (in progress) on the public roadmap.

**Processing and AI**

Two-stage split, and the split is the privacy story. STAGE 1, on device: "When you stop recording, Cleft transcribes your audio locally on your device using the Whisper model", with live progress shown. "All transcription happens on your device using OpenAI's Whisper model" and "No audio leaves your device" for transcription. STAGE 2, cloud, opt-in: "Only the transcribed text is sent to AI for formatting" — OpenAI is the primary provider, Groq the failover; Anthropic appears on the vendor list but in the no-personal-data-access group (internal dev use). No training on user content, bound by DPAs. AUDIO RETENTION: the m4a stays on device ~1 hour and then moves to Cleft's servers; notes are encrypted at rest; all data is kept 2 years after last login unless deleted earlier on request; users can download the original audio at any time. OUTPUT STYLES — three built in for everyone: Structured ("Clean summaries with headings, bullets, and action items"), Structured Prose ("Flowing prose without bullet-heavy formatting"), Clean Transcript ("Keeping your words close to the original transcript"). Plus adds "Your Rules", a single 500-character standing instruction applied across all styles (50 characters on free) with examples like "No adverbs" or "Preserve my questions exactly as spoken", and up to 2 named custom styles with their own instructions (e.g. "Launch Brief", "Code Review"). Auto-titling is generated with a visible progress indicator (v1.12.1). LANGUAGES: 60+ supported, 57 benchmarked below 50% word error rate, auto-detect or a fixed language in Settings; anything outside the list "may still return results, but you might see garbled output or mixed-language text"; US English is best. A 1★ review complains it "can't transcribe Chinese". Notable: there is NO AI tagging and NO AI intent classification — auto-tagging is still an unshipped roadmap item with 45 upvotes. Cleft deliberately does structure-within-the-note and leaves filing to the human.

**Data model**

ONE first-class entity: the Note. Its exact shape is not guesswork — the Local Sync integration publishes the schema as YAML front matter, quoted verbatim in the docs: `cleft_id: 12431` ("Stable Cleft note ID. Namespaced under `cleft_` so it can't collide with `id:` fields written by other tools"), `title` (YAML-quoted for safe round-tripping), `tags: ["work", "ideas"]` (flow sequence), `created` and `updated` (UTC ISO 8601), `source: cleft`, `transcript` ("full transcript on one line (newlines collapsed to spaces)"), then the body = the AI-generated summary, then the tags repeated as inline hashtags. Filenames are `2026-05-06-meeting-with-jonny-12431.md` — UTC date + a kebab-case slug capped at 60 chars + the immutable id — and the original `.m4a` optionally exports beside it, "the recording as it was made. No perceptual filters touch the file." So a Note = {id, title, tags[≤3], created, updated, transcript, summary body, audio, attachments, style, archived flag, pinned-to-widget flag, share link}. NESTING: none. There are no folders, no notebooks, no projects, no nesting of any kind. Grouping is tags only, capped at three per note, USER-CREATED (open a note, tap Add Tag or ⌘L, type or pick a suggestion), with bulk add/remove across a multi-selection. Filtering is a small fixed set of views — "All. Every note in your library", Archived, and Shared Links — plus tap-a-tag-to-filter. INBOX/UNPROCESSED STATE: there is no triage inbox, and that is the point — every note lands finished. But there IS a failure/processing state machine, which is the more interesting borrow: v1.12.3 shipped "Failed notes show clear reason with retry", v1.14.3 made "Unfinished notes clearer to retry", background uploads auto-recover, and interrupted uploads protect the audio file. IS AI OUTPUT EDITABLE? Fully, and bidirectionally — this is the correction ritual mymind lacks. Title: "Tap the title at the top to change it." Body: full Markdown editing with bold/italic/strikethrough/headings/lists/quotes/code/links. Transcript: toggle to raw text and fix names or jargon, then choose "Transcript only" or "Transcript and summary"; edit the transcript and Cleft "will offer to rewrite your summary to match". Rewrite: long-press (iOS) or right-click (Mac) → Rewrite regenerates the summary from the transcript in a different style. Note that regeneration works from the EDITED TRANSCRIPT, not by re-running the audio. Other note actions: Continue (append more audio to an existing note), Pin to Widget, Duplicate, Merge (combine two selected notes into one), Archive/Unarchive, Delete, plus bulk delete/archive/tag. Attachments (Plus): images (JPEG/PNG/GIF/HEIC/WEBP/SVG), video (MP4/WebM/MOV) and documents (PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT/MD/XLS/XLSX/PPT/PPTX/ZIP/GZ/TAR/PATCH) — no OCR or AI analysis of attachments is documented. HOW A NOTE BECOMES A TASK — deliberately outside the app: Cleft has no Task entity. Markdown checkboxes (`- [ ] Task 1`) written into the note body are extracted INDIVIDUALLY by Zapier into 13 destinations (Todoist, Microsoft To Do, Google Tasks, TickTick, Things, Jira, Asana, ClickUp, Trello, GitHub, Notion, Reflect, WorkFlowy), and the trigger is the user applying the literal tag "tasks" — the docs call this "a human review checkpoint before automation runs". Field mapping: checkbox → task title, with title/body/tags/timestamps/published link available and optional custom mapping to priority, due date, assignee, category. HOW A NOTE LINKS TO A PERSON OR PROJECT: it doesn't. No Person, Contact, Project, Meeting, backlink or graph entity exists anywhere in Cleft. A "project" is a tag; a "person" is a word in the transcript. Sharing: a web link any recipient can open without an account, optionally password-protected (Plus); recipients can leave comments, which email the owner and can fire Zapier (e.g. to Slack); links are removed only by deleting them, no expiry documented.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Deliberately thin, and the thinness is a positioning choice. In-app search is title-only — "Pull down on your notes list (iPhone/iPad) or press ⌘/ (Mac) to search by note title". The real search is Apple's: Spotlight indexes "across titles, transcripts, summaries, and tags" (iOS 18+/macOS 15+), works offline, and since v1.12 also finds tags. So Cleft outsources retrieval to the OS rather than building semantic search. Filters: All / Archived / Shared Links, plus tap-a-tag. Sorting options, saved searches, semantic or vector search, chat-with-your-notes, and any review or resurfacing flow are all absent — there is no 'surface an old note' mechanic at all. Mac keyboard navigation is good (⌘1–9 to jump to notes, ⌘1–5 for tabs, ⌘L to tag, ⌘/ to search, ⌥⌘N to record). Auto-tagging remains the second-most-upvoted roadmap request (45 upvotes) and is unshipped.

**Outputs and integrations**

This is the strategy: be a front door, not a destination. Local Sync writes Markdown + YAML front matter (schema above) plus optional .m4a into a folder — free into a fixed 'Cleft' folder, Plus into any folder, which is how Obsidian, NotePlan and Logseq are supported without building three integrations. Notion: native OAuth sync (Plus), sending title + transcript + AI summary into a database you pick, with auto-send available; one-way only — "Edits you make in Notion never come back to Cleft" — and appending audio creates a new page rather than updating the old one. Zapier (Plus): triggers on new notes, note updates, tag changes, comments and link changes, reaching 6,000+ apps, with a documented template library for Asana, ClickUp, GitHub, Google Tasks, Jira, Microsoft To Do, Notion, Reflect, Things, TickTick, Todoist, Trello and WorkFlowy. Apple Shortcuts: 10+ actions plus a published gallery of recipes — voice note to Apple Notes inbox, to Drafts inbox, to Craft inbox, to a daily Reflect note, Markdown-to-rich-text conversion, email a note, send a note to Perplexity or ChatGPT, advanced search, launch-and-record, custom-instruction recording. Apple Reminders, Bear, Drafts, Ulysses, iA Writer, Capacities and Things are reachable through Shortcuts/share sheet rather than native integrations. Share sheet to Mail, Messages, Slack, AirDrop. Shareable web links with comments. Original M4A download from any note. There is NO public REST API and no webhooks yet — webhooks are listed as Active on the roadmap, alongside expanded export destinations (Claude, Drafts, Bear, email), local file backup and upgraded Shortcuts. Vendors, published in full: AWS (hosting + SES), Apple, Cloudflare, OpenAI (primary AI), Groq (backup AI), Stripe, RevenueCat, Revolut Business, HubSpot, Mailerlite, Google Workspace, Metabase, 1Password, Sentry, Nango (OAuth token management for Notion), Fillout, Zapier, Namecheap; plus a no-personal-data group including Anthropic, GitHub, Figma, Mintlify, Fathom Analytics and TelemetryDeck.

**Strengths**

- One-press capture from a locked phone via Lock Screen widget or Action Button, with a Live Activity and Dynamic Island timer taking over. Nothing in the category beats it, and it is the whole product.
- The audience is named in the App Store title itself — "For Verbal Thinkers" — and it converts: ADHD, Parkinson's, medical-appointment and note-taking-anxiety use cases all show up unprompted in 5★ reviews.
- Genuine on-device Whisper transcription with a clean, publishable privacy split: audio never leaves the device for transcription, only text goes to OpenAI/Groq, no training, full vendor list published.
- Every AI output is editable and re-runnable: fix the transcript and it offers to rewrite the summary; Rewrite regenerates in another style; styles can be picked before recording, on the Watch, or in CarPlay.
- The Local Sync front-matter contract is a genuinely good interop design — a namespaced stable id, quoted title, ISO timestamps, transcript on one line, summary as body, unmodified .m4a beside it — and it buys Obsidian, NotePlan and Logseq support for the price of one feature.
- Explicit strategic clarity: the manifesto refuses to be "another everything app" and commits to feeding Notion, Obsidian, meetings and Shortcuts. That decision is visible in every integration choice.
- Reliability discipline: the last five releases are titled after dependability, recording survives mid-session audio-device swaps, failed notes carry a reason and a retry, uploads auto-recover, and time-to-note dropped from ~20s to ~3s in Feb 2026.
- Apple-platform breadth for a two-founder team: iPhone, iPad, native Mac, standalone Watch capture that queues offline, CarPlay, Spotlight, Siri, Shortcuts, Control Center.
- Aggressive, legible pricing — $39.99/yr with a genuinely usable free tier (5-minute recordings, on-device transcription, tags, search, export, Local Sync).

**Weaknesses**

- Reliability is still the top complaint despite the reliability releases: 1★ "Glitchy to the point of being unusable" (v1.8) — six recording attempts, two succeeded, the rest errored or showed gibberish with no way to recover the audio; 2★ "Was excellent at first" (v1.8) — declining transcription quality and crashes without notification, losing recordings; 2★ "Frustrated with Bugs" (v1.11) from a paying subscriber — unwanted auto-recording, white-screen crashes, sync failures, patch notes that would not dismiss.
- Speed complaints: 2★ "Super slow transcription" (v1.9), recommending Apple's own Voice Memos instead. On-device Whisper is a privacy win and a latency cost on older hardware.
- Billing/entitlement failure: 1★ "No Way To Cancel Subscription" (v1.8) — paid but stayed on the Free plan for three days with no upgrade activation.
- "Offline-First" leads the App Store listing but the docs say only your last 50 notes are cached, sharing and syncing are unavailable offline, and cloud AI formatting needs the network — while "offline-first for Cleft 2.0" is still an in-progress roadmap item. The marketing runs ahead of the build.
- No auto-tagging at all. It is the second-most-upvoted roadmap request (45 upvotes) and remains unshipped, so all organisation is manual — and capped at 3 tags per note with no folders, projects or nesting.
- In-app search is title-only; real retrieval is delegated to Apple Spotlight, which means no semantic search, no chat-with-your-notes, no saved searches, and nothing at all on non-Apple platforms.
- No Android (54 upvotes), no web app, English-only listing, and a 1★ for "can't transcribe Chinese" — a hard ceiling on TAM for a two-person team.
- Notion sync is one-way and creates a new page per appended recording, so a note edited downstream diverges permanently. No public API and no webhooks yet.
- Recording caps of 5 minutes free / 30 minutes Plus, with longer sessions only 'planned'; a paying reviewer (v1.6) asks for more.
- Only 68 US + 16 UK ratings and a self-reported "10,000+ people" — real, but small, and a single founder departure would put the product at risk.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Decide the Expo native question now, because Cleft's entire advantage is one press from a locked phone. A Lock Screen widget, an Action Button intent, a Live Activity and a Control Center control cannot be built in JS — they need a WidgetKit/AppIntents extension via a config plugin and a dev client. If Delta's pitch is 'zero typing friction' and our fastest path is unlock → open app → tap record (3+ actions), we lose the pitch to a two-person team. Either commit to the native extension or stop claiming friction as the differentiator.
- Give Delta a first-class capture state machine with a visible, recoverable failure state, and treat it as more important than the confidence gate. Cleft has no inbox but it does have 'unfinished' and 'failed with a reason and a retry', and its 1★ reviews are precisely the cases where that failed and audio was lost. Delta's states: recorded → transcribed → understood → filed | needs-review | failed. Rule: the raw audio and the raw transcript must survive every failure of the understand step and be replayable from the note. Our capture is worthless if a Supabase Edge Function timeout eats it.
- Make editing the transcript re-trigger the understand step, exactly as Cleft does. This is the correction ritual mymind lacks and it is nearly free for us because understand is already an idempotent function of transcript text, not audio. Fix a misheard project name in the transcript → Delta offers to re-derive intent, category, project and people. That single behaviour makes a wrong auto-file recoverable without a settings screen.
- Ship a 'Re-understand as…' action on every capture, the equivalent of Cleft's Rewrite. Let the user re-run the understand step with a chosen lens — as a task list, as a meeting brief, as a research question, as a raw transcript — instead of being stuck with the first classification. Combined with the point above, this makes our confidence gate reversible rather than final.
- Adopt the 'tasks' tag pattern as our gate on SIDE EFFECTS, not on filing. Cleft's cheapest, smartest design is that automation only fires when a human applies a tag — 'a human review checkpoint before automation runs'. Delta should let high-confidence captures file themselves silently, but require an explicit confirmation before anything leaves the app: creating a calendar event, sending a message, kicking off a research job. Filing is reversible; a sent message is not.
- Steal the Local Sync front-matter contract verbatim as Delta's export format, with our own namespaced id (`delta_id`), title, tags, created/updated, source, transcript-on-one-line, AI summary as body, and the .m4a beside it. It costs one feature and makes Delta a front door for every Obsidian/Logseq/NotePlan user without building three integrations — and namespacing the id prevents collisions the way Cleft explicitly designed for.
- Extract action items as individual markdown checkboxes in the note body, not only as rows in our actions table. Cleft's Zapier layer extracts each `- [ ] item` into its own downstream task across 13 apps. If Delta's actions are only rows in Supabase, they are unreachable; if they are also checkboxes in an exportable body, every task manager on earth becomes an integration for free.
- Answer Cleft's front-door question explicitly in CLAUDE.md rather than deferring it again. Cleft's manifesto picks 'feed the tools people already use'. Delta currently has captures, actions, projects, contacts, meetings and research results — that is a destination. If we stay a destination, retrieval has to beat Notion and Obsidian search, which means semantic search over transcripts is a P0, not a nice-to-have. If we feed them, freeze the entity list and ship export first.
- Do not delegate retrieval to Spotlight the way Cleft does — we are cross-platform (Expo + web on Vercel), so we get no free OS index. Cleft can afford title-only in-app search because iOS carries it; Delta cannot. Budget for real full-text and semantic search over transcript + summary + entity names from the start.
- Copy Cleft's honest note-lifecycle actions into our note UI: Continue (append more audio to an existing capture), Merge two captures, Duplicate, Archive, Pin to widget. 'Continue' in particular fits our raw-thought loop — a thought that arrives in three bursts should be one capture, not three Inbox items to triage.
- Match the free tier or lose on first impression: Cleft gives away 5-minute recordings with on-device transcription, tags, search, export and Local Sync for $0. Delta's free tier must at minimum be capture + transcribe + export, or it reads as strictly worse than a competitor at $39.99/yr.
- Be precise about offline instead of claiming 'offline-first'. Cleft leads its App Store listing with 'Offline-First' while its docs admit only 50 cached notes and no cloud formatting offline. Delta's honest, shippable promise is narrower and better: 'recording never fails and audio is never lost — it queues and processes when you're back online'. Build the local queue first, then say exactly that.

### flomo (浮墨笔记)

<https://flomoapp.com/>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

A deliberately anti-PKM "card note" tool for continuous, frictionless capture. Homepage tagline: "持续不断记录，意义自然浮现" (keep recording continuously, meaning emerges by itself), and the product is glossed as "flomo = flow + memo". The capture promise is explicit on the homepage: "无需格式、无需排版、无需分类" — no formatting, no layout, NO CATEGORISATION. The company publishes a "我们不擅长" (what we are bad at) section and states "拒绝 All in one", recommending Notion for long-form and Cubox for read-later. The CN App Store listing name as of v5.5.0 is literally "flomo浮墨笔记-AI语音备忘|卡片便签|日记回顾与洞察" — AI voice memo is now the FIRST feature in the store name, ahead of cards and review. The philosophy page /thinking/ai-era.html states the thesis for why a capture tool survives LLMs: "恰恰因为 AI 什么都懂，你的记录才第一次变得不可替代" (precisely because AI knows everything, your own records became irreplaceable for the first time), and refuses AI text generation because "你喂 AI 什么，它就还你什么" — feeding AI polished, sanitised notes produces "假数据" (fake data).

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Launched 2020-04-18 per https://help.flomoapp.com/about-us (first-party claim of "over a million" users — treat as marketing, unaudited). iOS app original release 2021-03-04 (iTunes lookup id 1552314395). Seller: Shanghai Xiandi Network Technology Co., Ltd. No public funding information found in the pages I fetched (unverified — I could not verify any raise, valuation or headcount). CN App Store, verified via the iTunes lookup API on 2026-08-31: averageUserRating 4.93, userRatingCount 51,993, version 5.5.0, currentVersionReleaseDate 2026-08-30 (i.e. shipped the day before this research), size 149.4 MB, minimum iOS 15.0, languages EN/ZH-Hans/ZH-Hant. The store page https://apps.apple.com/cn/app/id1552314395 renders this as "4.9 / 52,000 ratings". It carries "App Store 编辑推荐" (Apple editorial feature) and is the recommended app of the books 《笔记的方法》 and 《卡片笔记写作法》 — the team wrote 《笔记的方法》 themselves, i.e. they sell the methodology that sells the tool. The v5.5.0 release note is the Agent going native. flomo has also published a seven-year commitment to users (per /about-us). Six years old, still shipping weekly, 4.93 across ~52k ratings is genuinely exceptional — for calibration, the US App Store rating for Notta is 4.05 across 1,398 (iTunes lookup id 1480649572, US).

**Platforms**

iOS (iPhone + iPad, native, min iOS 15.0) · Android · HarmonyOS 鸿蒙 · macOS desktop client · Windows desktop client · Web + PWA · WeChat official account (capture channel) · Browser extension · MCP server for external AI clients

**Pricing**

Verified from the CN App Store IAP list on https://apps.apple.com/cn/app/id1552314395 : flomo Pro Monthly ¥15.00; flomo Pro Annual ¥99.00; flomo Max Monthly ¥39.00; flomo Max Annual ¥299.00; education-discount variants at ¥99.00 (Pro annual) and ¥249.00 (Max annual). App itself is free (price 0.00, iTunes lookup id 1552314395). Roughly EUR 12/yr for Pro and EUR 36/yr for Max at current rates (conversion is my arithmetic, not a quoted figure). https://help.flomoapp.com/membership/pro.html deliberately withholds prices: "最新价格与优惠请以 App 内展示为准". FAQ (https://help.flomoapp.com/faq.html) states there is NO lifetime/one-off purchase: "采用订阅制，是为了持续覆盖云服务的存储和维护成本". Free tier storage 500MB with compressed images; PRO 20G; MAX 500G (https://help.flomoapp.com/basic/storage.html); text storage is "可以认为是无限量存储" (effectively unlimited). Note a first-party contradiction on voice quota: /membership/pro.html says PRO "10分/条·500分/月" and MAX "30分/条·1500分/月", while /ai/aivoice.html says PRO "每月 1,000 次" and MAX "每月 9,999 次" — minutes vs. invocations, both first-party, unreconciled.

**Capture UX**

The input box is deliberately chat-shaped: "flomo 专门设计了如聊天窗口一般的输入框" (https://help.flomoapp.com/basic/quick-input.html) — no title field, no formatting. Documented capture routes: long-press the app icon; a home-screen quick-input widget ("在手机端，你可以通过长按 flomo 的图标，或者添加桌面快捷输入小部件"); desktop menu-bar icon (Mac top-right / Windows tray) with ⌘+N / Ctrl+N global shortcut; browser extension; WeChat official account ("flomoapp") capture; API/webhook; and the URL scheme flomo://create with `content` (max 5000 chars pre-encoding) and `image_urls` (up to 9) (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/api.html). VOICE (https://help.flomoapp.com/ai/aivoice.html): start by "长按底部「+」按钮" (long-press the bottom + button) or tapping the record button in the input field. Per-clip length: 1 minute free, 10 minutes PRO, 30 minutes MAX. WeChat capture accepts "文字、图片" only — NO voice messages, and only one WeChat account can be bound (https://help.flomoapp.com/basic/wechat-input.html). No documented Siri Shortcuts, no lock-screen capture button, and no Apple Watch app — a recent 5-star CN review explicitly requests one: "希望可以开发watch端…希望可以抬腕用watch进行简易语音记录" (iTunes customer-reviews RSS for id 1552314395). Offline input works; desktop needs one online init then "离线可继续记录和编辑" (FAQ). Hard limits: 30,000 chars per note, 8,000 chars per paste (FAQ). iOS lock-screen and home-screen widgets exist but for REVIEW output, not capture (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/lucky.html).

**Processing and AI**

Voice: "AI 转写，修复错字及去除口癖" — AI transcription that fixes typos and strips verbal tics (um, like). The cleaned text is "自动插入笔记正文" (auto-inserted into the note body) and the raw original transcript is kept but "默认折叠" (collapsed by default). Languages: "目前支持简体中文和英文，后续将逐步增加更多语言" — Simplified Chinese and English ONLY, more promised. NO DUTCH. No model or engine is named anywhere in the docs. Crucially: voice transcription does NOT auto-assign tags, does not classify, does not extract tasks — /ai/aivoice.html documents no tagging or summarisation beyond typo/tic removal. That is the whole point: the AI cleans the text and stops. Other AI: 相关笔记 (Related Notes, PRO) surfaces "与当前 MEMO 最有关的内容" on demand — mechanism not disclosed, but 认知地图 (Cognitive Map, PRO) explicitly states clustering is "笔记之间向量化相似度匹配的结果" (vectorised-similarity matching), so embeddings exist over the corpus. AI 洞察 (AI Insights, PRO 2/day, MAX 50/day) is invoked by swiping left on a single note and picks an analytic lens — "识别核心主题、揭示思维盲点、提出深刻问题" — with user-definable custom prompts (Daily Affirmation, Meaning Radar, Value Clarification). flomo Agent (MAX only) is "读过你所有笔记的 AI，在微信里和你对话"; v5.5.0 (2026-08-30) brought it natively into the iPhone/iPad app: "【重磅】flomo Agent 原生来到 iPhone 和 iPad…你可以直接和自己的笔记对话". Agent runs on a points economy: 1000 points/day for MAX, a conversation costs "几点到几十点", and consumption is higher at peak hours "9:00–12:00、14:00–18:00" (https://help.flomoapp.com/ai/agent-points.html). No model names disclosed. Processing is cloud (no on-device claim anywhere).

**Data model**

THE ENTIRE MODEL IS: one entity (MEMO) + tags + citations. No folders, no notebooks, no projects, no people, no status, no due dates.

MEMO: plain text up to 30,000 chars, optional images (≤9 via URL scheme), optional audio, created_at, version history (/basic/history.html). No title field. No status field. No type field. Nothing is a task.

TAG: created inline with `#tag` typed anywhere in the body — start, middle or end. Hierarchy is a STRING CONVENTION, not a structure: `#标签/子标签`, and the doc's example `#Books/Different/营销近视症` "这样就相当于创建了三个层级的标签" (creates three levels); no documented depth cap. Because the hierarchy lives in the tag string, renaming a tag to `#Parent/Child` is how you "move" it into a parent — reparenting is a string rewrite, not a move operation. Tags sort alphabetically; users are told to prefix `#01_` to force order. Deleting a tag offers "仅移除标签" (detach, keep notes) or "删除标签和笔记". Constraints: no emoji, no special chars (`#读书&学习` breaks queries), English multiword needs `#Apple_seed`. NO AI AUTO-TAGGING ANYWHERE — every tag is typed by the human at capture time or added during review.

LINKS: `@` inline quick-citation of another memo, annotation (批注) attaching a note to an existing note, and pasting a memo permalink. Backlinks exist but "本功能的反向链接部分，需 PRO 会员权限" (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/thread.html). No graph beyond this.

INBOX — the direct answer: there is NO inbox entity and NO unprocessed state. There are two substitutes. (1) A system-level filter: search offers a one-tap quick filter for "无标签笔记" (untagged notes) alongside has-image / has-audio / has-link / 去年今日 (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/search.html), and Daily Review can be scoped to untagged items. So "untagged" IS the de-facto inbox, derived rather than stored. (2) A methodological convention: flomo's own tag-planning doctrine is P.A.I.R — Project (项目), Area (领域), Inbox (收件箱, "temporary, unprocessed content held for later organization"), Resource (资源) (https://help.flomoapp.com/thinking/iarp.html). The inbox is a TAG the user is advised to create, not a state the system enforces. Nothing ever forces triage; a note can live untagged forever and still be surfaced by Daily Review, Random Walk, Related Notes and the Cognitive Map.

CAPTURE→TASK/PROJECT/PERSON: does not exist. There is no task type, no project entity, no person entity, no reminder. The App Store description explicitly lists what flomo refuses: document editing, web archiving, reminders, mind mapping, AI text generation. A "project" is at most a tag named `#项目/x`. A person is at most a tag.

WHY IT DOESN'T ROT (the counterexample mechanism): flomo replaces triage-at-capture with retrieval-at-review, and does it four ways. (a) 每日回顾 Daily Review (PRO): default "全部内容 + 全部时间 + 8 条/天", user-settable to 4/8/12/16/20/24 per day, scoped by content (all / specific tags / excluded tags / untagged), and by time window (all / 1y / 6m / 3m / 1m). Rule-based sampling, NOT relevance ranking. Delivered via sidebar, iOS+Android home-screen widgets, iOS lock-screen widget, and scheduled push framed as "这些推送更像是你写给自己的一封封信" (these pushes are more like letters you wrote to yourself). (b) 随机漫步 Random Walk (PRO, mobile only): "从一条笔记出发，沿着笔记之间隐约的关联，游走到另一条" — system-discovered associations that "常常串起你从未用同一个标签整理过、却其实是一回事的记录" (link records you never filed under the same tag but which were the same thing). Ends with a "明信片" summarising time span, note count, word count and where the biggest thematic jump happened. (c) 认知地图 Cognitive Map (PRO): embedding clusters rendered as contour "山峰", showing what you keep returning to without ever having tagged it. (d) 相关笔记 Related Notes (PRO), injected into the home feed, into Daily Review and into search results — with a toggle to HIDE same-tag results so recommendations stay diverse. The official review doctrine (https://help.flomoapp.com/thinking/how-to-review.html) makes review the filing step, not capture: during active review you "remove tags from consumed content, add new tags for broader connections, expand and edit notes, delete obsolete information", and "每次回顾，我们大脑不仅仅是找到过去存储的内容就结束了，还会增加许多相关的新信息". So filing is deferred, amortised, and optional — and the 4 surfacing engines mean an unfiled note is still reachable. That is the actual answer to "why doesn't unstructured capture rot": it rots only if retrieval depends on filing. flomo made retrieval depend on embeddings + scheduled resurfacing instead.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval has three doors and no folder tree. (1) Tag list in the sidebar, alphabetical, hierarchy rendered from the `/` string. (2) Search (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/search.html): keyword over note body and tags, "多个关键词之间用空格分隔", plus filters for date (本周 / 本月 / custom), tag include vs exclude (包含 / 不包含), and media type (has image / has audio / has link / untagged). Quick buttons include 去年今日 (this day last year). NO boolean operators documented, NO semantic/AI search surface documented in the search doc — the semantic layer is exposed only through Related Notes, Random Walk and the Cognitive Map, not through the search box. (3) The resurfacing loop described in dataModel. Also 热力图/统计 (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/notes-graph.html) with what the homepage calls "多达八个维度统计分析" — habit/streak gamification of the capture behaviour itself. Note the retention design in the onboarding rewards (https://help.flomoapp.com/membership/gift.html): free PRO days for adopting each capture route and the review loop — browser input +2天, app input +2天, WeChat +3天, API +3天, 每日回顾 +7天, import +3天 (20 days total). The single largest reward is for turning on Daily Review — they pay users to install the retrieval habit, which is exactly the habit that makes the no-folders model survive.

**Outputs and integrations**

Export is HTML only, for all users including free, from Settings → account detail, plus filtered export by tag or search result (https://help.flomoapp.com/basic/storage.html, https://help.flomoapp.com/faq.html). No Markdown, no JSON, no CSV documented — a real lock-in weakness. Inbound: incoming webhook write API ("flomo 提供了调用方便的记录 API"), flomo://create URL scheme, browser extension, WeChat official account, and "数十款产品" of third-party integrations per the homepage including 微信读书 (WeChat Reading) and 得到 (Dedao) highlight sync. Outbound AI: MCP server (https://help.flomoapp.com/advance/mcp) connecting flomo to "豆包工作、WorkBuddy、ChatGPT、Claude 等 AI 工具" so an external assistant can search old notes, read the AI 记忆档案 (MAX) and write conclusions back into flomo. Platforms per FAQ: iPhone, iPad, Android, HarmonyOS (鸿蒙), Windows, Mac, Web and PWA. No Zapier, no calendar, no task-manager integration — consistent with having no task entity.

**Strengths**

- Retrieval does not depend on filing. Four independent resurfacing engines (Daily Review, Random Walk, Cognitive Map, Related Notes) mean an untagged note is still findable, which is why six years of unstructured capture has not produced a graveyard. This is the direct counterexample to 'unstructured capture rots'.
- Zero decisions at capture. No title, no folder, no type, no status — literally a chat box. Capture cost is the lowest of any tool in this study, and the tag is optional and inline rather than a separate step.
- Monetises the RETRIEVAL side, not the capture side. Capture is free forever; Daily Review, Related Notes, Cognitive Map, Random Walk, backlinks, AI Insights are all PRO. Users pay to get their own past back, which is a recurring need, unlike capture which is a one-time need.
- Onboarding pays for the habit that creates the retention: +7 free PRO days for enabling Daily Review, the largest single reward in the programme.
- Ruthless scope refusal, published as a feature. The 'what we are bad at' section and 'refuses All in one' stance means the product never accumulates the config surface that kills capture speed.
- The AI does exactly one thing to a voice note — cleans typos and removes verbal tics — and then gets out of the way. No classification the user has to check, therefore no review queue.
- Sells a methodology (P.A.I.R, the book 《笔记的方法》) alongside the tool, which teaches users a workflow that fits the product's constraints instead of fighting them.
- Hierarchy without hierarchy: `#a/b/c` gives a tree's browsing affordance with a tag's many-to-many freedom, and reparenting is just a rename.

**Weaknesses**

- Voice supports Simplified Chinese and English ONLY. No Dutch, no other European language, with only a vague promise: '后续将逐步增加更多语言'.
- Export is HTML only. No Markdown, JSON or CSV documented — meaningful lock-in for a tool whose whole pitch is a lifelong personal archive.
- Free tier is now thin: 1-minute voice clips, 500MB storage with forced image compression, and every retrieval feature paywalled. Recent CN reviews complain about paywalled AI and 'aggressive AI feature promotion'.
- No task, reminder, project or person entity at all — by design, but it means flomo cannot be the only tool for anyone who has to DO something with a thought.
- No semantic search in the search box. The embedding layer only surfaces through recommendation UIs, so a user who knows what they want and can't recall the exact words is stuck with keyword search.
- Search has no boolean operators documented.
- Tag strings are brittle: no emoji, no special characters, spaces break English tags — a typed-tag model with syntax rules is a typo trap at capture speed.
- No Apple Watch, no lock-screen capture, no Siri Shortcut documented — the fastest routes into a phone are unused, and users are asking for them in reviews.
- WeChat channel accepts text and images only, not voice messages — the highest-volume Chinese capture surface can't take the flagship input mode.
- Recent CN reviews report Mac↔mobile sync losing edits, plus intrusive rating prompts.
- Agent point economy charges more during business hours (9-12, 14-18), which is a load-shedding mechanism dressed as pricing and will annoy the users who need it most.
- No published funding, headcount or audited user numbers — a small team carrying a lifelong-archive promise is a real durability question.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Steal the core inversion: make retrieval independent of triage. Our 0.75/0.45 confidence gate assumes an unfiled capture is a debt. flomo proves an unfiled capture is only a debt if the ONLY way back to it is the filing. Before shipping more Inbox pressure, build the resurfacing side — embeddings over transcripts + a scheduled daily resurfacing of N captures — and see how much of the Inbox stops mattering.
- Make our Inbox a DERIVED VIEW, not a stored state. flomo's inbox is a one-tap filter for 'untagged' plus a user-created #Inbox tag. For us, 'inbox' could simply be `actions where project_id is null and status = 'new'` — a saved filter, not a lifecycle stage. That removes the 'process your inbox' obligation without removing the affordance.
- Ship a Daily Review with flomo's exact settings shape: count (4/8/12/16/20/24), scope (all / specific category or project / EXCLUDE a category / only-uncategorised), time window (all / 1y / 6m / 3m / 1m). Rule-based sampling, not relevance ranking — it is far simpler than it looks and users configure it once.
- The lock-screen and home-screen widget is for OUTPUT, not input, in flomo. We are building a capture app; a review widget that shows one old capture on the lock screen is a retention mechanic we get almost free and that nobody in the voice-note category is doing.
- Copy the reward-for-habit onboarding literally: give free days for connecting each capture route AND the largest reward for turning on the review loop. Pay for the habit that predicts retention, not the habit that predicts signup.
- Adopt the voice-cleanup contract: transcribe, fix typos, remove verbal tics, insert the clean text, and keep the raw transcript COLLAPSED BUT PRESENT. That last detail is trust-preserving and cheap — our 'understand' step should always leave the raw transcript one tap away.
- 'Hide same-project suggestions' — flomo lets you suppress same-tag results in Related Notes so recommendations stay diverse. Our project matcher should have the same escape hatch, otherwise it will keep proposing the project the capture already looks like.
- Reconsider whether flat projects need to be an entity at all versus a path-string convention. flomo's `#a/b/c` gives hierarchy affordances with zero schema cost and reparenting is a rename. Our flat projects table buys us summaries; the string model buys us free nesting later.
- Publish a methodology, not just an app. P.A.I.R plus a book is why flomo's users tolerate the constraints — they were taught a workflow that the constraints serve. A short Dutch/English 'how to use Delta' doctrine is cheaper than features and does the same job.
- Note what flomo does NOT auto-tag: nothing. Zero AI classification, and 4.93 stars. That is evidence that our suggested_* columns and the confidence gate are a cost we are choosing to pay, not table stakes. Worth an explicit A/B: auto-file vs. never-file-but-resurface.
- The 'this day last year' quick filter is a one-line query that produces disproportionate emotional payoff. Add it.
- Their AI Insights takes ONE note and applies a user-chosen lens (blind spots, core theme, hard question), with custom prompts. That is a much cheaper and more differentiated AI surface than summarising everything, and it maps onto our 'statement' and 'question' intents.
