### Craft

<https://www.craft.do/>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

A beautiful document workspace that has been slowly annexing task management. Tagline: "Your space for notes, tasks, and big ideas", with "Craft isn't just for one thing, it's for your things" (craft.do). It bundles five pillars — Docs, Tasks, Calendar, Whiteboards, Daily Notes — and the writing pitch is "capture ideas instantly across all your devices, then refine them when you're ready." Design credibility is the moat: the site claims Mac App of the Year, Apple Design Awards finalist, three Webby Awards and a German Design Award (note: the site says FINALIST, not winner, for the ADA — worth correcting in our own notes). Target personas shown are individuals with a craft — podcasters, photographers, PMs, educators, writers, site managers, students — not enterprise teams. Recent strategic direction is developer/AI-ecosystem: an "Imagine" section touting 200+ builders, MCP connections to Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor and VS Code, and API partnerships with Lovable, Replit, Bolt and v0. Critically, Craft is a WRITING tool that grew tasks, and it did so very recently — the major Tasks overhaul shipped 2026-07-07 and tags only on 2026-07-21 (craft.do/blog). It is not a capture tool and it has no voice at all.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Craft Docs Limited. iOS app first released 2020-11-10 — nearly six years in market, roughly twice Superlist's age. Version 3.6.0 released 2026-08-31, i.e. today (itunes.apple.com/search, retrieved 2026-08-31); a lookup call minutes earlier still reported 3.5.6 from 2026-08-24, so they are shipping weekly. App Store US: 4.84 stars from 6,597 ratings — this matches the ~6,600 figure and is 14.8x Superlist's 446, at a materially higher rating. Bundle is 318.5MB across 151 supported device models. Localised UI in EN, FR, DE, HU, IT, JA, KO, ZH, ES — no Dutch. Docs localised into 11 languages (German, Spanish, French, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Polish, Brazilian Portuguese, Vietnamese, Simplified and Traditional Chinese), 169 pages each — no Dutch. Release cadence from craft.do/blog: 3.4.2 (2026-05-04, sidebar customisation + reminders), 3.4.3 (2026-05-19, Collections/Web/Windows), 3.4.4 (2026-06-05, flexible Assistant, BYOK, MCP v2), 3.5.0 (2026-07-07, "more than 50 improvements to task management" — the major Tasks update, plus Summer Sale, performance, better Windows app), 3.5.2 (2026-07-21, Tags), 3.5.4 (2026-08-10, Quick Entry and Daily Note template support). Awards claimed on craft.do: Mac App of the Year, Apple Design Awards FINALIST, three Webby Awards, German Design Award. A companion Craft Web Clipper app exists (id 6748613311, 3.80 stars from just 5 ratings). Recent App Store reviews are mostly glowing ("the most underrated app ever", 5 stars v3.5.4; "This app that I have been waiting for! It is all on one solution", 5 stars v3.5.4) with real bug complaints — a 2-star "🤬 Are the Devs even fixing stuff?!" (v3.5.1) reporting inability to type on the iPhone app, and repeated requests for an undo button (5 stars, v3.5.1) and side-by-side block layout (4 stars, v3.5.5).

**Platforms**

macOS (offline built in) · iOS / iPadOS (min iOS 17.0, v3.6.0, apps.apple.com id1487937127; Apple Pencil, gestures, Back Tap) · Windows (Assistant present but no Execute mode) · Web (Assistant present but no Execute mode) · Android — "The Android app launched in November 2025", still receiving offline and feature parity work · No Apple Watch, no voice, no audio recording on any platform

**Pricing**

CONFLICTING SOURCES — flagging both. craft.do/pricing (EUR, retrieved 2026-08-31): Free €0; Plus €6.40/month or €8.00/month (the page labels one as yearly-billed and one as monthly, but the fetched rendering was ambiguous about which is which — treat the €6.40 as the annual-equivalent monthly rate, UNVERIFIED); Family €12.00/€15.00 per month as a flat bundle for 2–6 Plus accounts; Team €50.00/month flat for up to 10 Plus accounts. The help centre (craft-support.mintlify.app/en/account-and-subscription/subscription-plans/plans-and-pricing.md) states USD and is cleaner: Plus "$96 annually or $10 monthly"; Family "$180 annually or $18 monthly", "Invite up to 5 members"; Team "$600 annually or $60 monthly", "Invite up to 10 members". The two pages do not reconcile on member counts either (pricing page says 2–6 for Family, docs say up to 5). FREE TIER LIMITS (craft.do/pricing, corroborated by the docs): "Up to 1,500 content blocks", 1GB storage, 25MB per-file upload, 7-day version history, 15 AI assistant credits, Core and Fast models only. Crucially the free tier has NO link-sharing, NO cross-device sync and NO Shared Space — a block-counted, single-device free tier, which is unusually restrictive. Paid Plus adds unlimited blocks and storage, 5 spaces, 1TB per space, 5GB per-file uploads, 30-day history, 50 AI credits/month, the Max model, custom domains and analytics. AI CREDITS translate to sessions (craft-support .../ai-assistant/usage.md): Free "~15–25 AI sessions (lifetime)" — lifetime, not monthly; Plus Monthly "~25–50 AI sessions per month"; Plus Yearly "~250–500 AI sessions per year"; Team Monthly "~150–300 AI sessions per month (team) + ~25–50 for the owner"; Education and Setapp variants also listed. "Cloud-based models (Core, Fast, Max) count toward your quota", while on-device models are free and unlimited. Education and Setapp plans exist. Solo/team split: Craft is priced solo-first with bundles bolted on — Family and Team are flat multi-seat bundles of the SAME Plus product, not a differentiated team tier. There is no per-seat business plan with admin controls in the fetched pricing.

**Capture UX**

Craft has NO VOICE CAPTURE AND NO AUDIO RECORDING. This is the headline finding. The mobile features doc (craft-support .../introduction/mobile-features.md) lists gestures, Quick Actions, Apple Pencil, Back Tap, Collections on iOS, Apple Shortcuts, widgets and drawings — and makes no mention whatsoever of voice dictation or audio recording. The Apple Shortcuts doc exposes only five actions — "Add to Doc", "Create Craft Doc", "Open Craft", "Open Document", "Open Space" — with NO task-creation action, and the only voice reference is deferring to the OS: "Voice notes: Use dictation to create voice notes that automatically save to Craft", i.e. iOS system dictation typed into a Craft action, not a Craft feature. Text capture, by contrast, is genuinely well designed. DESKTOP QUICK ENTRY (craft-support .../introduction/quick-entry.md): Ctrl+Space from any app opens a capture panel with four modes and explicit default destinations — New Document → Unsorted; New Task → Task Inbox; Daily Note entry → today's date; Text addition → the last document you selected. It converts Markdown (headings, lists, to-dos, quotes, code) into real blocks, lets you override the destination via a dropdown, switch Spaces or accounts, attach schedule/deadline in task mode, customise the shortcut in Settings → Advanced, and capture-and-open with Cmd+Shift+Return. It requires Craft running in the background and will not display over full-screen apps. MOBILE QUICK ACTIONS (craft-support .../introduction/mobile-features/quick-actions.md): tap the + at the bottom for three options — Document (with a neat heuristic: input under 20 characters becomes the title, 20+ characters becomes content and then prompts for a title, and you choose the destination folder), Task ("Your task will be added to your Inbox for later organization"), and Daily Note ("If you haven't created a daily note for today yet, Craft will automatically create one when you use Quick Add"). Other capture surfaces: Back Tap integration (double/triple tap the back of the iPhone to trigger a Shortcut), home-screen widgets, a Web Clipper (separate App Store app, id 6748613311), Email to Craft, and a URL scheme. Offline: native macOS and iOS have built-in offline support; Web, Windows and Android require a one-time Settings toggle. No lock-screen capture and no Apple Watch app are documented.

**Processing and AI**

There is NO capture-time AI. Craft Assistant is explicitly user-invoked and never runs on ingestion: "The Assistant responds to explicit queries rather than operating autonomously", and the docs contain no mention of automatic content categorisation or filing (craft-support .../ai-assistant/using.md). It works "in Documents, Tasks, Calendar, Collections, Code Editor, and browse views", operating at document-level and space-level context, and it "can understand Collections, including properties and relationships." Two modes on macOS and iOS: "Explore" proposes changes awaiting approval, "Execute" applies modifications directly; Windows and Web have the Assistant but lack Execute. Task creation/modification by the Assistant is NOT documented — unverified, and notable given Tasks is a headline pillar. Models are branded rather than named: Core, Fast and Max are the cloud tiers (underlying vendors unverified — the branding deliberately hides them), plus free unlimited on-device models. Craft shipped on-device AI early — a blog post dated 2025-01-31 covers "on device AI capabilities inside the Craft Assistant" via DeepSeek R1 (craft.do/blog/deepseek-r1-in-craft-assistant). There is also Bring Your Own Key ("Connect to AI Providers", shipped in 3.4.4 on 2026-06-05), Smart Search, Document Review, OCR over images and PDFs, Custom Prompts, and a Help Agent. LANGUAGES: the iOS app ships EN, FR, DE, HU, IT, JA, KO, ZH, ES — NO DUTCH (itunes lookup id=1487937127). Docs are localised into 11 languages, none of them Dutch. Transcription: not applicable, there is none. Net assessment for us: Craft's AI is a writing and retrieval assistant bolted onto a finished document, the polar opposite of our understand-step-at-ingestion architecture. Nothing in Craft ever guesses a category, a project or a person.

**Data model**

ENTITIES. (1) Space — the top container and the sharing/isolation boundary. "Each Space has its own documents, folders, and organization, so the contents of one Space stay independent from the others." You start with one default personal Space and can add more; Plus allows 5 spaces at 1TB each. Moving content BETWEEN spaces is not documented (unverified), and block links are only guaranteed "within the same Space." (2) Folder — organisational container inside a Space; documents can be starred, moved into folders, sorted, and deleted to Recently Deleted. New documents from Quick Entry land in "Unsorted". (3) Document — "top-level files in Craft". (4) BLOCK — the atomic unit and the whole point of the model: "In Craft, everything you create is a block. Every paragraph, image, table, or heading is its own independent unit." Each block can be styled, moved, duplicated independently. Block types include paragraph, headings, image, table, code, math formula, Mermaid diagram, toggle, card, drawing, whiteboard, file/link, date, and checkbox/todo. (5) PAGE — not a separate entity but a STATE of a block: "any block can become a page. When a block contains nested content beneath it, it's called a page." Every block "can serve a dual purpose: As a piece of text or content on the current page [or] As the title/entry point to a deeper page with its own nested content." Grouping is explicit: "The first block in your selection becomes the page title, and all other blocks move inside it." HOW THIS DIFFERS FROM AN OUTLINER: an outliner (Roam, Logseq, Workflowy) treats every bullet as a node in one infinite tree, and 'zooming in' is just changing the root — there is no document boundary and typography is uniform. Craft keeps a real Document boundary (documents are files, live in folders, export as PDF/Word/Markdown, publish to a URL, have cover images and version history) while making the INSIDE of a document recursive. And it adds a typographic layer an outliner refuses: the "Page" text style and Card style are "specifically designed for blocks that contain nested content, making them visually distinct as entry points." So nesting is a design affordance you opt into per block, not the universal substrate. Blocks maintain stable identity — "block links are preserved and continue to work correctly" when moved within a Space — but the docs give an important caveat: "a 'cut & paste' action will delete the connection", while drag-and-drop preserves it. Indent depth limits exist as a doc topic (craft-support .../blocks-and-pages/indent-limits.md) though the specific number was not stated in what I fetched — unverified. (6) TASK — a checkbox block, not a separate table. Created six ways: in the Inbox tab, the + in Tasks view, the + on hovering Tasks in the sidebar, the global Cmd+J New Task popover, converting text via Cmd+Shift+S (Schedule) / Cmd+Shift+D (Deadline) / Cmd+Shift+R (Reminder), or typing "x" then space inline in any document. A task carries "up to three dates": a SCHEDULE (when it appears on Calendar and the daily note), a DEADLINE, and a REMINDER — three separate date fields, which is a more expressive model than our single due date — plus repeat rules. Natural-language date entry was added in 3.5.0. Subtasks come from block nesting: "Nest notes, links, or even other tasks beneath it and it grows into a full page." The killer property: "the same task spans your whole space and shows up wherever it is relevant" — checking it off anywhere updates it everywhere. NO ASSIGNEE: assigning tasks to people is not documented anywhere in the Tasks docs. (7) INBOX — a genuine unprocessed state, but ONLY FOR TASKS: "The Inbox is the first tab and holds tasks that are not attached to any document." It exists to "capture tasks quickly without tying them to a document." Exit is a single move: "move an Inbox task into any document and it drops in at the top." So inbox-ness is defined as NULL document_id — elegant, but it means a captured NOTE has no unprocessed state at all; a note goes straight to Unsorted or into today's daily note and is never triaged. (8) Tasks views: Inbox, Today (auto-rolls incomplete past tasks forward, toggle past-dated and completed, group by document), Upcoming (by day/week/month with year headers, sortable by schedule, deadline or document), All (every task grouped by source document: Inbox first, then pinned documents, then the rest). (9) DAILY NOTE — a document keyed to a date, reachable from the date picker in the Calendar view sidebar, auto-created on first Quick Add for that day, and with a template applied at creation ("The template is applied at the moment a daily note is created"), set in Settings → Templates or inserted ad hoc via /template. Daily Note templates only shipped in 3.5.4 (2026-08-10). (10) COLLECTION — the database primitive, living INSIDE documents: "a fully customizable set of fields where each entry can hold more detailed information." 14+ field types: Title (mandatory, cannot be deleted, only renamed), Text, Email, URL, Phone, Number (currency/percentage/decimal), Checkbox, Single Select, Multiple Select, Date, Link to block, and a Relational Field connecting Collections to each other. Limits: max 20 custom fields plus Title (21 total), and under 1,000 rows recommended for performance. Each entry "expands into its own sub-page or sub-document" that can be shared standalone — so rows ARE documents. Views: Table, Gallery, Kanban, all with sort/filter/group and saved field visibility. (11) TAGS — shipped 2026-07-21. Hash-prefixed tokens indexed into a Tags view, with subtags via the FIRST slash only (later slashes are literal), bulk rename/delete across a Space, and deliberate exclusions (Collection select values marked with # are not indexed; ## is not a tag). (12) LINKS/BACKLINKS — "You can link another document or block inside the same Space", and "Backlinks are created automatically! Anytime you @-mention a document / block, a backlink will be added to the content that you linked", surfaced "at the bottom of the linked content under Links To This Page." NOT PRESENT: no Person/contact entity, no assignee, no Project entity (a folder, document or Collection stands in), no category enum, no confidence score, no AI-suggestion shadow fields, no capture entity, no audio, no transcript, no research-session entity. HOW A CAPTURE BECOMES A TASK/PROJECT/PERSON LINK: entirely manually. Quick Entry mode selection decides doc-vs-task-vs-daily-note at the moment of typing; a task leaves the Inbox when a human drags it into a document; a 'project' link is a human filing the document into a folder or @-mentioning it; a 'person' link is a human typing a name — there is no person object to link to at all.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Rich and manual: Spaces → folders → documents → nested blocks, plus tags (new, July 2026), automatic backlinks with a "Links To This Page" panel, Collections with relational fields and Table/Gallery/Kanban views, starring, pinning, Quick Open, in-document find and replace, and Smart Search (AI). Tasks aggregate across the whole Space into four views regardless of which document they live in — the single best structural idea in the product, and directly relevant to us: it decouples 'where the task is written' from 'where the task is worked'. Retrieval is strong for a human who has already organised; it does nothing automatically. Version history is 7 days free, 30 days on Plus.

**Outputs and integrations**

Export is a genuine strength and a stark contrast to Superlist: PDF, Word and Markdown per document, plus Folder Export and full Space Export. Import from Notion, Evernote, Quip, Google Docs, Obsidian, Bear, Apple Notes, OneNote, Roam Research, Anytype and TextBundle — an unusually complete migration story, clearly a deliberate acquisition strategy against Notion and Evernote. Publishing: documents publish to a public URL with custom domains, branding and analytics (Plus). Integrations: a Craft API, Email to Craft, Web Clipper (separate app), Apple Calendar integration, Apple Reminders sync (improved in 3.5.0), Readwise, widgets, a URL scheme, Apple Shortcuts (5 actions, no task action), Back Tap, and MCP (v2 shipped 3.4.4, with dedicated Security and ChatGPT-limitations docs). Free tier API/MCP access is 100 requests/min and 20,000 blocks/min. Collaboration: Shared Spaces, comments and notifications, team collaboration — but no task assignment.

**Strengths**

- Six years of compounding polish and 6,597 US ratings at 4.84 — the strongest satisfaction signal in this comparison by a wide margin, and proof that design quality alone can build a durable base in this category.
- The block-is-a-page recursion is genuinely elegant: one primitive, infinite structure, but with a real Document boundary preserved so files can be exported, published and versioned. It gets outliner flexibility without outliner homogeneity.
- "The same task spans your whole space and shows up wherever it is relevant" — writing a task inside a note and having it appear in Today without duplication is the single best idea in the product and directly applicable to us.
- Three separate date fields per task (schedule, deadline, reminder) is more expressive than a single due date and maps cleanly onto how people actually talk about time.
- Task Inbox is a clean formalism: inbox = no parent document. Defining an unprocessed state as a null foreign key rather than a separate table is a good pattern.
- Quick Entry's explicit destination-per-mode (Unsorted / Task Inbox / today's daily note / last document) is honest, predictable and fast — no AI needed to feel frictionless.
- The under-20-characters-becomes-the-title mobile heuristic is a small, cheap, delightful touch worth copying.
- Exceptional export and import story (PDF/Word/Markdown, folder and space export; import from 11 tools including Notion, Evernote, Obsidian, Apple Notes, Roam) — no lock-in, which builds trust.
- On-device AI models are free and unlimited, and Bring Your Own Key exists — a privacy and cost stance we should consider.
- Publishing with custom domains, branding and analytics turns private notes into a public output channel.
- MCP v2 with dedicated security docs positions them well in the agent ecosystem.

**Weaknesses**

- NO VOICE, NO AUDIO RECORDING, NO TRANSCRIPTION anywhere in the product, on any platform. The only voice path is iOS system dictation piped through an Apple Shortcut. For a six-year-old, award-winning, AI-marketing notes app in 2026 this is a gaping hole and the clearest evidence that voice-first is genuinely unoccupied.
- No AI at capture time at all. The Assistant "responds to explicit queries rather than operating autonomously" — no auto-categorisation, no filing, no intent detection. Structure is 100% manual labour.
- The Inbox covers TASKS ONLY. A captured note has no unprocessed state — it goes to Unsorted or today's daily note and is never triaged. Half the capture problem is unaddressed.
- No Person entity and no task assignee. You cannot record that a thought is about someone, and you cannot delegate. This is a hard ceiling on team use and on anything relationship-shaped.
- No Project entity — a folder, a document or a Collection stands in, with no summary and no rollup.
- Mode selection happens BEFORE capture: you must decide document vs task vs daily note in Quick Entry at the moment of typing. That is precisely the decision our confidence gate removes.
- The free tier is unusually hostile: 1,500 blocks, 1GB, NO cross-device sync and NO link-sharing. A notes app whose free tier does not sync between your phone and laptop is barely usable, and 15 AI credits is a LIFETIME allowance, not monthly.
- AI credits are opaque and stingy — Plus Monthly buys only "~25–50 AI sessions per month", and the models are branded Core/Fast/Max with vendors undisclosed.
- Pricing is inconsistent between the pricing page (EUR, €6.40/€8.00 Plus, Family 2–6) and the help centre (USD, $10/month or $96/year, Family up to 5), which is sloppy and makes the real price hard to state.
- Tasks are very new — the major overhaul only landed 2026-07-07 and tags 2026-07-21 — so the task half of "notes, tasks, and big ideas" is under a year old and still settling.
- Collections cap at 20 custom fields and degrade past ~1,000 rows, so it is not a real database.
- Block links break on cut-and-paste (only drag-and-drop preserves them) — a silent data-integrity trap.
- Windows and Web lack the Assistant's Execute mode; Android only launched November 2025 and still lacks offline by default.
- No Dutch UI and no Dutch documentation, across 9 app languages and 11 doc languages.
- Bug reports persist in recent reviews: a 2-star review on v3.5.1 reports being unable to type in the iPhone app, and users still ask for a basic undo.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Craft is the proof that our wedge is real: six years, an Apple-recognised design team, 6,597 ratings at 4.84, and still ZERO voice capture. If voice-first thought capture were easy or obviously valuable to incumbents, Craft would have shipped it. It has not.
- Adopt the 'a task lives where it was written but appears everywhere' principle. Our actions already have project_id and category; make sure an action captured against a project still surfaces in a global Today/Inbox without duplication — Craft proves users love this.
- Consider splitting our single due date into schedule vs deadline vs reminder. People say "I'll work on it Tuesday, it's due Friday" — three fields capture that; one does not. Cheap to add now, expensive later.
- Steal the mobile length heuristic: under ~20 characters becomes a title, longer becomes body. Small, cheap, and it makes capture feel intelligent without any model call.
- Model our unprocessed state the way Craft does — inbox membership as an absent link (null project_id / unconfirmed) rather than a separate table — but extend it to ALL action types, not just tasks. Craft's task-only inbox is a visible gap we can beat.
- Invert Craft's Quick Entry: they make you pick the mode before you speak; our understand step picks it after. That is the demo. Show the same thought captured in both apps side by side.
- Their free tier's lack of cross-device sync is a strategic error we should exploit — sync is the thing that makes capture trustworthy. Never paywall sync.
- Do NOT build blocks. Craft's block-is-a-page recursion is beautiful and took six years; our flat actions + flat projects model is the right choice for a solo beginner developer, and the CLAUDE.md decision to avoid nesting, backlinks and tags is validated by how much surface area Craft carries to support them.
- Copy the export posture early (Markdown export of captures and actions). Craft's 11-source import and PDF/Word/Markdown export build trust cheaply, and Superlist's total absence of export is a weakness we can turn into a differentiator against BOTH.
- Consider on-device transcription as a privacy story. Craft gives on-device AI away free and unlimited; Superlist names no subprocessor at all. 'Your voice never leaves your phone' would out-position both — though it conflicts with our cloud understand step, so it may be a later premium tier rather than a launch claim.
- Ship a Dutch UI. Neither product has one: Craft supports 9 app languages and 11 doc languages with no Dutch; Superlist ships EN/DE/ES. For a Belgian first market this is free differentiation.
- Watch the awards claim: craft.do says Apple Design Awards FINALIST, not winner. If we ever benchmark against them publicly, use their own wording.

### Gemini on Android (the voice entry point that replaced Google Assistant)

<https://support.google.com/android/answer/14554984>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The system-level assistant on Android. From 4 September 2026 it is not one option among several — Google Assistant is being switched off on Android phones, tablets, Wear OS watches and earbuds, and Gemini takes the assistant slot. Verified: androidauthority.com/google-assistant-shutdown-3694622 (5 Aug 2026) and howtogeek.com/google-assistant-shuts-down-on-android-in-september (5 Aug 2026) both state 4 September 2026; Google's user email says Assistant "will begin disappearing from Android phones on September 4" and "the process will take place over several weeks". This is the single biggest fact for our floor: the default voice capture path on every Android phone in Belgium is changing three days from now.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Google LLC. Play listing (fetched first-hand 31 Aug 2026, play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.google.android.apps.bard, en_US/US): 4.6 stars, 41.8M reviews, 1B+ downloads, updated 31 Aug 2026; the Phone-only tab shows 4.6 / 40.4M reviews. The listing itself carries the transition notice: "If you opt in to the Gemini app, it will replace your Google Assistant as the primary assistant on the phone. Some Google Assistant voice features aren't available through the Gemini app yet."

**Platforms**

Android (system assistant slot) · iOS · Wear OS 4+ (blog.google, 9 Jul 2025) · Web

**Pricing**

Free. Gemini app Play listing shows no purchase requirement; paid Google AI Plus/Pro/Ultra tiers exist for generation limits, not for note/task capture (verified: Play listing text, fetched 31 Aug 2026). Hidden price is privacy: Android Police (19 Apr 2025) quotes the requirement that "Enabling Gemini Apps Activity shares your prompts and personal data with Google for AI training. Google employees may read this data" — and Gemini cannot write to Keep/Tasks with that setting off (verified: support.google.com/gemini/answer/15230285 — "Have Keep Activity on. Gemini can't connect to these apps when this setting is off").

**Capture UX**

Invocation, verified from support.google.com/android/answer/14554984 (Android tab): "Say 'Hey Google' if enabled", "Press and hold the power button", "Touch and hold Home", "Swipe up from the bottom corner of your screen", or open the app. FROM A LOCKED DEVICE: support.google.com/gemini/answer/14576209 lists, among actions allowed without unlocking, "Handle some personal requests, like creating a reminder, events, or notes" — gated behind Settings > Gemini on lock screen > "Use Gemini without unlocking". Requests touching personal content still force an unlock: "if your requests include personal content, like when you read your calendar, Gemini will ask you to unlock your phone first". TAP COUNT FROM LOCKED PHONE: 0 taps with hotword ("Hey Google, maak een notitie…"); 1 long-press of the power button otherwise; then speak. That is the floor we must beat, and nothing a third-party app ships can equal it — the assistant slot is not available to us. Wear OS: "Hey Google", side-button long-press, or tapping the icon (verified: blog.google/products-and-platforms/platforms/wear-os/gemini-wear-os-watches, 9 Jul 2025), with examples "Remember I parked on level 4, spot 27" and "Remind me to go grocery shopping after work".

**Processing and AI**

Cloud (Gemini models; the Play listing names Gemini 3.6 and Gemini Omni as of the 31 Aug 2026 build). Intent routing to note/task apps is a first-party app-action layer, not something exposed to third parties. LANGUAGES: Dutch is explicitly listed as a supported Gemini app language and both Belgium and the Netherlands are in the country list (verified: support.google.com/gemini/answer/14579026, Android tab; the page states "Gemini mobile apps are currently available in multiple languages and more than 150 countries"). Caveat worth testing on a real device: support.google.com/gemini/answer/15984485 says the language setting "affects the languages that you can talk to Gemini in when you say 'Hey Google' or use the mic in the prompt field" but also that "Some features in Gemini Apps, like Gemini's voice setting, aren't available in all languages". Whether the specific Keep/Tasks write-actions fire reliably on Dutch phrasing ("zet op mijn boodschappenlijst") is UNVERIFIED — no Google page states this, and it is the cheapest high-value experiment we can run.

**Data model**

Gemini itself stores no user-facing capture entity. It is a router that writes into other apps' models. Notes/lists (verified: support.google.com/gemini/answer/15230597): "You can ask Gemini Apps to create and show your notes and lists in Google Keep, HONOR Notes, Lenovo Notes, OnePlus Notes, OPPO Notes, Samsung Notes, TECNO Notepad, vivo Notes, Xiaomi Notes". Phrases: "Create a note called [note name]", "Create a list called [list name] with [item 1, item 2, …]", "Add [items] to my list called [list name]", "Find my [list name] list". Hard limits, quoted: "Gemini Apps can't perform certain actions in your notes and lists apps for you, like: Share notes and lists, View images and drawings, Add an image" — and Android Police adds "Modify a note. Delete notes and lists." Tasks/reminders (verified: support.google.com/gemini/answer/15230285): targets are Google Tasks and Samsung Reminder (Galaxy S25+ / One UI 7+); phrases "Remind me on [date] at [time] to [task]", "What are my upcoming tasks?", "Mark [task name] as done in Tasks". So the capture verb set is CREATE-ONLY and APPEND-ONLY. There is no inbox, no unprocessed state, no confidence gate, no person link, no project. The routing decision (note vs task) is made once, silently, at capture time, and if it is wrong there is no re-file path through voice. History: 9to5google.com (21 Feb 2024) records that Gemini reminders were bolted on by calling Assistant to "save the reminder in Google Tasks at the correct date and time" — reminders were never a first-class Gemini entity.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Retrieval is by the destination app's own search (Keep search, Tasks lists). Gemini can read back "Find my [list name] list" and "What are my upcoming tasks?" but only by exact list/task naming — there is no semantic retrieval over your own captures, and no cross-app view. Gemini's own chat history is not a note store.

**Outputs and integrations**

Writes to Keep, Tasks, Calendar, Samsung Reminder and OEM notes apps. Documented gap: "The supported tasks and reminders apps aren't available in Gemini in Google Messages" (support.google.com/gemini/answer/15230285). Requires the Google Workspace connection toggled on in Gemini > Settings > Apps (Android Police, 19 Apr 2025).

**Strengths**

- Zero-tap, zero-unlock capture. Hotword or one power-button hold from a locked phone, with note/reminder creation explicitly permitted while locked. No third-party app can reach this.
- Dutch is a supported app language and BE/NL are supported countries — the language barrier we might have hoped for does not protect us.
- Pre-installed, free, and about to become unavoidable: the Assistant shutdown on 4 Sep 2026 pushes every Android user in our market onto it.
- Fans out to whatever notes app the OEM ships (Samsung Notes, Xiaomi Notes, etc.), so it works on cheap Android too.

**Weaknesses**

- Create-only. It cannot modify or delete a note, cannot re-file, cannot attach an image — verified from Google's own help page and corroborated hands-on by Android Police.
- The routing decision is invisible and final. You cannot see why it chose Keep over Tasks, and there is no inbox where a wrong guess can be corrected.
- Requires Gemini Apps Activity ON to write to Keep/Tasks, i.e. capture is paid for in training data. That is a concrete, quotable objection for a privacy-minded Belgian user.
- Retrieval is exact-name only ("Find my [list name] list"). Ask it about "that thing I said about the roof" and there is nothing.
- No entity richness: no people, no projects, no categories, no audio kept.
- Hallucination is disclaimed in the help page itself for the tasks flow.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Stop competing on time-to-capture. We lose that race by construction — we cannot own the power button, the hotword, or the lock screen. Compete on what happens AFTER the sound stops: the inbox, the correction loop, the project/person link. Our confidence gate is exactly the thing Gemini does not have.
- Position against the create-only ceiling explicitly: "Gemini can make a note. It cannot fix one." Our suggested_* columns and Inbox review are the product feature that answers this.
- Make Gemini a capture SOURCE rather than a competitor: a Keep note or Tasks item created by voice could be ingested by us (via Google Tasks API — see the Tasks entry) and enriched. Cheapest path to riding the free 0-tap surface instead of fighting it.
- Run the Dutch app-action test before any roadmap decision: does "Hey Google, zet melk op mijn boodschappenlijst" actually write to Keep on a Belgian phone? If Dutch routing is flaky, that is our beachhead and it is worth measuring properly.
- Treat 4 September 2026 as a market event. Assistant habits break that week; users go looking. That is a launch window, not a threat.

### Android system capture surfaces (home + lock-screen widgets, Quick Settings tiles, hardware buttons, Wear OS)

<https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/quicksettings-tiles>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The non-voice quick-capture real estate on Android. This is the surface an Expo app can partially occupy, so its tap economics decide whether we can ever be faster than opening our app from the launcher.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Lock-screen widgets on phones: shipped in Android 16 QPR2, first seen in beta 20 Aug 2025 (9to5Google) and covered as live on Pixel 4 Dec 2025 (Android Authority). Android Authority reports "you can use any existing Android widgets on the lock screen", "up to three widgets per page using the standard size", and calls the swipe gesture "very touchy". Quick Settings tiles: TileService is long-standing; the add-a-tile prompt is Android 13+.

**Platforms**

Android phones/tablets · Pixel lock screen (Android 16 QPR2+) · Wear OS

**Pricing**

Free / platform.

**Capture UX**

TAP COUNTS FROM A LOCKED PHONE, built from documented behaviour (each step sourced; totals are my arithmetic, not a Google claim): (a) Gemini hotword — 0 taps, note creation allowed while locked. (b) Gemini power-button long-press — 1 press. (c) Lock-screen widget on a Pixel — Android 16 QPR2 brought lock-screen widgets back to phones behind Settings > Display & touch > Lock screen > "Widgets on lock screen", reached by swiping left into what Android calls "hub mode"; 9to5Google states plainly: "Editing or tapping a widget through the new lock screen hub will require an unlocked device." So: 1 swipe + 1 tap + 1 biometric = ~3 actions, and the unlock is unavoidable. A lock-screen widget is therefore NOT a shortcut to instant recording; it is a shortcut to unlocking and then recording. (d) Quick Settings tile — pull down the shade (1–2 swipes) + 1 tap. Tiles are the most interesting case: "Your tile may display on top of the lock screen on locked devices", and for non-sensitive actions the tile can use startActivity() to launch over the lock screen, while sensitive ones call unlockAndRun(). (e) Home-screen widget, unlocked — 1 tap (Keep's Quick capture widget puts a dedicated audio button on the home screen; see the Keep entry). (f) Wear OS — raise wrist, swipe to a tile, tap: Google's own Recorder Play listing says it is "Available on Wear OS with a dedicated watch face tile to quickly capture moments and thoughts on your phone or Pixel Watch", and Google Keep offers Wear OS tiles/complications for "Add note"/"Add list" plus voice input (support.google.com/wearos/answer/7664632). The watch is the only surface where a third party gets close to zero-friction capture without owning the assistant slot — and it is the one Expo cannot reach at all.

**Processing and AI**

n/a (transport surfaces). The important platform rule lives here though: a widget tap or notification action IS an exemption from both the Android 12 background-FGS start ban and the Android 14 while-in-use restriction — Google's own list includes "The user performs an action on a UI element related to your app. For example, they might interact with a bubble, notification, widget, or activity", and the WIU exemption list names "The service starts by interacting with app widgets" and "...with a notification". Quick Settings TILES are not named in that WIU exemption list — whether a tile tap can legally start a microphone foreground service is UNVERIFIED and must be tested before we design around it.

**Data model**

n/a — these are launchers, not stores. Worth noting what they imply for ours: any widget-initiated capture must hand off to a foreground service with type "microphone" and show a persistent notification (see the platform-constraints entry), so our capture entity needs to survive a process that starts from a widget PendingIntent rather than from a warm app.

**Organisation and retrieval**

n/a

**Outputs and integrations**

requestAddTileService() (Android 13+) lets an app prompt the user to add its Quick Settings tile in one dialog rather than making them dig through the shade editor — the single best onboarding trick on this surface, and one almost no note app uses.

**Strengths**

- Widget and notification taps are explicitly exempted from Android's background-microphone restrictions — the platform has left exactly one legal door open for one-tap recording, and it is the widget.
- Quick Settings tiles can render over the lock screen and can launch a non-sensitive activity without an unlock.
- requestAddTileService() gives a one-dialog install path for the tile.
- Wear OS tiles genuinely beat the phone for spoken capture (no unlock, wrist-level).

**Weaknesses**

- Lock-screen widgets require an unlock to interact — the most-hyped 2025/26 surface is useless for instant capture.
- The lock-screen widget page is opt-in, off by default, and behind a swipe most users never discover.
- Fragmentation: lock-screen widgets are a Pixel/Android-16-QPR2 story; Samsung's lock screen behaves differently (UNVERIFIED for One UI specifics).
- Everything here is native Android surface area — see the platform entry for what Expo can and cannot reach.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Ship the home-screen Quick-capture widget as a first-class feature, not an afterthought: one tap, immediate recording, no app UI. This is the only place we can legally match a one-tap mic on Android, and it is where Keep already sets user expectations.
- Use requestAddTileService() (or its widget-pinning equivalent) inside onboarding so the shortcut actually gets installed instead of being a settings-screen rumour.
- Do NOT build a lock-screen widget expecting fast capture — it forces an unlock. If we build one, make it a status/inbox-count display, not a capture button.
- Test whether a Quick Settings tile can start a microphone foreground service. If it can, it is a genuinely differentiated 2-action capture from a locked phone; if it cannot, drop the idea early rather than discovering it in review.
- Treat Wear OS as out of scope for the Expo build and say so explicitly in planning, rather than leaving it as vague future work.

### Google Keep

<https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.google.android.keep>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The default destination for a spoken note on Android, and the app Gemini writes into. Pre-installed or one tap away on effectively every Android phone. Its own Play description leads with our pitch: "Inspiration strikes anywhere. Jot down a quick note, snap a photo of something you want to remember, or speak a voice memo on the go."

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Google LLC. Play listing fetched first-hand 31 Aug 2026 (en_US/US): 4.7 stars, 2.55M reviews, 1B+ downloads, "Updated on Aug 25, 2026"; the Phone-only tab shows 4.7 / 2.44M reviews. What's new: "Bug fixes and performance improvements". Recent reviews on that listing show the failure modes: a 27 Apr 2026 reviewer (33 found helpful) says "This app does NOT have autocorrect on my S23 Ultra, and hasn't for a couple years now"; a 12 Jun 2026 reviewer (202 found helpful) attacks the redesign — "I absolutely HATE the new look of this app... The notes which are pinned are much TOO NARROW!"; a 10 Mar 2026 reviewer (61 found helpful) describes the reminder-to-Calendar unification as a bug — "whenever you schedule something, it automatically creates a separate copy to your Google calendar app... You also cannot disable this feature."

**Platforms**

Android · iOS · Web · Wear OS · Chromebook · Tablet

**Pricing**

Free with a Google account.

**Capture UX**

In-app: "At the bottom right, tap Create. Select a type of note" with five types — "Audio", "Image", "Drawing", "List", "Text" (verified: support.google.com/keep/answer/2888246, Android tab). Home screen: three widgets — "Single note", "Note collection", and "Quick capture" (verified: support.google.com/keep/answer/13302793). The Quick capture widget carries a dedicated audio button: 9to5Google (10 Apr 2025) describes the redesigned widget as a text-note pill plus "list, audio, drawing, and camera" shortcuts in 5x1 / 4x1 / 3x1 sizes. So from an unlocked home screen a voice note is ONE tap. Hands-on friction, Android Police (29 Jun 2026): open Keep, tap Plus, select Audio — a three-step in-app path, and the author reports "Users can record up to approximately 30 seconds maximum" (that 30s figure is the reviewer's observation, not a Google-documented limit — treat as UNVERIFIED by Google). Wear OS: tiles and complications for "Add note" and "Add list", voice or text input, tap an item to check it off (verified: support.google.com/wearos/answer/7664632).

**Processing and AI**

Speech-to-text via the device's Google speech stack. Behaviour, verified from How-To Geek (8 Feb 2026): "This feature records a voice memo that you can playback, and it automatically inserts a transcript of the recording in the note as well" — so Keep keeps BOTH the audio and the text, in one note. That is architecturally the same choice we made (captures hold audio + transcript). Dutch: Keep's transcription rides the device recogniser, which does support Dutch for voice typing, but Google publishes no Keep-specific language list — UNVERIFIED. Quality caveat from the same Android Police piece: "I will admit that sometimes it isn't perfect...the microphone can't pick up some of my words", degraded by ambient noise. There is no AI cleanup, no summarisation, no intent detection, no auto-filing — the transcript is dumped raw into the note body.

**Data model**

Entities: Note, Label, Reminder, Collaborator/Permission, Attachment. Verified against the Keep API reference (developers.google.com/keep/api/reference/rest/v1/notes), which is the only place Google documents the shape precisely: a Note has name, createTime, updateTime, trashTime, trashed, attachments[], permissions[], title (max 1,000 chars) and body, where body is a Section containing EITHER TextContent (max 20,000 chars) OR ListContent (max 1,000 ListItems, single-level nesting only). Organisation is FLAT: labels are tags, "You can have up to 50 labels" (support.google.com/keep/answer/6191044), no nesting, no folders, no projects, no people-as-entities (only share permissions), no backlinks. States: pinned / normal / archived / trashed. THERE IS NO INBOX AND NO UNPROCESSED FLAG — a captured note is immediately "filed" by being in the undifferentiated grid, which is precisely why Keep degenerates into a pile. Reminders are not a Keep-owned entity any more: a dated reminder on a note is written into Google Tasks and shown on Calendar — "check your reminder in Google Tasks, Google Calendar, and Keep", with Tasks taking notification precedence (support.google.com/keep/answer/3187168); 2sync (8 Jun 2026) dates that unification to October 2025. A capture becomes a task by acquiring a reminder; it never becomes a project or a person link, because those entities do not exist. Note also what the API cannot do: v1.notes exposes create, delete, get, list and permission batch ops — there is no update/patch method at all, and the API is framed as "used in an enterprise environment to manage Google Keep content and resolve issues identified by cloud security software", i.e. it is an admin/DLP API, not an integration surface. Anyone wanting to build on top of Keep has effectively no supported write path.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Search over note text, plus filter by label, colour, type and reminder. Pinning to the top. No saved views, no semantic search, no automatic grouping. With 50 labels max and no hierarchy, a heavy user's Keep is a wall.

**Outputs and integrations**

Send a Keep note to another app, use Keep in Docs/Slides, export via Google Takeout. Gemini can create notes and lists in it but cannot modify or delete them. No usable public write API (see dataModel).

**Strengths**

- One-tap voice note from a home-screen widget, and it keeps audio AND auto-inserted transcript in the same note — the exact capture primitive we are building, already free and pre-installed.
- Wear OS tiles and complications for note/list creation, which we cannot match.
- 4.7 stars across 2.55M reviews and 1B+ downloads: it is not a weak incumbent, it is a beloved one.
- Genuinely instant and offline-tolerant for text; no account setup friction beyond an existing Google account.

**Weaknesses**

- No inbox, no triage, no unprocessed state. Every capture is immediately equal to every other capture — the pile problem is structural, not a UI oversight.
- Flat by design: 50 labels, no nesting, no projects, no people. Nothing to route a capture to.
- No AI processing of the capture itself: raw transcript in, raw transcript out. No intent detection, no category, no summarisation.
- Reminder behaviour now leaks into Tasks and Calendar in ways users experience as spam, per the 10 Mar 2026 Play review with 61 helpful votes.
- Transcription accuracy complaints in noise; short-form only in practice (~30s per the Android Police hands-on, unverified by Google).
- No supported write API — Keep is a destination, not a platform.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Our differentiator is stated most cleanly against Keep: same capture primitive, but an inbox and a confidence gate on top. Keep proves the market wants audio+transcript in one object; it never solved what happens next.
- Copy the Quick capture widget layout deliberately — a small row of typed capture buttons (voice first) is a proven pattern users already have muscle memory for.
- Steal the both-artefacts rule: never discard the audio when you have the transcript. Keep does this and users rely on it when transcription fails.
- Handle noise failure gracefully: when confidence is low (our 0.45 gate), surface the audio prominently instead of a mangled transcript. That is a concrete, demonstrable win over Keep.
- Do NOT compete on note-taking breadth (drawings, images, collaborators). Keep wins that and it is not our pitch.
- Because Keep has no write API, a "migrate from Keep" feature can only work via Google Takeout export — worth knowing before promising an import.

### Google Tasks

<https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.google.android.apps.tasks>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The default destination for a spoken task or reminder on Android, and now the system of record for Keep reminders and Gemini reminders too. Deliberately minimal.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Google LLC. Play listing fetched first-hand 31 Aug 2026 (en_US/US): 4.6 stars, 601K reviews, 10M+ downloads, "Updated on Aug 27, 2026"; Phone-only tab 4.6 / 579K reviews. Downloads are two orders of magnitude below Keep's 1B+, which tells you how few people actually adopt it as a standalone app. Recent reviews name the gaps precisely: 16 Jul 2026 (18 helpful) — "I really wish Google Tasks added location/proximity-based reminders... Outside of that, it's literally perfect"; 17 Aug 2026 — wants richer editing and repeatable checklists, "I find myself using Samsung Notes for this more often than Google Tasks for the flexibility it offers"; 6 Aug 2026 (7 helpful) — "They merged it with something else then made the assistant ai, and now my tasks/event reminders barely ever come up anymore... Nearly missed a doc appt because it just didn't pop up at all." Notification reliability is the live complaint, and it maps directly onto Android's Doze/OEM-killer problem (see the platform entry).

**Platforms**

Android · iOS · Web (Gmail/Calendar side panel) · Chromebook · Tablet — no Wear OS app

**Pricing**

Free with a Google account.

**Capture UX**

There is no voice capture in Google Tasks itself — no mic button, no audio note type. Voice only reaches Tasks THROUGH Gemini ("Remind me on [date] at [time] to [task]"), through Keep reminders, or via Gmail. This is a significant structural fact: the free Android stack has a voice front door (Gemini) and a task store (Tasks), but the task store has no ears of its own. Notably the Play listing's device tabs are Phone / Chromebook / Tablet — no Watch tab, unlike Keep and Recorder, so there is no Wear OS Tasks app (verified: Play listing fetched 31 Aug 2026).

**Processing and AI**

None. No transcription, no AI parsing of dates in-app, no summarisation. Any natural-language date parsing happens upstream in Gemini.

**Data model**

The cleanest documented model of the three, verified field-by-field from the Tasks REST API. TaskList: kind, id, etag, title (max 1,024 chars), updated, selfLink. Task: kind, id, etag, title (max 1,024 chars), updated, selfLink, notes (max 8,192 chars; "Tasks from Google Docs cannot have notes"), status ("needsAction" | "completed"), due, completed, parent, position, deleted, hidden, links[], webViewLink, assignmentInfo. Structure: a task belongs to exactly one list, and nesting is EXACTLY ONE LEVEL — parent is "Omitted for top-level tasks" and is read-only via the API, so subtasks exist but sub-subtasks do not. The `due` field carries a documented and consequential limitation: "Due date of the task (as a RFC 3339 timestamp)" with the time portion discarded — "read/write time not supported" at the API level, even though the app UI offers a time. NO TAGS, NO LABELS, NO COLOURS, NO ATTACHMENTS, NO PEOPLE, NO LOCATION REMINDERS, NO INBOX. 2sync (8 Jun 2026) puts it well: "Tasks organizes by list and subtask, a stricter hierarchy with no tags or colors at all." A capture becomes a task by being created with a title; it becomes 'projected' only in the weak sense of landing in a named list. There is no unprocessed state — a new task is simply an incomplete task at the top of a list. Quota: "The Tasks API has a courtesy limit of 50,000 queries per day" and no documented per-user caps on task or list counts (developers.google.com/workspace/tasks/limits).

**Organisation and retrieval**

Lists, star, reorder by drag, sort by date/starred. Search is weak-to-absent in the mobile app (UNVERIFIED whether a search field exists in the Aug 2026 build). Retrieval by scrolling a list.

**Outputs and integrations**

Deep Gmail and Google Calendar integration; receives Keep reminders and Gemini reminders; a real, usable public REST API (unlike Keep) with create/update/patch/delete on both tasks and lists — which makes Tasks the one realistic two-way integration point in the free Google stack.

**Strengths**

- Dead simple, free, and now the sink for every reminder in the Google ecosystem, so a spoken reminder reliably ends up somewhere findable.
- A real public REST API with full CRUD — the only Google capture destination we could actually integrate with two-way.
- Deep Gmail/Calendar placement means it is in front of users who never open the app.

**Weaknesses**

- No voice capture at all. The task store has no microphone; it depends entirely on Gemini as its front door.
- One level of subtask, no tags, no colours, no attachments, no people, no location reminders.
- API-level `due` discards the time of day, which quietly limits any integration we build on it.
- No Wear OS app.
- Notification unreliability is the dominant theme in recent first-hand reviews.
- 10M+ downloads vs Keep's 1B+: as a standalone destination it has weak pull.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Our action entity is already a superset of the Task resource (we have action_type, category, project_id, people M2M, and separate suggested_* columns; Tasks has title/notes/status/due/parent). Say that plainly in positioning rather than inventing new vocabulary.
- The one-level nesting rule is worth copying, not beating. Both Google surfaces cap at one level; users evidently do not need more, and our flat-projects decision is consistent with that.
- Google Tasks is the realistic integration target if we ever want "push my action to the user's existing system" — it has the API Keep lacks. Remember the due-time truncation when mapping.
- Location reminders are an explicitly-requested, explicitly-absent feature in a 2026 top review. Cheap differentiator if we ever want one — though it costs background location, which collides with the battery constraints in the platform entry.
- Notification reliability is a competitive weakness we can win on. If our reminders fire when Google's do not, that is a demonstrable, review-quotable advantage — and it is an engineering choice about exact alarms and battery-optimisation prompts, not a feature.

### WhatsApp — "Message yourself" self-chat, native voice-note transcription, and Meta AI

<https://faq.whatsapp.com/1785465805163404>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The de-facto inbox in Belgium and the Netherlands. Not a notes product, but the place where a huge share of people already dump thoughts, links and voice notes to themselves, because it is the app already open. The previous study flagged this as the highest-value unanswered question; the answer is below and it is favourable to us.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

WhatsApp LLC. Play listing fetched first-hand from the NETHERLANDS store 31 Aug 2026 (hl=en, gl=NL): 4.4 stars, 242M reviews, 10B+ downloads, "Updated on Aug 29, 2026", and the store badge "#1 top for €0 communication" — i.e. the number-one free communication app in the Dutch store. Message-yourself has been available since Dec 2022; transcripts since Nov 2024.

**Platforms**

Android · iOS · Web · Windows · Mac · Wear OS (voice messages from the wrist, per the Play listing)

**Pricing**

Free.

**Capture UX**

Self-chat setup, verified from the WhatsApp Help Center (Android tab): "You can send messages to yourself to keep track of notes and messages... From the Chats tab, tap [new chat]. Search for or select your name or phone number." The chat behaves like any other except "you can't make audio calls, video calls, mute notifications, block or report yourself, or see your last seen and online", and it can be pinned to the top of the chat list. Practical tap count once pinned: unlock + tap WhatsApp + tap pinned self-chat + press-and-hold mic = 3 taps plus a hold, from an unlocked phone. From a locked phone there is no path at all — no lock-screen entry, no hotword, no widget capture. So WhatsApp is slower than Gemini and slower than a Keep widget; its advantage is habit, not friction.

**Processing and AI**

THIS IS THE HEADLINE FINDING. WhatsApp voice-message transcripts are real, on-device, and DO NOT SUPPORT DUTCH. Verified first-hand from the Help Center, Android tab, both the English and Dutch renderings of the same article (faq.whatsapp.com/241617298315321), fetched 31 Aug 2026: "Supported languages — At this time, only English, Portuguese, Spanish, and Russian." The Dutch page says the same: "Ondersteunde talen — Op dit moment alleen Engels, Portugees, Spaans en Russisch." Three further verified constraints, each significant: (1) ON-DEVICE — "Voice message transcripts are generated on your device, and your personal voice messages remain protected by end-to-end encryption", matching the launch blog (blog.whatsapp.com/introducing-voice-message-transcripts, 21 Nov 2024): "Transcripts are generated on your device so that no one else, not even WhatsApp, can hear or read your personal messages." (2) RECIPIENT-SIDE ONLY — "Once enabled, the message recipient can see voice message transcripts. The sender can't see the transcripts." In a self-chat you are both, so whether your own voice note in your own self-chat gets a transcript is genuinely ambiguous from the documentation and UNVERIFIED — it needs a device test, and it matters a lot. (3) OFF BY DEFAULT and requires a language-pack download — "Voice message transcripts are disabled by default and must be enabled... Tap Set up now or Wait for Wi-Fi", and the feature "is only available on certain devices and may not be available to you yet". Failure modes are documented: "Transcript unavailable" occurs when "Your transcript language setting doesn't match the language of the voice message" or "The voice message language isn't supported" (faq.whatsapp.com/2014974829357105). Meta AI is separately available and DOES cover Dutch — the language list at faq.whatsapp.com/1303130641622232 includes Dutch, with the caveat "Meta AI and some experiences are currently only available in limited countries and might not be available to you yet."

**Data model**

There is no notes model. Entities are Chat and Message, ordered by timestamp, end-to-end encrypted. A self-note is a Message in a Chat whose only participant is you. There are no notes, no tasks, no labels, no folders, no due dates, no people links beyond the chat participant, no inbox/processed distinction — a message is read or unread, starred or not, pinned or not, and that is the whole state machine. Voice notes are audio blobs with an optional locally-generated transcript that is not searchable content in any structured sense. Crucially, E2E encryption means nothing server-side can ever process this content — WhatsApp cannot build our product inside the self-chat without breaking its own security model, which is a durable structural moat for anyone building outside it. A capture never becomes a task, project or person link; it just scrolls away. The independent critique (purposelabstudio.com, 18 Jun 2026, updated 9 Jul 2026) states the failure modes exactly: it is "a pile, not a system", "Everything lands in one endless scroll, in the order you sent it"; "WhatsApp search matches exact words. But you rarely remember the exact words"; "Nothing ever reminds you"; and voice notes "go to die" as "unsearchable audio, buried in the scroll, lost forever".

**Organisation and retrieval**

Exact-substring search, star a message, pin the chat. No dates, no structure, no reminders. Retrieval degrades to scrolling.

**Outputs and integrations**

None to speak of — forwarding to another app is the only export. A cottage industry has appeared to fix this: luckynote.io markets a "WhatsApp Message Yourself, Made Searchable" product (page dated 13 Jul 2026) accepting "links, screenshots, photos, voice notes, files, notes and tasks" with OCR, voice transcription, link summaries and reminders, pitched as "The habit is right. The chat is simply doing a job it was not designed to own." Its pricing and its actual WhatsApp connection mechanism are not stated on that page (UNVERIFIED). That such products exist in mid-2026 is direct evidence of demand for exactly our category.

**Strengths**

- Unbeatable habit and reach in BE/NL — 10B+ downloads, #1 free communication app in the Dutch store, and everyone's thumb already knows where the mic button is.
- Voice notes are trivially easy to record and are end-to-end encrypted.
- On-device transcription with a genuine privacy story where it works.
- Meta AI is available in Dutch as a device language, so an AI front-end inside WhatsApp is not language-blocked.

**Weaknesses**

- NO DUTCH TRANSCRIPTION. English, Portuguese, Spanish and Russian only, as of 31 Aug 2026. For a Flemish or Dutch user, a WhatsApp voice note to self is an opaque audio blob forever.
- Transcription is off by default, needs a language-pack download, and is device-gated.
- Documented as recipient-side only — the sender cannot see transcripts, which casts real doubt on self-chat transcription working at all.
- Zero structure: no notes, tasks, reminders, dates or organisation. The independent critique's four failure modes (pile, exact-word search, no reminders, dead voice notes) are all accurate against the documented model.
- No lock-screen or widget capture path — measurably slower than Gemini or a Keep widget.
- E2E encryption structurally prevents WhatsApp from ever adding server-side understanding to the self-chat.

**Ideas for Delta**

- This is the answer the previous study wanted: the WhatsApp self-chat is a large, real, high-frequency habit in our exact first market, and its voice notes are UNPROCESSED AND UNSEARCHABLE IN DUTCH. That is the sharpest wedge in this entire report.
- Positioning line, defensible and verifiable: "WhatsApp transcribes voice notes in English, Portuguese, Spanish and Russian. Not Dutch." Cite the Help Center; it is Meta's own page in Dutch.
- The habit to attack is 'voice note to self', not 'note-taking'. Frame onboarding as "you already do this — we make it findable", the same framing luckynote.io uses, which suggests the pitch converts.
- Verify on a real device whether a self-sent voice note in the self-chat gets a transcript. If it does not — likely, given "the sender can't see the transcripts" — the gap is even wider than the language list implies, and it is worth a screenshot in marketing.
- Do NOT try to build inside WhatsApp. E2E encryption means no legitimate read access to the self-chat, so any "forward to our bot" flow is manual, adds taps, and puts us at Meta's mercy. Our own capture surface is both faster and safer.
- Competitor watch: luckynote.io and similar are chasing this exact wedge in 2026. Speed matters more than feature breadth.

### OEM recorder apps — Google Recorder (Pixel) and Samsung Voice Recorder (Galaxy)

<https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.google.android.apps.recorder>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

The pre-installed long-form voice capture with transcription that ships on the two phone families that matter in BE/NL. This is the closest thing to a free version of our capture-and-transcribe primitive, and on Pixel it is genuinely good.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Google Recorder Play listing fetched first-hand 31 Aug 2026 (en_US/US): 3.3 stars, 15.8K reviews, updated 26 Aug 2026, Phone/Watch/Tablet tabs, Phone-only 3.3 / 15.6K. That 3.3 is the lowest rating of any surface in this report and the reviews say why: 23 Jul 2026 (52 helpful) — "The search function for transcripts is pretty much useless. The automatic transcription is severely flawed. You need a way to be able to identify speakers more reliably and permanently"; 23 Jul 2026 (6 helpful) — "Lack of ability to access the recording files is annoying... there's no easy way to access your own files other than sharing each recording and transcript individually"; and, directly relevant to us, 8 Jun 2026 (15 helpful) — "I don't want Recorder to be able to record in the background... every time I start a recording, I have to go through a pop-up warning me that background recording is blocked." Samsung Voice Recorder Play listing fetched first-hand from the NL store 31 Aug 2026: 3.9 stars, 293K reviews, 1B+ downloads.

**Platforms**

Pixel phones/tablets + Pixel Watch (Recorder) · Galaxy phones/tablets/watches (Samsung)

**Pricing**

Free, pre-installed on Pixel / Galaxy respectively.

**Capture UX**

Google Recorder: open app, tap record. Its Play listing states it is "Available on Wear OS with a dedicated watch face tile to quickly capture moments and thoughts on your phone or Pixel Watch" — a watch tile is the fastest documented capture on this list after the assistant. Samsung Voice Recorder Play listing (NL store): "Start recording quickly and easily from anywhere, including from your Galaxy smartphone, tablet, or watch", with General and Interview modes, bookmarks, and "Block calls while recording". Neither offers lock-screen capture.

**Processing and AI**

Google Recorder, verified from support.google.com/pixelphone/answer/16267698: real-time transcription on Pixel 6 and later covers exactly "Chinese (Mandarin), English (Australia), English (India), English (Ireland), English (Singapore), English (United Kingdom), English (United States), French, French (Canada), German, Hindi, Italian, Japanese, Spanish, Spanish (Latin America)" — DUTCH IS NOT IN THAT LIST. Dutch appears only in the re-transcribe list ("Dutch (Netherlands)", among 39+ languages), and "Re-transcribe may process audio files on Google servers", so the Dutch path is neither real-time nor on-device. Summaries run "in a secure, isolated environment in the cloud", are Pixel 8+, cover six languages (English, Mandarin, Hindi, Italian, French, German, Japanese — no Dutch), and handle "recordings from 5 minutes up to a maximum of 1 hour". Speaker labels are Pixel 6+. Storage: "Your recordings are only available on your Pixel phone or Pixel Tablet and not synced to your Google Account unless you choose to" (support.google.com/pixelphone/answer/16267696). Samsung: Galaxy AI Transcript Assist "stelt je in staat om transcripties, samenvattingen en vertalingen te maken met de Spraakrecorder en de Notities-app", with speaker recognition (samsung.com/nl support page). Dutch IS supported by Galaxy AI — Samsung's April 2024 press release listed 13 languages without Dutch and promised it "later this year"; galaxyclub.nl reports the Dutch language pack shipped 28 Oct 2024 as a ~345 MB download via Settings > Algemeen beheer > Taalpakketten, and lists Transcript Assist and Note Assist among the features that work in Dutch. So on a Galaxy, Dutch transcription is free and on-device-capable; on a Pixel, Dutch is cloud re-transcription only.

**Data model**

A Recording entity: audio file + transcript + optional summary + speaker labels + title, stored locally by default, optionally backed up. No notes, no tasks, no tags, no projects, no people, no inbox. Retrieval is by search over transcripts — Recorder lets you "search for words, phrases, or sounds like music or applause" and jump to "the exact time the word was spoken", which is a better retrieval story than Keep for long recordings. Export: audio file, transcript .txt, transcript to Google Docs, transcript to NotebookLM, video clips up to 5 minutes, or a share link via recorder.google.com. There is no mechanism by which a recording becomes a task, and no destination app integration.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Full-text transcript search with timestamp jump (Recorder); bookmarks (Samsung). Flat list of recordings otherwise.

**Outputs and integrations**

Recorder exports to Docs and NotebookLM — note that Google is quietly wiring voice capture into its AI note product. Samsung exports transcribed text to other apps.

**Strengths**

- Free, pre-installed, and on Pixel offers real transcript search with timestamp jump — better long-form retrieval than Keep.
- Recordings stay on-device by default; strong privacy default.
- Wear OS tile on Pixel Watch for fast capture.
- On Galaxy, Dutch transcription and summarisation genuinely work via Galaxy AI language packs (since 28 Oct 2024).

**Weaknesses**

- Google Recorder does not transcribe Dutch in real time — Dutch is re-transcribe-only and goes to Google servers, so the on-device privacy story evaporates precisely for our market's language.
- No summaries in Dutch on Pixel at all (six languages, none of them Dutch).
- 3.3 stars / 15.8K reviews: users report broken transcript search, unreliable speaker ID, and file-access friction.
- Pixel-only or Galaxy-only — neither is a cross-platform floor.
- No structure whatsoever: a flat list of recordings, no tasks, no projects, no inbox, no route from a recording to an action.

**Ideas for Delta**

- The Dutch gap is consistent across the free stack: Pixel Recorder real-time excludes Dutch, WhatsApp excludes Dutch. Only Samsung's Galaxy AI covers Dutch well. Our Dutch-first transcription quality is a defensible product claim, not marketing fluff — but it must actually be good, because Galaxy users already have a working Dutch alternative.
- Segment by device in any BE/NL user research: a Galaxy owner's free baseline is materially better than a Pixel owner's for Dutch. Do not treat 'Android user' as one persona.
- Copy the timestamp-jump transcript search. It is the one retrieval feature Recorder does well and the one users complain loudest about when it breaks.
- The 8 Jun 2026 review about the background-recording pop-up is a warning: Android forces a visible, nagging notification for background microphone use and users read it as an intrusion. Design our foreground-service notification as reassurance, not as an alarm.
- Recorder exporting to NotebookLM signals where Google is heading — voice capture feeding an AI notebook. Assume that convergence happens and make the inbox/triage layer, not the transcription, our defensible part.
