### Android platform constraints on background recording and battery — and what Expo can and cannot match

<https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/services/fgs/service-types>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

Not a competitor: the rulebook. This entry answers the explicit question of which parts of the Android quick-capture floor an Expo app can reach without native modules.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

Android 12/13/14/16 platform behaviour as documented on developer.android.com, fetched 31 Aug 2026. Expo SDK v57 docs fetched the same day. Note Android 17 exists in the wild per press coverage of the Assistant shutdown (howtogeek, 5 Aug 2026, mentions "Gemini Intelligence" on higher-end Android 17 devices) — its behaviour changes are UNVERIFIED and worth checking before shipping.

**Platforms**

Android

**Pricing**

n/a

**Capture UX**

What the platform permits, all verified from developer.android.com. Microphone capture from a service requires foregroundServiceType="microphone", the FOREGROUND_SERVICE and FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE manifest permissions and the RECORD_AUDIO runtime permission, and has been mandatory since Android 14 (API 34). The hard rule: "The RECORD_AUDIO runtime permission is subject to while-in-use restrictions. For this reason, you cannot create a microphone foreground service while your app is in the background and you cannot launch a microphone foreground service from a BOOT_COMPLETED receiver". On Android 12+ starting any FGS from the background throws ForegroundServiceStartNotAllowedException, and on Android 14+ even an exempt background start cannot hold a while-in-use permission. THE ESCAPE HATCHES THAT MATTER FOR US: the background-start exemption list includes "The user performs an action on a UI element related to your app. For example, they might interact with a bubble, notification, widget, or activity", and the separate while-in-use exemption list names "The service starts by interacting with app widgets" and "The service starts by interacting with a notification". Quick Settings tiles are NOT named in the while-in-use list — UNVERIFIED whether a tile tap qualifies; test before designing around it. Consequence: always-on ambient listening is not buildable; one-tap-from-widget recording is.

**Processing and AI**

Battery, verified from developer.android.com/training/monitoring-device-state/doze-standby: in Doze the system "Suspends network access", "Ignores wake locks", "Defers standard AlarmManager alarms, including setExact() and setWindow(), to the next maintenance window", and "Doesn't let JobScheduler run" — which by extension stops WorkManager, and therefore stops expo-background-task. Exemptions: setAndAllowWhileIdle() / setExactAndAllowWhileIdle() fire in Doze but "can[not] fire alarms more than once per nine minutes, per app"; setAlarmClock() works normally; high-priority FCM wakes the app. An app with a foreground service is not considered idle by App Standby, but Google warns "Don't start a foreground service just to prevent the system from determining that your app is idle." On top of AOSP, OEM battery managers are worse: dontkillmyapp.com states "Android manufacturers listed below prefer battery life over proper functionality of your apps" and ranks Huawei, Xiaomi, OnePlus and Samsung at the worst tier, with Google/Nokia/AOSP clean. Samsung and Xiaomi are heavily represented in BE/NL, so deferred upload and reminder delivery WILL be killed on real user phones unless we prompt for battery-optimisation exemption.

**Data model**

n/a — but two design consequences for ours. First, capture must be crash-safe from a cold, widget-initiated start: the capture row and the audio file must be durable before any network call. Second, background upload/transcription cannot be assumed to run — expo-background-task uses WorkManager with a documented "minimum 15 minutes" interval, runs "sometime after the interval has passed, provided the specified conditions are met", and the Expo docs themselves link to dontkillmyapp.com because "behavior varies by device vendor". Any state machine that assumes a background job completes is wrong on a Samsung.

**Organisation and retrieval**

n/a

**Outputs and integrations**

WHAT EXPO CAN MATCH WITHOUT WRITING NATIVE CODE (all with a development build, not Expo Go): foreground recording and, notably, BACKGROUND recording — expo-audio in SDK 57 exposes an `enableBackgroundRecording` config-plugin option that adds FOREGROUND_SERVICE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE and POST_NOTIFICATIONS and declares the audio-recording foreground service, with the docs stating "On Android, background recording requires a foreground service, which displays a persistent notification" reading "Recording audio" with a stop button, plus the warning "Background recording can significantly impact battery life." Also available: expo-notifications, expo-background-task / expo-background-fetch (≥15 min, deferrable, Doze-subject). Community packages with Expo config plugins, still no hand-written native code but a dev build and third-party risk: react-native-android-widget for real Android home-screen widgets (MIT, 892 GitHub stars, ships app.plugin.ts and an example-expo folder) and jamsch/expo-speech-recognition for on-device Android SpeechRecognizer with `requiresOnDeviceRecognition` and `androidTriggerOfflineModelDownload()` (672 stars, dev build required). WHAT EXPO CANNOT MATCH: (1) Android home-screen widgets are NOT in the Expo SDK — the SDK 57 "Widgets" library is iOS-only, "A library to build iOS home screen widgets and Live Activities using Expo UI components", so Android widgets require the community package or native code. (2) Quick Settings tiles — no Expo API, needs a native TileService. (3) The assistant slot: hotword, power-button long-press, corner swipe and lock-screen note creation are reserved for the system assistant; a third-party app cannot take them, in Expo or in native. (4) Wear OS — React Native/Expo has no Wear OS support, so watch-tile capture is off the table. (5) Always-on background listening — forbidden by the while-in-use rule regardless of framework.

**Strengths**

- Expo SDK 57 gives us background recording legitimately, via a documented config-plugin flag — a bigger capability than most people assume Expo has.
- Widget and notification taps are first-class exemptions from the microphone restrictions, so the one-tap capture we want is legal.
- On-device speech recognition is reachable through a maintained community package if we ever want offline or cheaper transcription.

**Weaknesses**

- No Android widget support in the Expo SDK itself — our single most important capture surface depends on a 892-star third-party package or on ejecting to native.
- No Quick Settings tile, no Wear OS, no assistant slot, ever.
- Doze plus OEM battery managers (Samsung and Xiaomi both worst-tier, both huge in BE/NL) will silently kill deferred uploads and delay reminders.
- Background recording forces a persistent notification users experience as nagging — see the 8 Jun 2026 Google Recorder review.
- expo-background-task cannot run more often than every 15 minutes and cannot be relied on at all.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Decide the Android widget question early and explicitly: adopt react-native-android-widget, or accept that our fastest capture is opening the app. Everything about our competitive position against Keep's one-tap widget hangs on this, and it is a config-plugin + dev-build decision, not a rewrite.
- Turn on expo-audio's enableBackgroundRecording only if we actually need capture to survive a screen-off — it costs a permanent notification and battery. For short thought-capture it may be the wrong trade.
- Never model the pipeline as 'record → background job transcribes later'. Upload opportunistically while the app is foreground, and treat any background completion as a bonus. Doze plus Samsung guarantees the pessimistic path is the common one.
- Prompt for battery-optimisation exemption at the right moment (after a missed reminder, not at onboarding) and use setExactAndAllowWhileIdle / setAlarmClock semantics for anything time-critical, respecting the once-per-nine-minutes cap.
- Write down in CLAUDE.md, as a product decision, which surfaces we are conceding: hotword, power button, lock screen, Wear OS. It prevents repeated re-litigation and stops us from planning features Android will not permit.
- Consider on-device recognition (expo-speech-recognition) as a cost lever and an offline story, but benchmark its Dutch quality against our cloud pipeline before promising anything — Android's on-device Dutch model is not the same thing as a good Dutch transcript.

### Turbo AI (formerly TurboLearn AI)

<https://www.turbo.ai/>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

"The fastest way to learn anything." A student cram tool, not a voice app. Audio is one of seven accepted inputs (text, documents, PDFs, slides, websites, videos, lecture recordings); the product is the study artifact factory bolted on the back. App Store subtitle is "AI notes, quizzes, lessons" (apps.apple.com id6502794561). Positions explicitly against ChatGPT and StudyFetch in its own blog. This is the most professionalised of the three and the only one with a real company behind it: TechCrunch (23 Oct 2025) names Rudy Arora and Sarthak Dhawan, both 20, childhood friends who dropped out of Duke and Northwestern in 2025, running a 15-person team in Los Angeles. Times of India (28 Jul 2026, via turbo.ai/blog/turbo-ai-on-times-of-india) headlines a "$13 million AI startup".

**Maturity and evidence of use**

iOS released 31 Jul 2024; Android released 5 Aug 2024 (Play metadata). Company founded 2023 as a Duke dorm-room project. Raised $750,000 in 2024 (TechCrunch, 23 Oct 2025); no valuation disclosed there, though Times of India (28 Jul 2026) headlines "$13 million". TechCrunch: "eight-figure annual recurring revenue", "cash-flow positive and have been profitable", "20,000 new users joining daily", 15-person LA team. Growth: 1M→5M users in six months to Oct 2025; 5M (blog, 16 Oct 2025) →10M (blog, 8 Jul 2026). Cumulative: "50+ million notes" and "1 billion+ flashcards, quizzes, and podcasts". RATINGS (verified 31 Aug 2026): iOS US 4.75 average on 33,334 ratings (iTunes lookup id6502794561), ranked #95 in Education (chartPosition badge in the App Store page JSON). Android 4.7 on 318,000 reviews with 1M+ installs — the underlying Play install integer is 2,884,174. Note the asymmetry: 318K Android reviews vs 33K iOS. The turbo.ai homepage claim of "4.8★ across 300k+ reviews" is therefore an almost entirely Android number presented as cross-platform. The homepage "10 million+ users" claim also sits against ~2.9M Android installs plus an unknown iOS base. Last iOS update 21 Aug 2026, release notes: "Bug fixes and improvements" — no meaningful public changelog. Legal entity is inconsistent across surfaces: App Store seller "TurboLearn LLC", terms of service "TurboLearn, Inc.", site copyright "© 2026 Turbolearn LLC".

**Platforms**

iOS (App Store id6502794561, v1.5.6, 21 Aug 2026, 106.6 MB, 4+, English only) · Android (Play: ai.turbolearn, listed as "Turbo AI - Notetaker", v not captured, updated 12 Aug 2026) · Web (turbo.ai) · iPad (revamp announced Oct 2025, status unverified) · Mac/Windows desktop (announced Oct 2025 as forthcoming, shipping status unverified)

**Pricing**

NO PUBLIC PRICING PAGE. https://www.turbo.ai/pricing returns HTTP 404 — verified. Price is only revealed at the in-app paywall. The full SKU ladder is visible on the App Store listing (apps.apple.com id6502794561, In-App Purchases block): Turbolearn Unlimited (1 Week) $9.99; (1 Month) $19.99 and $14.99; (1 Year) $119.99, $89.99, $69.99, $29.99 and $19.99; plus a SKU literally named "50% Off Forever Monthly" at $7.49. Google Play (ai.turbolearn) states the range as "$7.49 - $99.99 per item". Their own blog post best-studyfetch-alternatives quotes list price as "$20/month billed monthly, or $10/month billed annually ($120/year)" — which matches the $19.99 / $119.99 SKUs. The $29.99 and $19.99 annual SKUs and the "50% Off Forever Monthly" SKU are not list prices; they are cancellation-deflection and win-back offers, priced at a quarter of list. TechCrunch confirms "students pay about $20 a month" and that the founders are "experimenting with other pricing and running A/B tests". Terms of service (turbo.ai/terms-of-service, legal entity TurboLearn, Inc., 6287 Claridge Lane, Frisco, TX 75035) name three tiers Free / Pro / Unlimited and state: "All fees are non-refundable and non-creditable, including for partial periods, except where required by law", with a narrow exception for unresolvable technical malfunction claimed within 30 days. Cancellation must be at least 24 hours before period end. FREE-TIER LIMIT IS NOT PUBLISHED ANYWHERE — reviewers describe it as one note set (Laura Katherine Williams, v1.4.2, 1★: "Free tier limited to one note set before requiring paid subscription") and a daily note cap (Jeneudnsk, v1.4.2, 2★). Treat the exact number as unverified.

**Capture UX**

Capture is upload-first, record-second. The App Store description lists "Upload text, documents, PDFs, presentation slides, websites, videos, or lecture recordings". Recording a lecture is one supported path ("Record a lecture and let Turbo create clear, organized notes automatically"). Sync across app and website is claimed. No widget, lock-screen, watch, shortcut, or offline capture is mentioned anywhere in the description or on turbo.ai — unverified whether any exist. iOS app is 106.6 MB, English only (languageCodesISO2A = ["EN"], iTunes lookup) — no Dutch. Recording length limits are not published; a 1★ review (Daisypurple, v1.5.6) reports "Inaccurate and cuts recording" and another (WWJD Belle, v1.5.2, 3★) reports audio lost after a UI redesign. A v1.5.2 redesign moved the flow from automatic lecture notes to a chat-based interaction and generated a cluster of 1★ reviews demanding the old behaviour back (JossNY: "Bring Back Automatic Lecture Notes"; Seanpkelly: "They changed everything..it's useless now").

**Processing and AI**

Transcription and model vendors are NOT disclosed — unverified which ASR or LLM they use. The description claims notes "complete with key ideas, tables, diagrams, and equations", quizzes, flashcards, podcasts, summaries, and an AI chat scoped to the user's uploaded materials. "Learn Mode", shipped around the 10M-user announcement (Jul 2026), converts "as little as a few simple words into a structured course". Everything is cloud-side; no on-device claim. English only on iOS, so Dutch is effectively unsupported on the iPhone build. Quality complaints in reviews are specific and repeated: wrong quiz answers, notes that "pull from external sources" beyond the uploaded material (Warrier2019, v1.5.2), and missing chapters.

**Data model**

Inferred from the store description, the blog, and what reviewers complain about — I could not log in, so treat structure as evidence-backed inference, not confirmed schema. ENTITIES: (1) Source/Upload — text, document, PDF, slide deck, website URL, video, or lecture recording. (2) Note — the central addressable object, roughly equivalent to a session or a set. Reviews confirm it is the unit of both the free-tier quota ("one note set") and of failure ("this note does not exist" errors, Jnc123445689987262892, v1.5.1). (3) Derived artifacts, all hanging off a Note in a strict one-to-many fan-out: lesson, quiz, flashcard deck, podcast, summary, transcript. Blog counts confirm the fan-out ratio: 5M users produced "over 15 million notes" and "350 million+ flashcards" (Oct 2025), i.e. roughly 23 flashcards per note. (4) Course — introduced by Learn Mode, a generated sequence, apparently synthesised from a prompt rather than from stored captures. (5) Chat — scoped to uploaded materials, i.e. retrieval bounded by the Note/upload set. ORGANISATION: "Organized notes for every class and topic" implies a class/topic grouping, flat. NO INBOX, NO UNPROCESSED STATE, NO CONFIDENCE GATE. Generation is synchronous: you upload, it produces, it is filed. There is no triage step because there is nothing to triage — the AI is not asked to make a decision that could be wrong about WHERE something goes, only to transform content. NO ACTION/TASK ENTITY, NO STATUS, NO PEOPLE ENTITY, NO PROJECT ENTITY, NO REMINDERS. A capture never becomes a task or a person link; it becomes study material and stops. The organisational layer is weak enough that a 1★ reviewer (Reviei, v1.4.7) complains notes "lack dates/chronological ordering" and that "sorting problems make content unusable" — meaning the Note likely lacks reliable temporal or hierarchical metadata beyond a title. This is the sharpest structural contrast with Delta: Turbo AI has no concept of an entity that persists and accumulates across captures.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Class/topic grouping of notes; AI chat as the retrieval interface, scoped to uploaded materials. No search index, no tags, no backlinks, no cross-note retrieval described. Reviewer evidence indicates the sort/date layer is broken or absent. Sync across iOS app and web; desktop Mac/Windows apps and an iPad revamp were announced as forthcoming in Oct 2025 — shipping status unverified.

**Outputs and integrations**

Outputs: lessons, quizzes, flashcards, podcasts (audio), summaries, organised notes with tables/diagrams/equations. Turbo.ai homepage claims a "Google Docs-style editor for shared document editing" and a library of pre-made AP study guides. No calendar, task-manager, CRM, or note-app integrations found. No API found. Export formats not documented.

**Strengths**

- Real company with real economics: eight-figure ARR on $750K raised and a 15-person team is a genuinely efficient business, and it is profitable. This is the competitor to take seriously.
- Named, credible founders with earned media (TechCrunch, Business Insider, Times of India) — trust that the other two commodity apps cannot buy.
- Industrial SEO content operation. The blog is a keyword farm: "Best ChatGPT Alternatives for Studying", "Best StudyFetch Alternatives", "Turbo AI vs. ChatGPT", "Turbo AI vs. Studley", "Study for AP Calculus with Turbo AI", "Best Study Apps for ADHD Students in 2026". This produced 1.4M monthly visits (Similarweb, Jul 2026) with organic search as the second-largest channel.
- Keyword-loaded app name plus subtitle ("Turbo AI - Learn Faster" / "AI notes, quizzes, lessons") occupying App Store search real estate.
- Genuine breadth of input types — competing on "it eats anything" rather than on audio quality.

**Weaknesses**

- Ships breaking redesigns. The v1.5.2 update produced a wall of 1★ reviews reporting lost audio, a confusing chat-first flow replacing automatic lecture notes, and an accessibility regression for a vision-impaired user (Ch3rr1_c0la).
- Data model has no persistence layer worth the name. Notes reportedly lack dates and reliable ordering; nothing accumulates.
- Systematic billing complaints: unauthorised $89.99 charge (Laurien86, v1.4.8, 1★, the only review in the feed with a helpful vote); refund refused after non-use (Mustafa Bula, v1.5.6); "Be aware this Malicious software keeps you in subscribe payments" (BetterAi, v1.5.2).
- Hostage paywall. Bud907 (v1.4.7, 1★) recorded an hour-long lecture and was then asked for $20 to generate the notes, and missed the assignment deadline. This is the archetypal pattern of the tier.
- Free tier is effectively a demo, not a product — around one note set. Taeryns game (v1.5.6, 2★) reports being locked out by the note limit during finals.
- English only on iOS. No Dutch, no Flemish, no EU-language coverage on the phone build.
- No published price, no published free-tier limit, no real changelog, and three different legal-entity names across the site, the terms and the App Store.

**Ideas for Delta**

- Their blog is the highest-ROI thing they do and it costs almost nothing. A comparison-and-alternative content set in Dutch ("beste spraakmemo app", "Otter alternatief", "vergadernotities app Nederlands") is uncontested — none of these three publish a word of Dutch marketing.
- Their fan-out ratio (23 flashcards per note) is the whole business: one capture must yield many visible artifacts to feel worth $20/month. Delta's equivalent must be that one capture yields an action, a project link, a person link and a notification — the perceived output density has to be visible or the price feels absurd.
- Copy the SKU ladder mechanically, not ethically: a genuine annual discount and a win-back offer are fine; a hidden "50% Off Forever Monthly" that only appears in the cancel flow is the thing to refuse and then say publicly that you refuse.
- Their v1.5.2 disaster is the cautionary tale for Delta's confidence gate: they replaced an automatic flow with an interactive one and users revolted. If Delta's inbox makes the user do work in session 1, expect the same reviews.
- They have no persistent entity. Every claim Delta can make about projects, people and status is a claim Turbo AI structurally cannot answer without rebuilding.
- Publish a pricing page. Turbo.ai/pricing is a 404. In a category where the leader hides its price, a public price is a differentiator that costs nothing.

### Coconote

<https://coconote.app/>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

"Never take notes again." Lecture-recording-first study tool for students, with a meeting-minutes side door (App Store subtitle: "Lecture Recorder, Minutes"). Now owned by Quizlet — the App Store seller is "Quizlet Inc" (iTunes lookup id6479320349, artistId 546473128, the same artist ID family as Quizlet's own app) and the homepage footer reads "© 2026 Quizlet, Inc." The acquisition itself is undocumented publicly: a TechCrunch site search for "coconote" (techcrunch.com/?s=coconote) returns exactly one article, from Nov 2024, which predates it, and quizlet.com/press returns HTTP 403. Acquisition date and terms are UNVERIFIED — the only hard evidence is the store seller record and the site copyright. Tellingly, coconote.app/privacy still names "Coconote, LLC" as the entity and was last updated 24 December 2025 with no mention of Quizlet at all. The App Store description is not a description; it is a competitor keyword list, quoted in full: "Let AI take notes for you! Record lectures and automatically generate study guides, quizzes, and flashcards from lecture notes. Get the grade you want and deserve by using AI legally and ethically. Coconote is the more trusted and reliable alternative to Turbo AI, YouLearn, Unstuck, Study Fetch, Quizard, Otter, and Wave AI." That is the entire description.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

iOS released 15 Apr 2024 — one day before Voicenotes (16 Apr 2024), i.e. the same cohort and the same start line. Android came a full year later, 20 Apr 2025. Acquired by Quizlet at an unverified date; the App Store seller record now reads "Quizlet Inc" while the privacy policy (last updated 24 Dec 2025) still reads "Coconote, LLC", so the acquisition is recent enough that the legal documents have not caught up. RATINGS (verified 31 Aug 2026): iOS US 4.77 average on 16,544 ratings (iTunes lookup); NOT in the top 100 of Education — the App Store page carries no chartPosition badge, unlike Turbo AI's. Android 4.6 on 5,330 reviews, 500K+ installs (underlying integer 524,488). Web traffic is small: 200.7K monthly visits (Similarweb, Jul 2026), up 21.94% month-on-month, 50.1% organic search, with a bizarre country mix led by the Dominican Republic at 31.9% (over 5,000% growth) ahead of the US at 14.97% — which reads like a single viral influencer or a bought-traffic spike rather than organic demand. Ship cadence is fast: v2.50 shipped on 31 Aug 2026, the day I checked, and the changelog is a real one-line feature note rather than "bug fixes". Independent coverage: TechCrunch, 20 Nov 2024, Amanda Silberling, "'PDF to Brainrot' study tools are a strange iteration on a TikTok trend".

**Platforms**

iOS (App Store id6479320349, v2.50, released 15 Apr 2024, updated 31 Aug 2026, 89.9 MB, 4+, 33 languages incl. Dutch) · iPad · Apple Watch (claimed on homepage; function unverified) · Mac (native app, coconote.app/desktop) · Android (Play: app.coconote, released 20 Apr 2025, updated 22 Jul 2026) · Web (coconote.app)

**Pricing**

NO PUBLIC PRICING ANYWHERE. https://coconote.app/pricing returns HTTP 404 — verified. The homepage FAQ answers "Is Coconote free?" with only: "Yes, you can download and use Coconote for free to get started. For best results, we recommend upgrading to Unlimited Pass for unlimited notes, priority support, and added features." No number, no limit, no price. The CTA is "Try for $0", repeated across the page. The only prices that exist publicly are the App Store IAP records (apps.apple.com id6479320349), all named identically "Coconote Pro": $9.99, $9.99, $19.99, $19.99, $19.99, $71.99, $99.99, $129.99, $129.99, $152.99. Google Play (app.coconote) states "$2.49 - $189.99 per item" — a wider ladder than iOS, bottoming at $2.49. Because every SKU carries the same name, the billing period of each price is not inferable from the store — UNVERIFIED which are weekly, monthly, annual or multi-year. The $99.99–$152.99 SKUs are corroborated by a 1★ review reporting a $100 charge. FREE-TIER LIMIT UNPUBLISHED; a 1★ reviewer ("Barely can use free", v2.46) reports it as roughly two notes: "I took maybe two notes with Coconote and after that it stopped working and it started forcing me to pay." A 3★ reviewer (CRISTINAZV, v2.46) reports the trial itself is capped: "if your in trial you can't study more it has like a limit and you have to obligatorily pay for the anual planner." Terms of service are served as an 819 KB PDF at https://assets.coconote.app/tos.pdf (coconote.app/tos 308-redirects to it) — I could not extract its text, so the refund and auto-renewal clauses are UNVERIFIED.

**Capture UX**

Record-first, with broad upload. Homepage lists inputs as "lectures, PDFs, documents, videos, links" and "short-form video". Cross-device sync is claimed across "iPhone, iPad, Android, Web, Mac, Apple Watch" — the Apple Watch claim is notable and is the only watch capture surface among the three apps here, though I could not verify what the watch app actually does. No widget, lock-screen, Shortcuts, or offline capture is documented — unverified. Recording length is a real weak point: a 1★ reviewer ("App is bad", v2.46) reports that on lectures over one hour "the app doesn't register about 45% of my lecture" and the note generator "will only write the notes on the 60 minutes and not the entire other half" — suggesting a ~60-minute processing ceiling. 33 languages including Dutch (iTunes languageCodesISO2A includes NL). iOS app 89.9 MB.

**Processing and AI**

Transcription and LLM vendors NOT disclosed — unverified. The privacy policy names no sub-processors at all: no OpenAI, no Deepgram, no AssemblyAI, only "third-party payment processors" and unnamed "online analytics providers". It also does not state whether audio recordings are used for AI training. 100+ languages claimed on the homepage; 33 declared on the App Store, Dutch among them. All cloud; no on-device claim. Known quality gap: mathematical symbol transcription fails on calculus content ("Math missprint", v2.46, 1★).

**Data model**

Inferred from description, FAQ, and reviews; not confirmed by logged-in inspection. ENTITIES: (1) Source — lecture recording, uploaded PDF, document, video, web link, or short-form video. (2) Note — the central object and the free-tier quota unit ("unlimited notes" is literally what Unlimited Pass sells, so the Note is the metered entity). (3) Derived artifacts fanned out from a Note: transcript, study guide / note outline, quiz, flashcard deck, study game, AI podcast, and — as of v2.50 (31 Aug 2026) — slides. The changelog entry for v2.50 is exactly "You can now generate slides from your notes", confirming artifacts are generated FROM the note, i.e. the Note is the parent and everything else is a child projection. (4) Folder — a flat container; a 4★ reviewer (Superfrwgalisticexpelaladoshis) praises "setup/folders". (5) Chat — scoped to a single note. A 3★ reviewer (Safish, 04 Oct 2025) explicitly requests "cross-note chat functionality", which confirms retrieval today is note-bounded with no corpus-wide index. NO INBOX, NO UNPROCESSED OR TRIAGE STATE. NO TASK/ACTION ENTITY, NO STATUS FIELD, NO PROJECT ENTITY, NO PEOPLE ENTITY, NO REMINDERS. A capture becomes study material and terminates there; it never becomes a task, never links to a person, never joins a project. There is no equivalent of Delta's suggested_* columns because there is no decision for the AI to make — only a transformation. Deletion is destructive with no recovery: a 3★ reviewer (lover 303, v2.46) asks for a way to recover accidentally deleted notes, and a 1★ reviewer ("Terrible", v2.46) reports "everything was deleted" after an error. Notably the closest thing to structure in the whole product is the folder, and it arrived before any of the study-game features — organisation is an afterthought bolted to a generator.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Flat folders. Per-note AI chat. No cross-note chat (explicitly requested by users and absent). No tags, no search index documented, no backlinks. "Monthly reporting feature" mentioned by one reviewer (Penny ixxl, v2.46) in a tutoring context — unverified what this is.

**Outputs and integrations**

Notes, transcripts, study guides, quizzes, flashcards, study games, AI podcasts, and slides (new in v2.50). No calendar, task, or CRM integrations found. No API found. Export formats not documented. Desktop Mac app and web app exist (coconote.app/desktop).

**Strengths**

- Quizlet ownership: distribution, brand cover, and a parent with 1.1M ratings on its own app to cross-promote from.
- Real ship cadence with a substantive changelog — v2.50 added slide generation, and the store entry says so plainly. Neither of the other two does this.
- Breadth of languages (33 declared, 100+ claimed) including Dutch — genuinely broader than Turbo AI's English-only iOS build.
- Widest surface coverage of the three: iPhone, iPad, Watch, Mac, Android, web.
- The App Store description is a masterclass in cynical ASO: it names seven competitors by brand so Coconote surfaces on searches for each of them, and wraps it in "legally and ethically" to catch academic-integrity anxiety. Whether or not it is admirable, it works.
- Free-to-download with a $2.49 entry SKU on Android — the lowest entry price in this whole comparison.

**Weaknesses**

- A ~60-minute processing ceiling that silently drops the back half of long lectures, reported by a reviewer as losing 45% of the content. For a lecture-recording app this is a core-function failure.
- Destructive deletion with no recovery path, plus at least one report of total data loss after an error.
- The worst-documented billing in this set. One 1★ review, verbatim: "First off, this app does not work as advertised, so I cancelled before the end of the free trial. I was charged, told it was actually a shorter trial than advertised (was in the fine print but said 7 days on the banner 😡. Now 6 months later I was charged $100 out of the blue. I'm not showing any active subscription". Another: cancelled an annual plan, told access runs to 12 Dec, "but conveniently it doesn't recognize me and customer service will not respond to my email."
- Ten identically-named "Coconote Pro" SKUs from $9.99 to $152.99 with no published mapping to billing periods. This is not an accident; it makes the price un-comparable before purchase.
- Terms of service shipped as an 819 KB PDF — unreadable on a phone, unsearchable, and unlinkable to a specific clause.
- Privacy policy names zero sub-processors and is silent on whether audio trains models — for EU buyers this is not a policy, it is a placeholder.
- Aggressive in-app review prompting, called out by a 3★ reviewer verbatim: "pressured reviews when I haven't had the chance to even really use the product at all yet."
- Undisclosed influencer marketing on TikTok, documented by TechCrunch: the account @aliistudies posted a video claiming a teacher had assigned Coconote's "PDF to Brainrot" tool; it drew 1 million views and "appears to be undisclosed Coconote sponcon".
- Web presence is negligible relative to installs — 200K visits versus 524K Android installs means the App Store is doing essentially all the work.

**Ideas for Delta**

- The competitor-naming description is the single cheapest ASO tactic available and it is legal. Delta's Dutch listing can name the tools Flemish professionals actually search for. It is also a tactic that erodes trust with a professional buyer — decide deliberately which side of that you want to be on.
- Their Note is metered and their Unlimited Pass sells "unlimited notes". Delta must NOT meter captures — metering the capture is exactly what turns a thinking tool into a cram tool. Meter something the user does not do in a panic.
- Cross-note chat is the top unmet request in their reviews and they cannot ship it, because their model has no corpus, only isolated notes. Delta's project/person entities are precisely the index that makes cross-capture retrieval possible. That is the feature to lead with.
- The 60-minute silent truncation is a warning: long-audio handling is where these products break, and it is where a careful implementation buys credibility with professionals.
- Publish a real HTML terms page and a named sub-processor list. Coconote ships a PDF and names nobody. For a Belgian seller under GDPR this is free differentiation.
- Their Dominican Republic traffic spike shows how volatile influencer-driven acquisition is: 5,000% growth in one country in one month is a channel you do not control and cannot repeat.

### Voice Recorder: AI Note Taker (Hangzhou Suoyi Network Technology / "Voice Inc." / MagicTool)

<https://apps.apple.com/us/app/voice-recorder-ai-note-taker/id6503227362>

_Added in wave 2, to close a gap the completeness critic identified._

**Positioning**

There is no positioning. There is no marketing site, no landing page, no blog, no changelog, no press, no founder, no brand. The iTunes record has an empty sellerUrl. The product is a search result: the app is NAMED after the query it wants to win — "Voice Recorder: AI Note Taker" — with the subtitle "Transcribe, Summary & AI Memos" packing three more keywords in. On Android the very same product is named something different again, "Voice Memos: AI Note Recorder" (package net.magictool.voicememos), because the winning keyword string differs by store. The App Store seller is "Hangzhou Suoyi Network Technology Co., Ltd." while the displayed developer name is the deliberately generic "Voice Inc." This is not a product company. It is an app factory: the same artistId (1463498615) carries twelve unrelated iOS apps — Heart Rate Monitor: Pulse (6,950 ratings), Followers for Instagram Report, Massager Phone App, Scanner App-PDF Documents Scan, AI Chatbot - Chat Cat, AI Song Generator: Magic Music, AI Garden Design: Makeover, Voice Translator - Travel, Water Tracker-Dolphin Reminder, Photo Widget: Magic, For Me - Daily quotes. One monetisation shell, ported to whatever category is hot. The voice recorder is their 2024 shot at the AI wave, and it is now their biggest app by ratings.

**Maturity and evidence of use**

iOS released 16 Jun 2024, two months after Coconote and Voicenotes. RATINGS (verified 31 Aug 2026): iOS US 4.66 average on 7,517 ratings — which is MORE than Voicenotes' 6,719 US ratings (iTunes lookup id6483293628, Coping Hard Inc., released 16 Apr 2024), the comparison that prompted this teardown. NOT charting: the App Store page carries no chartPosition badge. Android is far weaker: 4.4 on 50K+ installs (underlying integer 99,689) — so the iOS ratings are doing all the work, and the ratings-per-install ratio on iOS must be extraordinary. iOS was last updated 18 May 2026 — over three months stale as of 31 Aug 2026 — while Android shipped 26 Aug 2026. The corporate site magictool.net is titled, in Chinese, "Hangzhou Suoyi Network Technology Co., Ltd overseas APP official website", carries the Chinese ICP filing 浙ICP备17007501号-8, is copyright "© 2017-2022", and lists only Pulse Heart Rate Monitor, Vibrator Phone Massager and Scanner App — the voice recorder, their most successful product, is not listed on their own website at all. Similarweb reports NO MEASURABLE TRAFFIC for magictool.net (Jul 2026, "No Data Available" across all metrics). Developer contact is a Gmail address, hzsuoyi999@gmail.com. NO INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THIS APP EXISTS that I could find — no press, no blog coverage, no hands-on. That absence is itself the finding: 7,517 US ratings and not one journalist has ever written about it.

**Platforms**

iOS (App Store id6503227362, v1.7.3, released 16 Jun 2024, last updated 18 May 2026, 197.4 MB, Utilities + Productivity, 4+, 15 languages incl. Dutch first) · Android, under a different name — "Voice Memos: AI Note Recorder", package net.magictool.voicememos, released 30 Jun 2024, updated 26 Aug 2026 · No web app, no Mac app, no iPad-specific or Watch app documented

**Pricing**

Weekly-first, and the SKU names give away the entire funnel because the developer never bothered to rename them for public display. From the App Store listing (apps.apple.com id6503227362): "Premium_Weekly_Free_3D" $6.99 — the "_Free_3D" suffix is a 3-day free trial; "Premium_Weekly" $7.99 and $6.99; "Premium_Monthly_Free_3D" $16.99; "Premium Monthly" $16.99; "Premium_Yearly" $69.99; "Premium Yearly" $69.99; "Premium_Yearly_Discount" $39.99; "Annual Pro Plan" $79.99. Google Play states "$6.99 - $69.99 per item". THE WEEKLY IS THE HEADLINE PRICE AND IT IS THE MOST EXPENSIVE THING HERE: $6.99/week is $363.48 a year — five times the $69.99 annual SKU and roughly 3.4x Voicenotes Pro at $9/month. "Premium_Yearly_Discount" at $39.99 is a cancellation-deflection SKU, priced at 57% of the $69.99 list. FREE-TIER LIMIT: three recordings per day, per a 1★ reviewer (Shywolf2, v1.6.3): "They make you pay for more than 3 per day? and have barely any new features." Another (JustALuffyFan, v1.5.5): "Limits free recordings to 3; requires payment for unlimited access." Not published anywhere by the developer — UNVERIFIED as an exact figure, but corroborated across two independent reviews.

**Capture UX**

Record, stop, get transcript and summary. "Record, transcribe, and summarize in one easy step." Auto-naming of recordings is claimed. Audio import from uploaded video is supported. Trim, cut and reverse editing. Playback speed control. No widget, lock-screen, watch, or Shortcuts capture documented — unverified. The description claims Whisper runs on-device "for on-device mobile offline privacy and offline use", which is the only offline claim among the three apps, though the same sentence also routes work to Deepgram Nova-3 and three cloud LLMs, so what is actually offline is unclear. Fifteen declared languages with DUTCH LISTED FIRST in the iTunes languageCodesISO2A array (NL, EN, FR, DE, HU, IT, JA, KO, PL, PT, ZH, ES, SV, ZH, TR) — this app already targets the Dutch market. iOS app is 197.4 MB, more than double Coconote's, consistent with a bundled on-device model. RECORDING RELIABILITY IS THE HEADLINE COMPLAINT: recordings cut off mid-meeting (Colton from Austin, v1.5.2, 2★), the app stops recording after minutes when idle (Dsms83, v1.7.1, 2★: "Useless"), transcription hangs at 70% (Loli SF, v1.6.1, 1★), records but will not play back (ghem1146, v1.6.3, 1★), and generation fails with "retry" errors and no in-app support channel (Pop's Grandma412, v1.6.1).

**Processing and AI**

The most explicitly disclosed stack of the three, and it names everything: transcription via "Whisper for on-device mobile offline privacy and offline use, ASR for real-time transcription, and Nova-3 for fast, multi-language transcription"; note generation via "Google Gemini 3.1 Flash for long-text processing", "OpenAI GPT-5 for general tasks", and "DeepSeek V4.0 for Chinese-specific scenarios". The v1.7.3 changelog confirms: "Upgraded transcription engine to Deepgram Nova3, now recognizing more languages in automatic mode - Note features updated with Gemini 3.1 and DeepSeek v4.0 for smarter insights." Automatic language detection. Dutch supported. CRITICAL FINDING ON PRIVACY: the linked privacy policy (magictool.net/voicememos/protocol/privacy.html) is for a differently-named app, "Voice Memos - Recorder Audio", and its text is copy-pasted boilerplate from a DATING APP. Verbatim excerpts from the live page: it collects "matches, numbers of matches made by members, match durations"; "in case your conversation partner reports your abusive behavior or language to us, then the conversation information such as voices, screenshots or contents of conversation which have been stored only on your partner's device may be transferred to our servers"; it requests Facebook login and address-book access. It contains NOTHING about audio recordings, transcription, AI processing, model training, or any of Deepgram, OpenAI, Google or DeepSeek — despite the store listing naming all four. So the app ships user audio to at least four third-party AI vendors including a Chinese one, under a privacy policy that never mentions audio and was written for a different product in a different category.

**Data model**

The flattest model of the three, and the closest of the three to a raw voice app. Inferred from the App Store description and reviews. ENTITIES: (1) Recording — the ROOT entity, and unlike Turbo AI and Coconote the audio file itself is primary, not a source that gets consumed. Attributes: auto-generated name ("Automatically manage and name your recordings"), duration, and an audio format (MP3, M4A, WAV are all offered on export). It is editable as audio — trim, cut, reverse — which confirms the file persists as a first-class object rather than being discarded after transcription. (2) Transcript — one per Recording, editable status disputed (drivenbysarah, v1.5.1, 2★: "Transcription filled with errors; no text editing capability available" — so transcripts are READ-ONLY, a real structural limitation). (3) AI Note / summary — generated from the transcript. (4) Derived text projections off the AI Note: to-do lists, highlights, and drafted emails ("Draft polished emails from audio content"). NOTE: the to-do list is TEXT INSIDE A NOTE, not a task entity — there is no status, no due date, no completion, nothing to query across recordings. This is the important distinction for Delta: the commodity tier already claims "turn voice notes into organized to-do lists", but it is a rendering, not a record. (5) Folder — flat, user-created, with create / rename / move operations. That is the entire organisational system. NO INBOX, NO UNPROCESSED STATE, NO CONFIDENCE OR TRIAGE CONCEPT. NO PROJECT, NO PERSON, NO ROLE, NO REMINDER, NO NOTIFICATION, NO STATUS FIELD, NO LINKS BETWEEN RECORDINGS. A capture never becomes anything with a lifecycle; it becomes a file in a folder with some generated text attached. Retrieval is browsing folders. Sharing is exporting a file or a link. Nothing accumulates, nothing is queryable, and after fifty recordings the user has fifty files in some folders.

**Organisation and retrieval**

Custom folders only — create, rename, move recordings between them. No tags, no search index documented, no cross-recording chat or retrieval, no linking. Auto-naming of recordings is the only automatic organisation. This is a 2010-era file manager with an LLM stapled on.

**Outputs and integrations**

Export audio as MP3, M4A or WAV; share transcribed text; shareable links. Generated to-do lists, highlights and drafted emails as text. No calendar, task-manager, CRM or note-app integrations. No API. No web app, no desktop app.

**Strengths**

- It ranks. That is the entire strength, and it is sufficient: an app with no site, no brand, a stale build and a dating-app privacy policy has more US ratings than the well-designed category leader.
- Keyword-perfect naming on both stores, with different names per store tuned to each store's search behaviour.
- Dutch first in the language list — this anonymous operator is already indexed for Dutch-language search while the two better-funded competitors are not prioritising it.
- Discloses its AI stack in the store listing more openly than either funded competitor does anywhere: Whisper, Deepgram Nova-3, Gemini 3.1 Flash, GPT-5, DeepSeek V4.0.
- The audio file stays a first-class object with real editing (trim, cut, playback speed) — Turbo AI and Coconote treat audio as disposable input, and some users genuinely want the recording back.
- A portfolio operator can afford to be wrong: eleven other apps subsidise the experiment, so there is no pressure to make this one good, only to make it convert.

**Weaknesses**

- Core function is unreliable. Recordings cut off, recordings will not play back, transcription hangs at 70%, generation returns "retry" with no support channel. For a recorder, these are not bugs, they are the product failing.
- Transcripts are not editable — a fatal flaw for professional use, where the whole value is a correctable record.
- $6.99/week = $363/year, presented as the default. That is more than five times its own annual SKU and the most expensive pricing in this entire teardown.
- Three free recordings per day, which means a paywall encounter within the first session for anyone with real work.
- Cancellation is reportedly broken (Jenbrou, v1.5.1: "Can't unsubscribe"), charges disputed as unauthorised (Milonsgsmejeheb, v1.5.1: "I didn't authorize the purchase"), and one user reports being charged after cancelling (ADK68, v1.5.5).
- No in-app support. Users resort to leaving App Store reviews as a help desk — see extraNotes; this is the mechanic that inflates the rating count.
- The privacy policy is a copy-pasted dating-app document that never mentions audio, while the app ships user speech to four third-party AI vendors across at least two jurisdictions. Under GDPR this is not a grey area.
- iOS build three months stale; corporate site last updated 2022 and does not list the app; contactable only at a Gmail address.
- Its own operator does not care about it: it is one of twelve unrelated apps spanning heart-rate monitoring, Instagram follower analytics and a phone vibrator.

**Ideas for Delta**

- This app is the honest picture of the price floor and it is NOT low. The floor is not free — it is a $6.99 weekly SKU sold to someone who will not do the arithmetic. Delta at €10/month is CHEAPER than the commodity tier's default plan. Say that out loud in the marketing.
- The SKU names leak the funnel ("Premium_Weekly_Free_3D", "Premium_Yearly_Discount"). Reading competitors' raw IAP identifiers is a free intelligence channel — do this for every competitor.
- Dutch is already contested at the commodity tier but not at the quality tier. Nobody is selling a well-built Dutch voice tool with a real privacy story. That is the gap.
- EU hosting, named sub-processors, an explicit "your audio never trains a model" line, and a real DPA are claims none of these three can make and, more importantly, none of them will bother to make — their buyer is panicking at 11pm, not running a compliance check.
- Their "to-do lists from voice notes" is text in a note with no status and no lifecycle. Delta's action entity with action_type, status and project_id is the same promise actually implemented. The pitch writes itself: everyone generates a to-do list; only Delta remembers it next week.
- Editable transcripts. It is a small feature and its absence is a 2★ review generator for a competitor with 7,500 ratings.
- Do not build a folder system and call it structure. Folders are what this app has, and folders are why nothing accumulates.
