## Cross-cutting patterns

From the completeness critic, which read the whole study and was asked what recurs across competitors and what nobody has solved. Reproduced in full.

1. TABLE STAKES — ONE-PRESS CAPTURE FROM A LOCKED DEVICE. Cleft (Lock Screen widget, Action Button, Live Activity, CarPlay, standalone Watch), TwinMind (widget tap or Action Button without unlocking), Tana (lock-screen, home-screen and Control Center widgets pre-bound to a destination), Todoist (Lock Screen Add Task), Drafts (Lock Screen widget plus Control Center) and Apple itself (one Action Button press) all ship it. Voicenotes — the category leader by ratings — does not, and requires the user to hand-build a Shortcut. This is now an expectation, not a differentiator: shipping it wins nothing, omitting it loses the 'zero friction' claim outright. For Delta on Expo it requires a native WidgetKit/AppIntents extension, so it is a build decision that must be made before the positioning is committed to.

2. TABLE STAKES — POST-CAPTURE STRUCTURING THAT IS EDITABLE AND RE-RUNNABLE. Every serious product generates a title plus a summary plus extracted actions automatically, and the good ones let you edit the transcript and regenerate downstream from the EDITED TEXT rather than from the audio (Cleft's transcript-edit-then-rewrite, Voicenotes' Fix, TwinMind's edit-notes-then-regenerate-summary). This is cheap for us because the understand step is already a pure function of transcript text. Products lacking a correction ritual (mymind's wrong tags, Voicenotes' hallucinated to-dos) generate the angriest reviews in the entire dataset.

3. TABLE STAKES SINCE ROUGHLY MID-2025 — AN MCP SERVER. Voicenotes (Mar 2026), Tana, TwinMind, Otter, Capacities, Todoist, TickTick and SpeakApp all shipped one inside about twelve months; Cleft has webhooks on its roadmap. Notably every one of them is READ-ONLY or read-mostly. This has become the default answer to 'my thoughts are trapped in one app' and is now expected rather than impressive.

4. NEARLY UNIVERSAL — FLAT TAGS, NOT FOLDERS, AND FILING DELEGATED OUT. Voicenotes (tags only, explicitly no folders), Cleft (three tags, no folders, no projects), Drafts (flat tags with a slash convention), Bear, mymind, flomo. And almost everyone routes actionable output to an external task manager rather than modelling tasks: Cleft to 13 destinations via Zapier, Capacities to six task managers, Drafts to 22 services, Braintoss to email, Voicenotes to Todoist/Things. The consistent conclusion across independent teams is that owning the destination is a losing position.

5. NOBODY HAS SOLVED DURABLE CAPTURE, AND IT IS THE #1 SOURCE OF ONE-STAR REVIEWS ACROSS THE WHOLE CATEGORY. Voicenotes admits in its own docs that unsynced audio is unrecoverable and asks users to babysit the app for 30-60 seconds. Braintoss has a decade of undelivered-email complaints and its v10 rebuild introduced new silent failures. Cleft ships releases titled 'Rock-Solid Reliability' and 'Built to Last' and still has one-star reviews about lost recordings. Apple's own reviewers report recordings stopping on screen lock and incoming calls. This is the clearest unoccupied ground in the category and it is an engineering problem, not an AI problem: write audio to local storage before any network call, upload via a retry queue with visible per-capture state, and never let the understand step block the save. Everything else in the study is a preference; this one is a defect nobody has fixed.

6. NOBODY HAS SOLVED THE PAYBACK PROBLEM: STRUCTURE WITHOUT RETRIEVAL CHURNS. This is the single most repeated failure pattern. Tana's schema-first outliner churned so badly the company built a second product and exiled the original to a subdomain. Mem needed a ground-up rewrite. Capacities' learning curve is its top complaint. Whisper Memos testers praise its speed then abandon it as 'disconnected'. Voicenotes has no project container at all and its users say notes become an unmanageable pile. Conversely mymind's testimonials are all about FINDING, and its headline 2026 release was making search fast. The lesson is blunt: users pay for the thought coming back, not for it being filed correctly. Filing accuracy is an input to retrieval, not the product.

7. NOBODY SHIPS REAL RESURFACING. Apple has time and location triggers on things you already knew were tasks; mymind has a shuffle mode; TwinMind has a daily digest; flomo has a review loop (and is the largest of them). Otherwise the entire category is capture-and-search, and no studied product brings an old, unactioned capture back to you on its own. Given that 'recorded it, never opened it again' is the stated demand-side pain in almost every review corpus in the study, this is the widest genuinely open space — and it is cheap to build on Supabase.

8. THE INDUSTRY HAS CONVERGED ON A PROPOSAL GATE, AND THE CONVERGENCE IS RECENT AND DELIBERATE. Tana replaced un-disableable autofill with a 'For Review' queue where AI items wait as proposals until accepted, and bet the company on it. Capacities requires user approval for every AI create/update/delete and restricts auto-tagging to tags that already exist. Todoist shows the parse inline and editable before commit. Cleft gates automation behind a human-applied tag as 'a human review checkpoint before automation runs'. Against that, TwinMind — which reached almost exactly Delta's entity model of people/places/projects/to-dos from ambient input — has NO gate, no inbox and no review state, and cannot even document whether an auto-generated Person page can be corrected. Delta's confidence gate plus Inbox is therefore the design the market is converging on, which is validating; the open question the study half-asks is whether our AUTO-FILE band should exist at all at launch, since Tana proposes everything and accepts nothing silently.

9. THE STRONGEST SINGLE INTERACTION PATTERN IN THE DATASET IS PRE-COMMIT CORRECTION, AND IT ARGUES AGAINST A PURE POST-HOC INBOX. Todoist highlights the parse inline while the user is still looking at the input, and reverting a token costs one tap; Fantastical does the same for events; Ramble takes voice corrections mid-stream. Todoist's own AI fills every cheap field pre-commit and defers exactly ONE thing — the project — to the Inbox. The Inbox there is a null-pointer parking lot for a single unresolved foreign key, not a review queue for the whole interpretation. That is a materially better design than parking the entire capture, and it is the highest-value borrowable idea in the study.

10. NOBODY DOES MULTI-ENTITY FAN-OUT FROM ONE UTTERANCE, WHICH IS THE ACTUAL DIFFERENTIATION. Apple's three capture apps are disconnected silos where nothing can become anything else. Voicenotes has no project, person or task entity. Todoist has no note, person or meeting entity. Cleft has no task, person or project entity by design. Drafts is text plus tags plus a three-value folder enum. mymind has one entity. Capacities and Tana have the model but require the user to build and declare it. TwinMind has the model but no consent, no gate and no correction path. So one ninety-second ramble producing a note plus two actions plus a project link plus two people — all approved, all reversible, all findable — is not shipped by anyone. That, and not transcription or summarisation, is the demo.

11. MULTI-LANGUAGE IS A REAL, DOCUMENTED HOLE IN DELTA'S HOME MARKET AND ALMOST NOBODY IS EXPLOITING IT. Apple's Notes audio transcription plus summary covers 26 locales but only 8 base languages, and Dutch is deliberately excluded even though Dutch is supported for other Apple Intelligence features; Voice Memos transcribes 'select English variants' only; iOS 27's Siri AI is announced English-first and not initially available in the EU. Google Recorder is Pixel-only and weak on code-switching. This is the one place where the free preinstalled incumbent is not merely worse but non-functional for the target user, it is checkable against Apple's own feature-availability table, and it has a dated window. It is worth more than any feature in the study.

12. CROSS-PLATFORM PARITY IS AN UNCONTESTED WEDGE. Cleft, Drafts, Things, NotePlan, Bear and Just Press Record are Apple-only. Tana Outliner is 4.02 on iOS and 2.9 on Android with a three-month-stale release, and new Tana has no native mobile at all. Capacities' top complaint is desktop/mobile feature inconsistency. Voicenotes is 4.77 on iOS and 3.9 on Android. Delta on Expo gets near-parity almost free, and every serious competitor is visibly failing at it — but the same choice is what makes the one-press native capture affordance expensive for us, and those two facts have to be traded off explicitly rather than assumed away.

13. THE CATEGORY LEADER IS VACATING THE POSITION WE WANT. Voicenotes earned 6,719 ratings at 4.77 on personal thought-capture and has repointed its homepage at botless meeting notes, where Granola already sits at 12,532 ratings and 4.96. Tana did the same thing at company scale. The personal raw-thought loop is being abandoned by the two teams best placed to own it, in favour of the lane with clearer enterprise revenue. That is simultaneously the opportunity (the position is opening) and the warning (two funded teams with better data than ours concluded the personal loop does not pay), and the study reports the first half without ever confronting the second.

14. SEGMENT-SPECIFIC POSITIONING BEATS GENERAL 'CAPTURE YOUR THOUGHTS', AND THE EVIDENCE IS UNAMBIGUOUS. The largest verified user bases in voice-to-structure are student-specific (Turbo AI 33,334 ratings; Coconote 16,544 under Quizlet). The most engaged small base is explicitly neurodivergent (Cleft, whose App Store title is literally 'For Verbal Thinkers'). Twos markets to ADHD. Saner is ADHD-first. mymind runs a dedicated ADHD landing page. Meanwhile the generic quick-capture inboxes plateau — Twos at 25,000 users after five years, Braintoss at ~183 iOS ratings after thirteen. 'Speak the thought, it gets structured and stays findable' is a mechanism, not a market; the products that grow name a person.

## Corrections and unverified claims

The adversarial critic was asked to attack the study. These are the claims it found unverified, internally contradictory or wrong. **Read this before quoting any number above.**

1. DIRECT INTERNAL CONTRADICTION ON BRAINTOSS, AND THE RANKED LANDSCAPE IS THE WRONG ONE. The landscape says 'Verified this session on the live App Store listing: v10.0.3, last updated 22 June 2024... so the claimed May 2026 native rebuild circulating in secondary sources is wrong' and builds a 'visibly declining' narrative on it. The teardown says the opposite: v10.0.3 released 2026-06-22 and 'the 2026 native rebuild claim is VERIFIED'. I checked the iTunes lookup API this session: v10.0.3, currentVersionReleaseDate 2026-06-22, 3.85/5 from 48 ratings, $2.99. The year was transposed. This matters because the landscape's conclusion — 'friction reduction alone stopped being a product' — leans on a two-year-stale app that is in fact two months old.

2. COCONOTE IS DESCRIBED AS STALE AND ITS ACQUISITION AS UNCONFIRMED; BOTH ARE WRONG. The study says 'last updated v2.46, May 2026' and 'the App Store listing shows Quizlet Inc as developer, implying an acquisition that could not be confirmed'. Verified this session: v2.50, currentVersionReleaseDate 2026-08-31 (the day of the study), 4.77/5 from 16,544 ratings, sellerName exactly 'Quizlet Inc'. It is shipping today and the developer of record is unambiguous.

3. THE APPLE 'HUNDREDS OF UNREVISITED MEMOS' CLAIM IS LAUNDERED FROM UNVERIFIED INTO FACT. The teardown is honest — 'I could not verify it from a citable source in this research — treat the number as unverified'. The ranked landscape then states it flatly as evidence: 'users report hundreds of memos never revisited - the demand-side hole in Delta's own words'. It is a plausible architectural inference presented as a user research finding, and it is load-bearing for the entire wedge.

4. TANA'S FUNDING IS ASSERTED IN THE LANDSCAPE AND EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED IN THE TEARDOWN. The landscape states '$25M total ($14M Series A led by Tola Capital, Feb 2025... ~$100M valuation); 160,000+ stealth waitlist with staff from 80%+ of the Fortune 500'. The teardown says 'I could NOT verify a figure in this session — tana.inc/mission and tana.inc/careers disclose no funding, no founders, no team size and no user numbers, and Crunchbase returned 403'. The same conflict pattern repeats for Capacities (funding 'not disclosed anywhere I could find') and mymind (founder attribution 'not confirmable from sources I read').

5. TODOIST'S REVENUE IS THREE DIFFERENT NUMBERS. The landscape says '~$20M ARR reported'; the teardown says 'The commonly quoted ~$20M ARR number I could NOT verify from any source I read; treat it as unverified' and cites Latka at $11.2M, itself a third-party estimate. The '40-50M users' figure is company-published and flagged as varying by source. None of these should be quoted without the caveat.

6. VOICENOTES' SCALE FIGURES DO NOT RECONCILE. Homepage claims '1 million downloads' while the Play listing (second-hand via APKCombo) shows 100K+ and only ~81 Android ratings; the landscape cites '100,000+ users by Oct 2024, ~$8.5K MRR Nov 2024' alongside '13 employees May 2026'. $8.5K MRR does not support 13 employees eighteen months later without a growth story nobody produced. Also self-contradictory on its own site: 60+ languages on the meetings page, 100+ on pricing and the App Store.

7. TWINMIND CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON BOTH PRICE AND BATTERY, AND THE STUDY REPORTS BOTH SETS AS IF EITHER MIGHT BE TRUE. Pricing page says $15/$40; App Store IAPs and the help centre say $14.99/$49.99. Homepage says '16+ hours of capture'; help centre says 'optimized for 12 hours'. The teardown flags these correctly, but the ranked landscape then prints 'Pro $15/mo; Max $40/mo' unqualified.

8. WISPR FLOW'S TOTAL RAISED IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT AND THE STUDY SAYS SO WITHOUT RESOLVING IT: 'the raw research contains conflicting totals ($81M vs $361M)'. A $280M Series B at a $2B valuation in Aug 2026 is a very large claim to carry on unreconciled arithmetic.

9. OTTER'S REVENUE SOURCES 'DISAGREE BADLY' BY A FACTOR OF FIVE — $100M ARR company-announced versus $22.3M from an aggregator — and the entry proceeds to use the $100M figure. At minimum the direction of the discrepancy should be explained before either number is used in a strategy document.

10. AUDIONOTES' MARKETING CLAIM IS CALLED 'FALSE' ON INSUFFICIENT GROUNDS. The study says the vendor's '4.9+ by 200K+ users' is 'contradicted by that count and should be treated as false' because the iOS listing shows 91 ratings. An App Store rating count is not a user count, and Audionotes is primarily a web product — this is a category error being stated as a debunking. The same slack should be extended that the study extends to AudioPen's self-reported 200,000+.

11. TALKNOTES' EVIDENCE MAY NOT DESCRIBE THE SAME PRODUCT. The study admits 'it is unverified that it is even the same product as talknotes.io' — the iOS listing shows a different developer (Collin Brewer / Clutch Codeworks) and a listing over a year stale — yet keeps the $0→$7,500 MRR arc and the $200,000 Acquire.com exit as the entry's headline evidence.

12. MULTIPLE 'VERIFIED' TAP COUNTS ARE ACTUALLY DERIVED, AND THE STUDY THEN RECOMMENDS MARKETING AGAINST THEM. Drafts: 'this is derived from documented mechanisms, so treat the timing as my estimate, not a vendor claim'. Braintoss: 'the following is derived from the listing, the FAQ and reviews, not a vendor claim'. Tana and Voicenotes both: '1 tap to the recorder, auto-start UNVERIFIED'. The recommendation to 'publish the tap count and the time-to-first-byte as a product claim' rests on benchmarks that were never actually measured on a device.

13. APPBRAIN-SOURCED INSTALL FIGURES ARE USED WHILE THE STUDY NOTES APPBRAIN ITSELF WOULD NOT LOAD. Google Keep's '~4 billion cumulative installs and ~1.2M downloads/day' is attributed to AppBrain with the parenthetical 'AppBrain itself returns 403 on direct fetch'. Samsung's '~2.6 billion downloads' is preinstall-inflated by the study's own admission. Neither number means what it appears to mean.

14. MYMIND'S PRICING IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT: the study reports no free tier and no advertised trial, then cites a 2024 reviewer referring to 'passing the free 100 limit', and lists a '$299/yr' Newton tier that the pricing page itself says is 'not yet available'. The landscape entry nonetheless prints 'annual tier advertised around $299/yr' as a live price.

15. SMALLER UNRESOLVED CONFLICTS THAT SHOULD NOT BE QUOTED AS FACT: Pocket's hardware price ($129 on the site vs $199 in a TechCrunch roundup); Omi's seed ('$2-3M... figure inconsistent across Dealroom/Crunchbase/Tracxn'); Voicepal's last update ('2 Jul (2024 in one source, 2026 in another)'); Drafts' Mac app returning '0 US ratings' which the study itself calls implausible; Cleft's claimed 56% annual price cut, its Verge quote and its Fast Company placement, all explicitly unretrieved; superwhisper's 'hundreds of thousands of users' self-reported against 820 ratings; and Aqua Voice's '>50% of users in Japan' and ~$330K ARR, both from secondary sources.

16. LIMITLESS IS CHARACTERISED WITHOUT EVIDENCE IN A WAY THAT UNDERSTATES THE TRUE FINDING. The study asserts Bee and Limitless 'are now demonstrating publicly' the problems of ambient capture, with no citation. Verified this session: the Limitless AI iOS app sits at 3.34/5 from 122 ratings and was last updated 2026-04-18. The claim is directionally right and the actual evidence is stronger than what was written — but as written it is an unsourced assertion doing real argumentative work against the ambient thesis.

## Products the first sweep missed

The critic verified each of these independently. The wave-2 teardowns above cover the most important ones; the rest are recorded here as leads.

- **flomo (浮墨笔记)** — <https://flomoapp.com/>  
  THE BIGGEST MISS IN THE STUDY. Verified this session on the CN App Store: 4.93/5 from 51,993 ratings, v5.5.0 shipped 2026-08-30 — roughly 8x Voicenotes' 6,719 rating base, and the largest verified rating count of any dedicated quick-capture app in this entire landscape. Its 2026 App Store name is literally 'flomo浮墨笔记-AI语音备忘|卡片便签|日记回顾与洞察' (AI voice memo | card notes | review and insights) and the description promises 一站式笔记应用 integrating AI voice transcription, smart insights and mind maps. Its founding slogan is '持续不断记录，意义自然浮现' — 'keep recording continuously, meaning naturally emerges' — which is the exact thesis the study attributes to mymind, executed at 100x the scale and now with voice bolted on. It is also the strongest counterexample to the study's 'capture without triage degrades into an unsearchable wall' conclusion: flomo deliberately has no folders, only tags and a review/resurfacing loop, and has retained a large paying base for years. Studying it would have changed the mymind and Voicenotes conclusions.

- **Granola** — <https://www.granola.ai/>  
  Verified: 4.96/5 from 12,532 US App Store ratings, v1.15.4 (2026-08-18) — the second-largest verified rating base in the AI-notes tier after Apple's first-party apps, nearly double Voicenotes. Absent entirely, yet the study's own Voicenotes teardown says Voicenotes has repositioned its homepage into exactly Granola's market ('botless meeting notetaker', naming Granola as the competitor) and recommends 'DO NOT COPY THE MEETING PIVOT'. That recommendation is made without ever looking at the product the incumbent is pivoting toward. Verified on site: macOS, Windows, iOS, Android and Apple Watch; explicitly meetings-only, no project/person entity model; free tier with a 30-day retention window (the same retention-window gate Voicenotes uses). Its 4.96 rating is the highest in the landscape and deserves a teardown on capture-UX and trust grounds alone.

- **Apple Journal** — <https://apps.apple.com/us/app/journal/id6447391597>  
  Verified: 4.78/5 from 314,931 US ratings, Apple Inc., v1.7. The study's Apple entry is titled 'Voice Memos + Notes / Reminders' and covers those three apps in depth while omitting the fourth preinstalled capture app — which supports audio recording entries and reflective prompts. This matters twice over: (1) it is the third-largest verified rating base in the landscape, preinstalled and free; (2) the Rosebud entry explicitly says Delta's intent taxonomy lacks a capture class for 'I am processing a feeling' — Apple ships a free, preinstalled, 315K-rating app for exactly that class, which makes Rosebud's positioning far more precarious than the study concluded and removes a suggested roadmap direction.

- **Day One** — <https://dayoneapp.com/>  
  Verified: 4.83/5 from 117,927 US ratings, Bloom Built Inc (Automattic-owned), v2026.18 shipped 2026-08-31. Supports audio entries with transcription, end-to-end encryption, and a decade-plus of continuous development on a subscription. It is the largest independent paid capture product in the landscape by rating count and is missing entirely. Relevant as the proof that a private, non-actionable, retrieval-through-time capture product can sustain a real business at scale — and as the acquirer-backed competitor for the journaling/reflection class.

- **Turbo AI** — <https://apps.apple.com/us/app/turbo-ai-learn-faster/id6472591985>  
  Verified: 4.75/5 from 33,334 US ratings, TurboLearn LLC, v1.5.6 (2026-08-21). Twice the rating base of Coconote, which the study included as 'the largest download base of any consumer voice-to-structured-notes app here'. That claim is wrong on the evidence: Turbo AI is bigger on US ratings and was never examined. Both belong to the same pattern the study half-noticed — the student segment has the largest actually-used voice→structure products, and the study dismissed the segment rather than studying why it converts.

- **Voice Recorder: AI Note Taker (Hangzhou Suoyi Network Technology)** — <https://apps.apple.com/us/app/voice-recorder-ai-note-taker/>  
  Verified: 4.66/5 from 7,517 US ratings, v1.7.3. A commodity Chinese-built AI voice recorder with MORE US ratings than Voicenotes, the study's #1-ranked competitor. Its existence is the most important omission for pricing strategy: the study concludes the wedge is 'structure and findability' and prices Delta at €8-10/mo, without registering that an undifferentiated record→transcribe→summarise app from an anonymous developer already outranks the category leader on installed base. The commodity tier sets the price floor and the study never priced against it.

- **Limitless (formerly Rewind)** — <https://www.limitless.ai/>  
  Named in passing inside the TwinMind and Bee entries ('the consent, privacy and volume problems that Bee and Limitless are now demonstrating publicly') but given no entry, no evidence and no relevance score, despite being the best-funded pure competitor to TwinMind's ambient thesis. Verified this session and the data contradicts the study's framing: 'Limitless AI Voice Recorder' by Limitless AI Inc. sits at 3.34/5 from only 122 ratings, v2.1.10 last updated 2026-04-18 — over four months stale. That is a materially stronger and more citable argument against ambient capture than the hand-wave the study offers, and it should be an entry with the evidence attached.

- **Notta** — <https://www.notta.ai/>  
  Verified: 4.05/5 from 1,398 US ratings, Mind Cruiser Limited, v6.79.14 shipped 2026-08-26 — and its real base is Japan and Greater China, where it is one of the dominant transcription products. Missing entirely. Relevant because the study's entire multi-language analysis is 'Apple does not do Dutch', with no examination of how any competitor actually handles non-English capture, code-switching, or Asian-market ASR — Notta is the obvious primary source for that and was never consulted.

- **Craft** — <https://www.craft.do/>  
  Verified: 4.84/5 from 6,597 US ratings, Craft Docs Limited, v3.6.0 shipped 2026-08-31. Apple Design Award winner, funded, with a daily-note capture surface, block model and Web Clipper. Comparable in scale to Voicenotes and absent. It is the missing member of the hybrid tier alongside Capacities and Reflect, and notably Cleft's own Shortcuts cookbook ships a 'send to Craft inbox' recipe — the study read that cookbook and did not follow the reference.

- **Fireflies.ai** — <https://fireflies.ai/>  
  Verified: 4.82/5 from 4,892 US ratings, FIREFLIES.AI CORP., v0.5.52 (2026-08-27). The study keeps Otter as 'the single representative of the meeting-transcription lane' at relevance 2 and names Fireflies only inside a list of Voicenotes' competitors. Given that the category leader is pivoting into this lane and Granola sits at 12.5K ratings, treating the whole lane as one relevance-2 placeholder under-weights where the commercial gravity in voice actually is.

- **Superlist** — <https://www.superlist.com/>  
  Verified: 4.51/5 from 446 US ratings, Superlist Software GmbH, v1.57.1 (2026-08-25). Built by the Wunderlist team (Christian Reber), well funded. Its own App Store description is the finding: 'the to-do list, notes, and AI meeting app that keeps your work and life organized' — a third funded team converging on exactly Delta's combination of notes plus tasks plus AI capture in one product. Small but directly on the collision course the study's convergence analysis is about.

- **Google Tasks / Keep via Gemini voice on Android** — <https://tasks.google.com/>  
  Google Tasks verified at 4.80/5 from 106,035 US ratings. The study devotes an entire deep teardown to the Apple friction floor (Action button, Siri, Lock Screen widgets, tap counts) and covers Google Keep and Google Recorder only as passive transcription baselines. There is no equivalent analysis of the Android capture floor: Gemini replacing Assistant as the voice entry point, 'remember this' / create-a-task-by-voice, Quick Settings tiles, Android home-screen and lock-screen widgets, or Wear OS. Delta ships on Expo to both platforms; half the friction-floor analysis is missing, and the study never verified a single Google Play rating first-hand (every Android figure in it is an APKCombo mirror flagged as second-hand).

- **WhatsApp (message-yourself + built-in voice-note transcription)** — <https://www.whatsapp.com/>  
  Included in the study only as an inbound channel for Voicenotes and Capacities, never as a competitor in its own right — while Telegram Saved Messages got a full entry at relevance 4. In the Netherlands and Belgium, Delta's actual home market, WhatsApp is the dominant messenger and Telegram is marginal, so the study's 'strongest do-nothing competitor' is the wrong app for the target market. WhatsApp now transcribes voice notes natively and 'Message yourself' is a first-class feature; scale is obvious but I could not verify current transcription language coverage for Dutch this session — that specific question is the single highest-value unanswered research item for a Dutch-market launch. Marked unverified on the Dutch-transcription detail.

- **MacWhisper** — <https://goodsnooze.gumroad.com/l/macwhisper>  
  Widely used paid indie Mac Whisper front-end (Jordi Bruin) sold outside the App Store, frequently cited alongside superwhisper in Mac dictation comparisons. Relevant as the make-vs-buy datapoint for local ASR and as evidence that a one-time-purchase local transcription tool sustains a business. UNVERIFIED this session — it is not on the App Store so I could not confirm scale from a store API, and my search budget was exhausted before I could fetch its sales page. Flagging it as a lead to verify rather than a finding.

## Coverage gaps

What this research did not cover, in the critic's own words. Summarised in the intro; reproduced here in full.

1. NON-ENGLISH MARKETS ARE ABSENT, AND IT IS THE LARGEST GAP. Every product studied is US/EU-Anglophone. flomo alone (51,993 ratings) has more verified users than the top three studied voice apps combined, and the Chinese and Japanese markets contain the biggest actually-used quick-capture and transcription products (flomo, Notta, Mubu, and commodity AI recorders outranking Voicenotes). The study's own conclusions about what retains users are drawn from a sample that excludes the largest working examples.

2. ANDROID IS ANALYSED AT A FRACTION OF iOS DEPTH, AND NOT ONE PLAY RATING WAS VERIFIED FIRST-HAND. Every Android figure in the study is an APKCombo mirror explicitly flagged as second-hand or unverified (Voicenotes, Tana, TwinMind, Braintoss, Capacities). There are no Android tap counts, no analysis of Quick Settings tiles, home-screen/lock-screen widgets, the Gemini voice entry point, Wear OS, or Android background-recording restrictions — despite Delta shipping to Android via Expo and despite Tana's 2.9-star Android rating being one of the study's own headline warnings.

3. THE MEETING LANE WAS DELIBERATELY DOWNWEIGHTED WHERE THE COMMERCIAL EVIDENCE ACTUALLY IS. Granola (12,532 ratings), Fireflies (4,892) and Otter ($100M ARR) sit in a lane kept at relevance 2-3, while the study's own #1 competitor has moved its homepage into that lane and Tana bet its entire company on it. The study advises 'do not copy the meeting pivot' without examining why two of the strongest teams in the category independently made it.

4. UNIT ECONOMICS AND COST-PER-CAPTURE ARE NEVER COMPUTED. Prices are catalogued exhaustively but nobody costed a capture: ASR minutes plus understand-step tokens plus storage against €8-10/mo. The study recommends 'never meter capture' (from Tana) and 'transcription should feel free at the point of use' without checking whether that survives contact with our margins. TwinMind gives away unlimited transcription because it owns Ear-3 at $0.23/hour; we would be reselling someone else's ASR, which is a completely different cost structure and the study does not say so.

5. ONBOARDING AND TIME-TO-FIRST-VALUE ARE ESSENTIALLY UNEXAMINED. Empty states, first-run imports, how a project/contact graph gets seeded before any capture exists, and what the app looks like on day one with zero data are mentioned only glancingly (Saner's too-tight free tier, Capacities' blank-page problem, Tana's 'steep learning curve'). For a product whose value comes from matching against existing projects and people, the cold-start problem is the central design risk and it got no dedicated treatment.

6. RETENTION MECHANICS ARE ASSERTED FROM REVIEW ANECDOTES, NOT MEASURED. There is no cohort data, no D30 evidence, no churn-reason analysis beyond one-star reviews — which are a biased sample. The recommendation to 'track time to inbox zero as the retention metric' is invented by the study, not observed working anywhere.

7. ACCESSIBILITY IS NAMED AS THE BEACHHEAD BUT NEVER ENGINEERED. Cleft's ADHD/neurodivergent positioning is correctly identified as the wedge, yet there is no analysis of VoiceOver, Dynamic Type, motor-impairment capture paths, Switch Control, or — most importantly — ASR accuracy on atypical, disfluent, stammering or dysarthric speech. If the beachhead is verbal thinkers and disabled users, word error rate on non-standard speech is a product requirement, and no competitor was tested on it.

8. EU LEGAL AND PRIVACY GROUND IS COVERED ONLY FOR TwinMind. GDPR lawful basis, data residency, sub-processor disclosure, the EU AI Act's transparency duties, and Dutch/Belgian recording-consent law are the operating constraints for Delta's actual market and appear once, in passing. The study identifies 'Apple's Siri AI is not available in the EU at launch' as an opportunity without examining why, which is the same regulatory environment we would ship into.

9. MEASURED LATENCY IS ABSENT EVERYWHERE. Tap counts are repeatedly derived rather than measured ('auto-start UNVERIFIED' for both Tana and Voicenotes; 'this is my estimate, not a vendor claim' for Drafts and Braintoss), and time-to-first-byte, time-to-transcript and time-to-structure were never stopwatched for any competitor. The study then recommends publishing our tap count and time-to-first-byte as a marketing claim, against benchmarks it did not actually take.

10. BATTERY, THERMAL AND STORAGE COST OF CAPTURE GO UNEXAMINED. TwinMind's own docs contradict themselves at 12 vs 16 hours and the study flags the contradiction without pursuing it. For any always-available recording affordance this is a real constraint on both sides of the ambient/deliberate argument.

11. VOICE AS AN OUTPUT AND RETRIEVAL MODALITY IS BARELY CONSIDERED. Willow's verbal editing and Tana's 'Hey Tana' get one line each. Nothing covers hands-free recall (driving, walking), spoken read-back of the day's captures, or conversational triage by voice — which is the obvious way a zero-typing product empties an inbox without a keyboard, and is a genuine unexplored design space.

12. SHARED, FAMILY AND MULTI-USER CAPTURE IS REPEATEDLY NOTED AS ABSENT AND NEVER ANALYSED. mymind loses a subscription over missing Family Sharing, Cleft gates Family Sharing to annual, Capacities refuses teams outright, Todoist and Tana monetise per seat. CLAUDE.md lists the multi-user instance model as an open product decision, and the research offers it no input at all.

13. MIGRATION AND SWITCHING COSTS ARE ONE-DIRECTIONAL. Export is discussed extensively as a trust feature; import is discussed only as mymind's refusal. Nobody analysed what it takes to bring in an existing Apple Voice Memos library, a Notion database, or an Obsidian vault — which is the actual acquisition path for every user worth having.

14. DISTRIBUTION AND ACQUISITION ARE ALMOST UNSTUDIED. One observation about Speakwise's programmatic SEO and one about Monologue launching off an owned audience. No ASO analysis, no keyword landscape, no channel economics, no explanation of how Coconote or Turbo AI reached tens of thousands of ratings while better products sit at a few hundred. For a solo-built product this is more decision-relevant than any feature comparison in the study.

15. LONG-HORIZON GRAPH DECAY IS RAISED AND DROPPED. Tana's smart merging is correctly identified as the answer to duplicate entities, but no competitor was examined at two-plus years of accumulated data to see whether any auto-populated entity graph actually stays usable. That is the central long-term risk of Delta's architecture and the study has no evidence on it either way.

## Excluded products

Dropped during deduplication as dead, not real, or out of the agreed scope. Recorded so we do not research them again by accident.

| Product                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Reason for exclusion                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Limitless Pendant / Rewind                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Dead as a purchasable product. Acquired by Meta (announced 5 Dec 2025), hardware sales stopped, Rewind desktop wound down, EU/UK service terminated with a hard data-export deadline, team moved to Reality Labs. Two lessons kept for the brief rather than the list: reviewers consistently said capture worked and recall/organisation did not, and 'what happens to my captures if you disappear' is now a stated purchase criterion.       |
| Humane AI Pin                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Fully dead. Cloud services shut off 28 Feb 2025 bricking all AI features; fewer than 10,000 units shipped on ~$230M raised; assets sold to HP for $116M. Lesson retained: every feature was cloud-bound, so keep raw audio and transcripts recoverable independently of the understand pipeline.                                                                                                                                                |
| Airchat                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Effectively dead. Users reported it non-functional by late 2024 and the team announced in Mar 2026 it was sunsetting the current version to relaunch. No verified active user base.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Friend                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Out of scope and negligible usage: it produces companionship, not structured notes. ~5,000 units sold against enormous (largely negative) press and a Museum of Failure entry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Sandbar Stream                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Not yet a real product - still pre-order/pre-launch as of Aug 2026 with a summer 2026 commercial target and zero real-world usage, despite $36M raised. Its philosophy (deliberate push-to-talk, capturing your own thoughts rather than recording other people) is the closest hardware match to Delta's interaction and worth revisiting once it ships.                                                                                       |
| TalkTastic (formerly Oasis)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Pivoted out of scope and stalled. Oasis was almost exactly the voice-memo-to-synthesised-note product; its founder abandoned that framing for a Mac dictation keyboard, the marketing site returns almost no content, and 2026 reviews still describe a free beta. Kept as a cautionary note (the note-shaped product lost to the keyboard-shaped one), not as a competitor.                                                                    |
| Roam Research                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Out of scope (text-first PKM, no voice or quick-capture story) and in visible decline - 2026 coverage is dominated by exit guides. Its daily-notes-as-inbox pattern survives in Logseq, Capacities, Amplenote and NotePlan, which are in the list.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Granola                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Pure meeting recorder - the unit is a meeting, not a thought, and there is no inbox or triage step. Excluded on scope despite being the best-executed product in that lane ($125M Series C at $1.5B, 5.0/13K ratings). One pattern worth borrowing anyway: it merges raw audio with the user's own sparse typed input into a single artefact, which is a good model for a gate where user signal supplements rather than replaces AI inference. |
| Fireflies.ai                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Pure meeting bot that joins calls; enterprise seat-based. Out of scope. Its own numbers also conflict badly (claims 20M users against ~35K paying customers).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Fathom                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Pure meeting notetaker. Out of scope. One pricing datapoint retained for the brief: 6,000+ people rate it 5.0 on G2 while paying nothing, which is the anchor price for turning speech into text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Wave AI Note Taker                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Pure background call/meeting recorder with speaker labels and no structuring layer, despite a 12K-rating base. Out of scope, and it confirms a large chunk of the 'voice notes' market is really call-recording demand that Delta should not chase.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Notta                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Pure transcription service selling on language coverage and SOC 2 / ISO certification to business buyers. No capture-to-structure loop.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Rev                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Pure transcription marketplace (AI plus human at $1/min). Pre-AI incumbent, no structuring or capture loop.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Transkriptor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Pure commodity transcription at volume (5M+ Android installs) with no structuring. Kept out; the volume only shows how little that demand overlaps with ours.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Hedy AI                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Real-time meeting assistant - out of scope as a meeting product, and traction is thin (214 AppSumo reviews, a vendor-issued 'award' press release). Its on-device live in-conversation suggestions are noted as evidence that real-time assistance during capture is feasible on a phone.                                                                                                                                                       |
| MacWhisper                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Pure desktop file-transcription utility, not a capture product. Retained only as the price floor for the transcription layer alone: roughly 300,000 copies at about €59 one-time (that unit figure is from secondary sources and unverified).                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Obsidian Whisper plugin (nikdanilov)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | A community plugin, not a product - folded into the Obsidian entry. 55,100 registry downloads and 376 GitHub stars are real, but its last release was ~5 months before Aug 2026 and it exists only inside Obsidian.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| VoiceTask AI (id6753657106) and Voice Inbox AI (id6760556777)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Effectively zero users - VoiceTask AI is 'not yet rated', Voice Inbox AI has 5.0 from exactly one rating - despite describing almost precisely Delta's feature set (voice in; tasks, notes, reminders and projects out). Worth recording as evidence that the idea is not novel and that execution and reliability are the whole game.                                                                                                          |
